OFSEP Answer Keys and Explanations

Numbered to match the practice tests in your printed study guide.

Practice Test 1 Answer Key

  1. The correct answer is B (Authority names who decides, and consent is that person's agreement to a particular step.): Authority answers who is entitled to give directions about the deceased. Consent answers whether that person agreed to this specific step. A licensee needs both, because the person who holds authority may still refuse a procedure that is proposed.
  2. The correct answer is C (Ask questions to identify the person with authority and confirm it in writing where possible.): The competency profile asks the licensee to determine appropriate authorities by asking questions and obtaining written confirmation and supporting documentation wherever necessary and possible. Arriving first, being chosen as a spokesperson or making the first telephone call does not by itself create the legal right to give directions.
  3. The correct answer is B (A signed statement confirming his role together with documents that support his family relationship.): The competency profile asks for written confirmation and supporting documentation wherever necessary and possible. A statement from the son together with documents that show his relationship gives the file a record of how the authority was established. The contract and the receipt deal with the purchase of services and do not prove who may give directions.
  4. The correct answer is C (Gather documents from both women that show their legal relationship to the deceased.): The worked example in the chapter starts with gathering information rather than choosing a winner. The licensee asks for the marriage or separation agreement, records that show who lived with the deceased, any will or estate trustee appointment and identification for each person. The licensee does not decide who is right, and a disputed disposition choice waits while the question is settled.
  5. The correct answer is A (Hold the disposition in abeyance and explain that a court direction may be needed.): When a dispute cannot be resolved, the funeral home may need the parties to obtain a court direction before the disposition proceeds. The licensee does not choose between two claimants and does not act on a contested instruction. Refusing service leaves the family without support and does not decide who holds authority.
  6. The correct answer is D (The name of the person who gave permission, the time and both locations.): Permission to transfer may be oral or written, and the record should show who gave permission, when it was given, where the deceased was and where the deceased was taken. The names of everyone who was told, the director's licence number and the cost belong to other parts of the file.
  7. The correct answer is A (The body must remain where it is until the coroner directs the movement.): Where a coroner has jurisdiction, the body cannot be moved except as the coroner directs. Family permission, physician approval and the end of police interviews are not the same as a coroner's release. The licensee explains the process and keeps the family informed while the coroner's work continues.
  8. The correct answer is B (Explain the coroner's process and offer the family another way to mark the death.): When the body cannot be released for some time, the licensee helps the family find a way to mark the death, such as a gathering without the body, and explains each step honestly. Moving the body, announcing a release that has not happened and promising a date all break the coroner's control of the process.
  9. The correct answer is C (Permission to prepare and permission to embalm are two separate decisions.): The competency profile separates permission to complete the preparation performed on the body from specific authorization to arterially embalm. Many families treat washing and dressing differently from the injection of preserving chemicals. One general permission does not replace the specific authorization that embalming requires.
  10. The correct answer is C (Obtain her specific authorization for the embalming of her husband in a respectful way.): An agreement to take care of everything is not specific authorization to embalm. The licensee asks for permission for that procedure in plain language, in a respectful manner, and writes down what was said. The choice of cosmetics, the service date and the location are separate decisions.
  11. The correct answer is D (The name of the deceased, the daughter's name, her relationship and the date.): Permission for embalming should name the deceased, the person giving permission, that person's relationship to the deceased and the date. The embalmer and the chemicals belong in the preparation report, and the price belongs in the contract for services. Contact details do not identify the decision that was made.
  12. The correct answer is D (A Class 2 licensee may not provide the services of arterially embalming a body.): Section 40 makes it a condition of every Funeral Director Class 2 licence that the licensee shall not provide the services of arterially embalming or restoring dead human bodies. The matching condition in section 39 allows a Class 1 director to embalm only when acting on behalf of an operator holding a Funeral Establishment Operator Class 1 licence. Operator approval or extra training does not change the licence condition.
  13. The correct answer is C (Explain that the establishment cannot provide embalming and review the other options available.): The competency profile asks the licensee to communicate the limitation of scope of practice and the available services or facilities. Section 34(3) prevents a Class 2 establishment from providing funeral services other than maintaining premises, and section 33(4) requires a Class 1 establishment that embalms to have an approved embalming room and a Class 1 manager. The licensee states the limit honestly and works through the choices that remain.
  14. The correct answer is A (Washing, disinfecting, setting the features, dressing the body and applying cosmetics to it.): Preparation without embalming includes washing, disinfecting, setting the features, dressing, applying cosmetics and placing the body in a casket. Arterial injection and restoration are part of the embalming scope that a Class 1 licensee performs. A sealed casket does not stop natural changes, and the family's refusal of embalming is respected.
  15. The correct answer is A (Review the notes passed from the arranging director and any cultural or religious requirements.): Before embalming begins, the licensee confirms effective and accurate communication between the director who made the arrangements and the director performing the embalming, and identifies any cultural, religious or personal requirements that affect the procedure. Payment, casketing and the visitation schedule are separate checks that do not replace that handover.
  16. The correct answer is D (A certificate issued by the coroner authorizing the cremation of the body.): Section 31(2)(a) forbids a crematorium operator to permit cremation without a certificate issued by the coroner authorizing it. Family consents and the funeral director's identification record do not replace that certificate. A burial permit is a separate document under the Vital Statistics Act.
  17. The correct answer is B (The crematorium must not permit cremation while the pacemaker remains in the body.): Section 31(2)(b) prevents a crematorium operator from permitting the cremation of a body that has a pacemaker. The two-year exception in section 31(2.1) applies only to a radioactive implant, not to a pacemaker. The device must be removed by a person described in section 52 before the cremation can proceed.
  18. The correct answer is A (A Funeral Director Class 1 acting for a Class 1 establishment or transfer service.): Section 52 allows the operator to engage a person who holds a Funeral Director Class 1 licence and is acting on behalf of an operator holding a Funeral Establishment Operator Class 1 or Transfer Service Operator Class 1 licence. A legally qualified medical practitioner may also perform the removal. A Class 2 director, a sales representative and a preplanner are not among the persons the section allows.
  19. The correct answer is B (The cremation may proceed because at least two years have passed since the implant.): Section 31(2.1) lifts the prohibition for a body with a radioactive implant once at least two years have passed since the implant, or if a shorter period has passed and it is safe to cremate the body. The family's permission and the coroner's cremation certificate are separate requirements. Neither one replaces the rule that deals with the implant.
  20. The correct answer is D (The operator may not cremate a body in a casket with fibre-reinforced plastic.): Section 31(2)(b) prohibits cremating a body in a casket that consists of or has on or in it non-flammable or hazardous material or chlorinated or fibre-reinforced plastic, apart from incidental metal used in the construction of the casket. A family release, a lining and a small quantity of plastic do not change that condition. The licensee checks the casket before promising a cremation date.
  21. The correct answer is D (Six years from the date the contract was fully performed or cancelled.): Section 101(1) requires an operator to retain records relating to a contract for six years from the date the contract is fully performed or cancelled. The other periods do not appear in that provision. The retention clock starts with performance or cancellation, not with the signature or the final payment.
  22. The correct answer is B (Six years from the date of the cremation of the deceased person's body.): Section 101(5) requires a crematorium operator to retain a coroner's certificate authorizing cremation for six years from the date of the cremation. The record about cremated remains in section 100 has its own six-year rule that runs from the date the remains are claimed or interred. Records that must be made available to the public are kept for as long as the operator remains licensed.
  23. The correct answer is D (Six years from the date on which each of those records was created.): Section 101(6) requires an operator to retain every other record required under the Act or Part II of the regulation for six years from the date it is created. Contract records run from performance or cancellation, the coroner's cremation certificate runs from the date of cremation, and the cremated remains record runs from the date the remains are claimed or interred.
  24. The correct answer is A (Share information only with the people who have a genuine need to know it.): Confidentiality protects the records as well as the conversations, and personal information about a deceased person and a family is shared only with people who need it. Funeral information is not opened to anyone who asks, and a caller's curiosity is not a need to know. The licensee can offer to pass a message to the family without confirming their private details.
  25. The correct answer is D (That the donation organization has confirmed the arrangements with the family in writing.): Anatomical donation involves the donation organization, the hospital and sometimes the coroner. The licensee confirms the arrangements with the donation organization in writing and explains how donation affects the timeline for transfer, preparation, visitation and disposition. A casket deposit, a proof of death form and the coroner's interest do not confirm the donation arrangements.
  26. The correct answer is B (Explain the need to confirm who holds authority and take steps to reach the daughter.): The licensee does not decide who is right, and the licensee asks for documents that show who has the legal right to give directions. A year of care does not create that right, a statement signed by the caregiver does not transfer it and splitting the arrangements leaves the file without a clear decision maker. The steps that cannot wait, such as keeping the body safe, continue while the question is settled.
  27. The correct answer is C (The certificate of appointment of estate trustee or other proof of his appointment.): Where authority is claimed through a will, the licensee asks for supporting documentation, which in practice is proof that the person has been appointed as estate trustee. A lawyer's acceptance of a will and a spouse's approval do not establish the appointment. The licensee records what was provided and reviews it before taking directions about disposition.
  28. The correct answer is A (Explain that the document applied during his life and that authority after death is different.): A power of attorney for personal care governs decisions made during the person's life and does not continue after death. Authority over the arrangements after death rests with the person entitled to give directions, such as an estate trustee, and the licensee confirms that with documents. A new power of attorney cannot be signed by someone who has died, and the coroner decides only the matters within the coroner's jurisdiction.
  29. The correct answer is D (Refer the question to the operator and to legal counsel or the coroner.): Where no family member or representative can be found, specific legal processes may apply and the licensee does not invent a solution. The question goes to the operator and, where needed, to legal counsel or the coroner. Signing the authorization, using the landlord and setting an objection deadline all place authority where it does not belong.
  30. The correct answer is B (Who is legally responsible to make the arrangements and who is the purchaser under the contract.): The competency profile asks the licensee to verify in a sensitive manner who is legally responsible to make arrangements and who will be financially responsible as the purchaser under the contract. The two roles can sit with different people, and the size of a share, closeness to the deceased and possession of a bank card do not settle either one.
  31. The correct answer is C (Grief is the internal response to a loss, and mourning is its outward expression.): Grief is the internal response to loss, including feelings, thoughts, physical sensations and behaviours. Mourning is the outward expression of grief, shaped by culture, religion and family custom. Bereavement is the state of having lost someone. A licensee can explain all three without treating grief as a clinical condition.
  32. The correct answer is B (Grieving people move back and forth between the loss and the tasks of living.): Grief education commonly describes a movement back and forth between focusing on the loss and focusing on the practical tasks of living, and that back and forth is normal. A person who seems calm at the funeral may struggle weeks later. Fixed stages, a completion date and one shared timetable do not describe how grief usually works.
  33. The correct answer is D (Fatigue, changes in sleep and appetite, chest tightness and poor concentration.): Common physical reactions include fatigue, changes in appetite and sleep, tightness in the chest and difficulty concentrating. Thinking can also become foggy, which is one reason a grieving family needs information repeated and written down. The licensee does not diagnose or treat these reactions, and a person who has them is not losing their mind.
  34. The correct answer is C (Slow the pace, give her simple information in writing and allow her to sit quietly.): Shock and numbness are common reactions, and the licensee responds by giving the person time, letting silence sit and writing down every decision. A family in acute distress remembers very little, so a written summary and a follow up call matter. Pressing for decisions, asking the person to work through feelings and calling the reaction unusual all go beyond the licensee's role.
  35. The correct answer is C (Listen without judging him, answer his questions honestly and avoid promising or blaming.): A family member who is bargaining with the past is not helped by a promise, a blame statement or a request to postpone the thought. The licensee listens without judging, answers what can be answered honestly and stays inside the scope of supportive listening. Where the reaction continues or deepens, the licensee offers information about counselling and a referral.
  36. The correct answer is A (Listen to her without assigning blame or offering her a promise of forgiveness.): Guilt is a common reaction, and the licensee does not assign blame or give an assurance the licensee cannot give. The licensee listens, acknowledges the feeling and offers a referral if the guilt persists. Telling her that the feeling will fade, offering forgiveness or redirecting her thoughts makes the licensee the judge of how she should feel.
  37. The correct answer is A (Offer water and a quiet space, then make only the decisions that cannot wait.): Practical care supports emotional care, and a glass of water, a quiet room and a written summary reduce the load on a grieving person. Where a decision can wait, it waits, and where a decision cannot wait the licensee gives the smallest possible choice. Repeating the options, sending her away and choosing for her all increase the pressure instead of reducing it.
  38. The correct answer is B (Explain that teenagers often show their grief through anger, withdrawal and risky behaviour.): A teenager may show grief through anger, withdrawal or risk taking, and that pattern does not mean the grief is absent or wrong. The licensee offers information about how teenagers commonly respond and lets the family decide what to do. Punishment, a judgment about proper grieving and sending the teenager away do not support the family or stay within the licensee's role.
  39. The correct answer is C (Acknowledge her loss, offer practical support and give her written bereavement resources.): An older adult may lose a spouse after decades together and may also be managing health problems, so the licensee acknowledges the loss, offers practical help and provides bereavement resources. The licensee does not advise on housing or health matters and does not predict how long the grief will last. A warm referral to a health care professional is available if the family wants one.
  40. The correct answer is D (Describe what she will see and hear, and let her choose whether to take part.): Children do better when they are told the truth in words they can understand, when they are prepared for what they will see and hear, and when they are given a choice about taking part. Saying the body is sleeping, excluding the child and sending her in alone all remove that preparation. The licensee can describe the room, who will be there and how people may behave.
  41. The correct answer is C (Give her a contact name and telephone number, and follow up to see if she called.): A referral should be made clearly and gently, with specific information about how to make contact, and it should include a follow up to see whether the person made contact. Referring is not abandoning the family, because the licensee continues to provide services and support. A pamphlet alone is not a referral, the licensee does not predict how long grief will last and the family should not be asked to arrange care on her behalf.
  42. The correct answer is B (Stay calm and ask only for the information needed to complete the funeral arrangements.): A death by suicide may leave family members with guilt, anger, shame and fear of judgment, and the licensee's first responsibility is to be calm and non-judgmental. Questions are asked only where the information is needed for the arrangement or for the safety of staff, and the answers stay confidential. Asking for detail, sharing the cause with staff and pressing the family to describe the death all add to their burden.
  43. The correct answer is A (Brief the staff so that nobody volunteers any information to callers or to visitors.): The family decides what is disclosed, subject to what the law requires, and staff are briefed so that no one volunteers information to a visitor or a caller. Confirming the cause on request, leaving the decision to each staff member and posting a notice at the door all release information in ways the family did not choose. The licensee follows the family's direction and keeps the information confidential.
  44. The correct answer is C (A burial permit is not required for products of conception that are not a stillbirth.): Section 32(2) states that a burial permit under the Vital Statistics Act is not required for the provision of funeral services with respect to products of conception that do not constitute a still birth and for which no burial permit is therefore required. Section 35(5) contains the matching rule for transfer services. The licensee explains the rule and supports the family without inventing a document requirement.
  45. The correct answer is A (Offer steady support, answer the children's questions simply and refer the family for help.): A death of a child is one of the deaths that intensify grief, and the licensee responds with patience, honest information and an offer of support and referral. The licensee helps the parents explain what has happened in words the siblings can understand and lets the family decide about the children's part in the visitation. Promises about peace, exclusion and a timeline for forgetting all go beyond the licensee's role.
  46. The correct answer is A (Ask the family to explain their customs and be honest when something is unfamiliar.): Culture, religion and personal belief shape mourning, and the professional response is to ask rather than assume, to learn the customs that appear in the community and to be honest when something is unfamiliar. Applying another family's practice, asking the family to simplify their customs and relying on a general online description all risk getting the details wrong.
  47. The correct answer is D (Give her information at her own pace and keep offering support and options.): Denial is a common reaction, and the licensee does not argue with it or announce that the death must be accepted. The licensee gives information at the family's pace, repeats what is needed and keeps the arrangement moving as far as the family can manage. Confrontation, removing her from the decisions and describing denial as unhealthy all push the person harder than the situation allows.
  48. The correct answer is B (Acknowledge his anger calmly, answer what is known and avoid speculating about the collision.): Anger is not treated as an attack on the funeral director. The licensee acknowledges the feeling, answers the practical questions honestly and does not speculate about a death that is under investigation. Asking him to leave, telling him that anger will not help and predicting when the anger will pass all dismiss the reaction or exceed the licensee's role.
  49. The correct answer is A (Explain the limit of the licence, offer resource material and refer him to a counsellor.): A funeral professional provides support, information and practical help, and does not diagnose, provide psychotherapy or decide what another person should feel. Recognizing the limit is part of competence, and the licensee explains it, offers bereavement resource material and refers the man to a physician, grief counsellor or therapist. Taking him on as a counselling client, judging whether counselling is needed and handing the arrangements to a doctor do not fit the role.
  50. The correct answer is B (Recognize that her grief needs assessment and refer her warmly to a professional.): Grief that appears stuck, including an inability to function long after the death and expressions of hopelessness, calls for a referral rather than continued waiting or treatment by the licensee. The licensee recognizes the limit of the scope of practice, refers warmly to an appropriate professional and follows up to see whether contact was made. Providing counselling, giving a timeline and steering her away from medical help all exceed the role.
  51. The correct answer is B (Two casket types with fixed linings or ornamentation from the cheapest available, and one plain casket.): Section 43 requires at least two types of caskets with fixed interior linings or ornamentation, drawn from among the least expensive available on the market. The same section requires at least one inexpensive casket that is neither ornamented nor has a fixed interior lining, other than a lining that prevents leakage. The low cost option must be offered wherever ornamented or lined caskets are displayed.
  52. The correct answer is C (It must be written in plain language and legibly printed in 10 point or larger type.): Section 54 of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires the price list to be written in plain language. The same section requires it to be legibly printed in 10 point or larger type. A bold face or handwriting is not what the regulation addresses, and the size rule is the part candidates often forget.
  53. The correct answer is B (A clear indication of which caskets are not suitable for cremation.): Section 62 (2) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires the price list to clearly indicate which caskets are not suitable for cremation. A family choosing cremation depends on that information when it compares casket prices. Manufacturer names and guarantees are not what the provision requires.
  54. The correct answer is C (Provide the copy free of charge to the person who requested it.): Section 68 (1) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires every operator to provide a copy of the price list, without charge, to every person who requests one. No appointment, deposit or contact information is a condition of receiving it. A person comparing prices is exactly the person the rule is meant to protect.
  55. The correct answer is A (That it is always up to date and its effective date.): Section 55 (6) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires the price list to always be up to date and to indicate its effective date. A family must be able to see when the prices took effect. Registrar approval and print run details are not requirements of that provision.
  56. The correct answer is D (The operator's name, the business address and telephone number, and the manager's role.): Section 59 of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires the price list to open with the operator's name, the address and telephone number of the business location and the position, address and telephone number of the person in charge of day-to-day operations. A reader must be able to identify who is responsible for the prices. Employee and supplier details are not part of that list.
  57. The correct answer is A (The package price and what each item would cost if sold separately.): Section 60 of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires the price list to include the details of any package the operator offers, the price of the package and the price at which each supply and service in it would be sold separately. That separate price is what lets a family see whether the package is good value. Wholesale cost and internal margins are not part of the disclosure.
  58. The correct answer is B (A disbursement, because the funeral home pays it on the family's behalf.): A disbursement is a cost the funeral home pays on the family's behalf, such as a newspaper notice or a cemetery charge. The family should know which amounts the funeral home keeps and which pass through to another business. A surcharge is different because it is the operator's own added charge.
  59. The correct answer is C (Not later than the thirty-fifth day after the money is received.): Section 78 (2) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires prepaid trust money to be deposited not later than the 35th day after the day the operator receives the money. The deposit goes into an individual trust account or a pooled trust fund that meets the regulation. Until then the money is still trust money and cannot be used for business expenses.
  60. The correct answer is C (After the thirtieth day, up to the amount it could keep if the purchaser cancelled.): Section 78 (1) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 lets an operator retain, on or after the 30th day after the contract was made, an amount that does not exceed what it would be entitled to keep if the purchaser cancelled under subsection 44 (1) of the Act. Anything above that amount stays trust money. The operator cannot use trust money for general business expenses.
  61. The correct answer is D (An insurance or annuity product that pays out at the time of need.): The two main funding methods for a prefunded arrangement in Ontario are a trust and an insurance or annuity product. In a trust arrangement the money is held in trust for the beneficiary and paid out under Ontario Regulation 30/11. In an insurance or annuity arrangement the contract is funded by the policy proceeds.
  62. The correct answer is D (The operator must not charge the purchaser any additional amount for the increase.): Section 38 of the Act provides that when money is paid in advance and the price rises before the supplies or services are provided, the operator cannot charge the purchaser the additional amount. The operator may keep only the amounts the regulation allows. This protection is one of the main advantages of paying in advance.
  63. The correct answer is C (Prearranging is planning the arrangements, and prefunding is paying for them in advance.): Prearranging means planning and describing funeral services and merchandise before the need arises. Prefunding means paying for those services and merchandise in advance. A family can do one without the other, and the licensee should make that distinction clear before discussing any contract.
  64. The correct answer is A (Caskets, urns, outer burial containers, vaults or grave liners.): Section 62 (1) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires the price list to include the prices for caskets, urns, outer burial containers, vaults or grave liners and related supplies and services. Markers have their own separate price list requirement under section 65. A family comparing cremation and burial costs needs both lists to be complete.
  65. The correct answer is A (The total cost, how the contract is fulfilled later, and the cancellation rights.): The competency profile expects a licensee to discuss the advantages and limitations of prefunding, including a clear explanation of the total cost, how a prepaid contract is fulfilled at the time of need, and the contractual and financial rights and obligations of all parties, including guarantees and cancellation rights. Profit margins and personal details of other clients are not part of that discussion.
  66. The correct answer is D (Describe both processes accurately, including the advantages and disadvantages of each.): The competency profile expects the licensee to explain processes and procedures accurately and thoroughly so the family has enough information to make an informed decision, including the potential advantages and disadvantages. Recommending one option takes the choice away from the family. Describing both is what supports an informed decision.
  67. The correct answer is B (Ask what range he is comfortable with and present options within that range.): The competency profile expects the licensee to identify the purchaser's financial parameters in a respectful and sensitive manner. That means asking what range the family is comfortable with, not asking intrusive questions about savings or involving relatives without permission. Presenting options within the range the family sets keeps the discussion honest.
  68. The correct answer is B (Check the cemetery by-laws and explain the rules that apply to that burial.): Each cemetery sets rules through its by-laws, and those rules affect what a green or natural burial may include. The licensee should find out what the cemetery permits and explain it accurately before the family commits to a choice. Quoting a total before that check produces a price the operator may not be able to honour.
  69. The correct answer is B (Contact the medical school to confirm whether it can accept the body.): The competency profile expects the licensee to explain available disposition options, including anatomical donation of organs, tissue or a whole body. Because each school has its own acceptance rules and timing limits, the licensee should confirm the facts with the school before advising the family. Guessing or refusing to discuss the option leaves the family without accurate information.
  70. The correct answer is D (Give the family the price list and time to review it before signing the contract.): Section 113 of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires the operator to make sure that, before a contract is entered into, the prospective purchaser has received a copy of the price list and the other prescribed disclosure. A verbal summary or a waiver does not replace that step. Giving the list first also protects the operator from a cancellation claim.
  71. The correct answer is D (The policy pays at the time of need, and the contract terms govern any excess.): An insurance funded contract is paid from the proceeds of an insurance or annuity policy at the time of need. The family should understand who receives any excess proceeds and what happens if the contract is cancelled. Claiming that a policy covers the whole cost is not accurate because the amount depends on the policy.
  72. The correct answer is B (The agreed supplies and services are provided, paid from the trust or policy funds.): A prepaid contract is carried out at the time of need by providing the supplies and services that were agreed on, using the trust money or insurance proceeds that fund it. Section 38 of the Act also protects the purchaser from a later price increase. Asking the family to pay current prices and return the prepaid money defeats the purpose of the arrangement.
  73. The correct answer is D (The plan's fees and how the choice of payment option can affect cancellation rights.): Section 113 of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires an offer to explain funding, financing and payment options, including any penalties or fees and how the choice affects rights and refunds on cancellation. A payment plan does not shorten or remove the family's cancellation rights. Explaining the fees up front lets the family compare the plan with paying in one amount.
  74. The correct answer is A (Check the current program requirements and explain what may be available.): The competency profile expects the licensee to provide clear information about government requirements and benefits that may be available. Because program rules and amounts change, the licensee should confirm the current requirements rather than guess or promise an amount. The family can then decide how to proceed with accurate information.
  75. The correct answer is D (Explain that the operator does not offer the plan and discuss the options it does offer.): The licensee must explain the payment options the operator actually provides and cannot invent a plan or move money into an undocumented arrangement. If the operator's plans do not fit, the honest answer is to say so and then look at what the family can manage, such as a less expensive arrangement. Promising a plan that does not exist creates a contract the operator cannot honour.
  76. The correct answer is B (Explain why the casket cannot be used and offer a suitable alternative for cremation.): The price list must indicate which caskets are not suitable for cremation under section 62 (2) of Ontario Regulation 30/11. The licensee cannot override that safety and operational limit, and a signed waiver does not make an unsuitable casket acceptable. The honest response is to explain the limit and offer a container that is suitable.
  77. The correct answer is C (Present the lower priced options early and let the family choose freely.): The competency profile expects the licensee to present, describe and explain the merchandise offered for sale and to communicate the price list as it applies to the options offered. A family that has raised cost should see that a lower priced choice is normal and available. Guessing at a family's income or showing only premium merchandise pressures the family toward spending more.
  78. The correct answer is A (That the choice is a normal one and reflects the family's own priorities.): When a family chooses a less expensive option, the licensee treats the choice as completely normal and never suggests that a lower cost choice means less love or respect for the deceased. Agreeing with the relative would pressure the family to spend more. The licensee's role is to support the choice the family has made.
  79. The correct answer is D (Identify each charge, say who receives it and what the funeral home keeps.): A disbursement is a cost the funeral home pays on the family's behalf, such as a newspaper notice or a cemetery charge. The family should know which amounts the funeral home keeps and which pass through to another business. Combining the charges into one line hides that distinction and makes the bill harder to check.
  80. The correct answer is B (Thank the family, give the written summary and invite questions later.): The law assumes that a grieving purchaser needs clear information, time and a written record, and a pressured decision is the opposite of informed consent. The licensee should let the family leave with the information and make contact again when it is ready. A limited time offer or a fear of rising prices is pressure, not service.
  81. The correct answer is C (It is made before the need arises and is funded in advance by trust or insurance.): A pre-need or prearranged contract is made before the need arises, and when it is prefunded the money is held in trust or the contract is funded by insurance or an annuity. An at-need contract is made when the death has occurred and the arrangements are needed at once. A pre-need contract does not lose its cancellation rights simply because payments have begun.
  82. The correct answer is B (Into a trust account or fund, and it is paid out as services are provided.): Prepaid trust money must be deposited into an individual trust account or a pooled trust fund, kept separate from other money, and paid out only in the ways the regulation permits. It is paid to the operator as the supplies and services are provided under the contract. Trust money is not the operator's money and cannot be used for business expenses.
  83. The correct answer is D (The purchaser may cancel in writing within 30 days and must be refunded within 30 days.): Section 42 of the Act gives the purchaser of a contract that meets the requirements in subsection 40 (1) a 30 day cooling-off period, which runs from the day the last requirement is met. The purchaser cancels by giving written notice, and the operator must refund the money within 30 days of receiving it. This is one of the rights the licensee must explain before the purchaser signs.
  84. The correct answer is C (The answer depends on the funding method chosen, because a trust contract may be transferable.): A trust funded contract is generally portable and can be transferred, while an insurance funded contract is governed by the terms of the policy. The licensee should explain both possibilities rather than promise a refund or a transfer that the contract does not provide. The purchaser needs that information before signing.
  85. The correct answer is C (That the cemetery does not require an outer container and the family may choose one.): The licensee must present, describe and explain the merchandise offered and must offer less expensive options honestly. If the cemetery by-laws do not require a vault or liner, the family should be told that the purchase is optional. Implying that the law requires the container would steer the family toward spending more than it needs to.
  86. The correct answer is A (A representative sample of upright and flat markers with a range of prices.): Section 65 (1) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires the price list to include prices for a representative sample of the upright and flat markers available, including a range of sizes, materials and prices. It also covers foundations, installation and inspections. Showing a single sample or hiding the prices does not give the family the comparison the regulation requires.
  87. The correct answer is A (Say early what the operator cannot provide and explain the alternatives that are available.): The competency profile expects the licensee to communicate the limits of the operator's scope of practice and the services or facilities that are available. Saying so early lets the family plan around the limit instead of discovering it later. Accepting a request the operator cannot deliver creates a promise the funeral home cannot keep.
  88. The correct answer is B (How the rental works, what it costs and when the cremation container is used.): The price list must indicate whether a supply is available for rent, and the licensee must explain the option accurately as it applies to the family's choices. A rental casket is one of the less expensive alternatives that a family may not know about. Explaining how it works lets the family keep the visitation without buying merchandise it does not need.
  89. The correct answer is C (Explain what a total would cover and what it would not, and wait for the choices.): When a family asks for a total before choosing services, the licensee explains what the total covers and what it does not. A price that depends on choices the family has not made cannot be quoted honestly. A total built from averages or extremes is not the price the family will actually pay.
  90. The correct answer is D (Describe each option accurately and explain the rules, costs and limits that apply to it.): The competency profile expects the licensee to explain available options for disposition and memorialization, including entombment and the interment or scattering of cremated remains, with the potential advantages and disadvantages of each. The family should hear about timing, cost, permanence and the ability to visit a place before deciding. Recommending one option takes the choice away from the family.
  91. The correct answer is D (Refuse the trade, give the price list and explain the cancellation rights before signing.): A licensee must not trade a required disclosure step for a price concession, because the family cannot give up its right to the information the Act requires. Giving the price list and explaining the cancellation rights takes only a few minutes and protects the family. A hurried decision is exactly the situation the disclosure rules are designed for.
  92. The correct answer is B (Refuse, because the money is trust money and must be deposited within 35 days.): Prepaid trust money must be kept separate from other money and deposited into an individual trust account or a pooled trust fund not later than the 35th day after the operator receives it. It is not the operator's money, and using it for business expenses or delaying the deposit breaches the Act and the regulation. Convenience in bookkeeping is not a reason to hold trust money.
  93. The correct answer is A (Explain that the guarantee depends on the funding method and review the options first.): The competency profile expects the licensee to discuss the advantages and limitations of each funding option, including guarantees. A trust funded contract may not keep pace with the price of the services, while an insurance or annuity product may guarantee the services regardless of price increases. Promising a guarantee before the funding method is known is a promise the licensee cannot support.
  94. The correct answer is C (The licensee is steering the family toward a costlier choice by disparaging the cheaper one.): The licensee must present less expensive options honestly and must not steer a family toward a more expensive choice. Describing a lower cost casket in a way that suggests it is not good enough for a proper viewing pressures the family to spend more. Describing quality honestly is allowed, but the description must not be used as a sales lever.
  95. The correct answer is A (A family member may provide funeral services without charge, other than arterial embalming.): Section 7 (2) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 exempts a family member from subsection 8 (2) of the Act when the family member provides funeral services for the deceased for no consideration, other than arterial embalming. The licensee should not tell a family that a simple home funeral is unlawful. The licensee can explain what the family may do and where it will still need professional help.
  96. The correct answer is A (Refuse, because false documents about the sale of services are prohibited.): Section 31 of the Act prohibits a licensee from furnishing, assisting in furnishing or inducing another person to furnish false, misleading or deceptive information or documents relating to the sale or provision of licensed supplies or services. A receipt for an amount that was not paid is a false document. A note from the family cannot make the receipt lawful.
  97. The correct answer is D (The family gets a signed copy when it is signed or as soon as reasonably practicable.): The competency profile expects the licensee to provide the purchaser with a signed copy of the contract at the time it is signed, or as soon as is reasonably practicable, or as required by law. Section 40 of the Act also makes delivery of a signed copy part of an enforceable contract. Mailing a copy the following week may fall outside what the law requires.
  98. The correct answer is C (The family cannot compare value, because the package contents and prices were never explained.): The price list must show the package price and the price at which each item would be sold separately, and the licensee must explain the list as it applies to the options offered. A package price without that explanation hides what the family is buying. Common problems in this area include leaving a package price unexplained, and prevention comes down to order and documentation.
  99. The correct answer is A (Confirm what the operator and local providers offer, then explain the process accurately.): The competency profile expects the licensee to explain available options for disposition and memorialization and to communicate the limits of the operator's scope of practice and services. Availability of an alternative process depends on what local providers are licensed and equipped to offer, so the licensee should check before answering. Promising the process or dismissing it without checking misleads the family.
  100. The correct answer is B (The operator must not generally provide the supplies or services within 30 days.): Section 43 of the Act provides that, except as permitted by the regulations, an operator shall not provide licensed supplies or services under the contract within 30 days after the day the contract is made. The purchaser also has a right to cancel during that period if the contract has not been fully performed. Written consent from the family does not remove the waiting period.
  101. The correct answer is B (The direction of the sister, because she signed as the legal representative.): The person who signed the arrangement contract is the legal representative and holds the authority to direct the plan. Other relatives may give their views, and the licensee may pass those views along. The licensee follows the legal representative unless a court or another lawful authority decides otherwise.
  102. The correct answer is C (A copy of the finalized arrangement summary for the family's records.): The family should leave with a written summary of every confirmed detail, and the funeral home keeps a matching copy in the file. Staff work from that same summary, and any later change is recorded and shared with everyone affected. A spoken summary alone leaves the family and the staff holding different versions of the plan.
  103. The correct answer is A (To let the family confirm the person in the casket is deceased.): Body identification is the family's confirmation that the person in the casket or container is the deceased. The licensee prepares the setting and presents the body professionally and with dignity. The licensee records who performed the identification and when it took place, and records the decision if the family declines to view.
  104. The correct answer is C (The name plate and the file agree on the person inside.): Before the lid is raised, the licensee checks that the casket identification, the name plate and the file all agree with the person inside. The competency profile also expects the licensee to confirm the identification of the human remains and of the casket, container or urn. A check at this point prevents the wrong person being presented to a family.
  105. The correct answer is B (A reserved area for the family, the coach and the casket bearers.): The competency profile lists a reserved parking area for the family, the funeral coach, the casket bearers and others as part of preparing the environment. Reserving it keeps the family and the coach close to the entrance and leaves the remaining spaces for guests. The licensee marks and, where needed, monitors the area as guests arrive.
  106. The correct answer is A (The register book, memorial folders and prayer cards for guests.): The competency profile lists the register book, memorial folders, prayer cards and other items as part of preparing the environment for a visitation. The register book records who attended, and families often keep it afterwards. Contracts, permits and staff schedules belong in the office file rather than on the guest table.
  107. The correct answer is D (Visitation, the funeral service, then the committal at the grave.): The visitation comes first and gives family and friends time with the deceased. The funeral service follows, and the committal takes place at the grave or crematorium as the remains are placed. The written plan lets staff set up each part in the right order and move the family between them.
  108. The correct answer is A (A memorial service, since the body is not in the building.): A memorial service is held without the remains present, and the photographs, music and eulogy carry the tribute. A committal is the rite at the grave or crematorium, not a gathering in the chapel. The licensee confirms which of these the family has chosen and records it in the plan.
  109. The correct answer is B (The committal service, held as the remains are placed in the earth.): The committal is the final rite of the day and takes place at the grave, the columbarium or the crematorium. The licensee keeps the family and the officiant in position and manages the move back to the vehicles afterwards. The eulogy and the vigil are parts of the earlier gathering, not the burial itself.
  110. The correct answer is D (A viewing, a period with the body before the wider gathering.): A viewing is time with the body, and it may be private or held just before the visitation opens. A visitation is the longer period when guests call to pay their respects, whether or not the body is present. The licensee confirms which arrangement the family has asked for and prepares the room accordingly.
  111. The correct answer is B (As a prescribed funeral service, since the cremated remains are present.): Section 1 (3) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 prescribes the co-ordination and provision of rites and ceremonies with respect to cremated human remains as funeral services. The ceremony must be provided in close proximity to the time of death and the remains must be present. Rites taking place at the time of interment or scattering are excluded from that description.
  112. The correct answer is C (Ask the family and the officiant what the funeral home should do.): A licensee is not expected to know every custom in detail, and the family and officiant are the people who can describe what the funeral home should do. Where a custom creates a practical problem, the licensee solves the problem without changing the custom. The plan is then written down and given to the staff working the service.
  113. The correct answer is B (Mark the family seats clearly so that guests do not take them.): The competency profile expects the licensee to designate reserved seating as part of preparing the environment. Marking the seats keeps the front area free for the family and saves a guest from being asked to move later. The licensee also keeps the sensitivity note from the arrangement in mind when the room fills.
  114. The correct answer is D (At the time of day when the visitation will be held.): Daylight changes through the day, so a room checked in the morning can look quite different in the afternoon. The licensee sets the lighting at the hour the family and guests will actually be in the room. Soft, even light that does not fall as a harsh beam on the face is the aim.
  115. The correct answer is A (That the family declined the viewing and what was offered instead.): The licensee records who made the decision and the alternatives that were explained, such as a closed casket visitation or a private family gathering. Recording the decision protects the family and the funeral home if questions come up later. The record does not suggest that the family was advised against viewing.
  116. The correct answer is C (Confirm with the legal representative which plan the family wants now.): The written plan and what the room is actually set for no longer match, so the licensee goes back to the legal representative and confirms the direction before the family walks in. The staff are corrected quietly, and the change, if there is one, is recorded in the file. The funeral home's convenience never decides the question.
  117. The correct answer is B (Confirm the change with the musician and announce it at the service.): The change can be honoured without a reprint, so the licensee tells the musician, tells the officiant and announces the change at the start of the service. The family is told what is possible and what is not, and the change is written into the file. Reprinting every folder is not needed when the order of service itself has not changed.
  118. The correct answer is B (Check the documentation with the family before the folders are printed.): A date that does not match the record is settled before anything is printed, and the family is shown the documentation so the decision is theirs. Printing either version without checking risks a mistake on a keepsake the family will keep for years. Leaving the date off the folder removes a detail the family asked for.
  119. The correct answer is D (Soft, even light and a staff member available nearby if needed.): The competency profile expects the licensee to position the container appropriately, set the lighting and present the body professionally and with dignity. The family decides how much company they want, so the staff member stays close enough to help and far enough to give privacy. Bright overhead light and direct sunlight fall harshly on the face.
  120. The correct answer is C (Check the name plate and the file against the person quietly.): A family's reaction at the casket is taken seriously, and the licensee rechecks the identification tag, the name plate and the file before saying anything to the family. If the check finds an error, the licensee corrects it, tells the operator and speaks with the family privately. Telling the daughter that she is mistaken before the check is complete has the order of events backwards.
  121. The correct answer is D (Cool the room now and check the temperature before the doors open.): The room is cooled early enough to be comfortable when the family walks in, and the temperature is checked again as the doors open. A room that is warm at the start becomes warmer once it fills with people. The licensee fixes the problem before the family feels it rather than waiting for a comment.
  122. The correct answer is C (Soften the beam so that no harsh light reaches the face.): Lighting is adjusted until it is soft and even, and the licensee checks how it falls on the people seated near the casket. Moving the family member's chair treats the person rather than the cause and shifts the problem to the next guest. Turning the lights off leaves the room too dark for a visitation.
  123. The correct answer is A (Keep it safely for the family and confirm who will collect it later.): The register book holds the names the family needs for thank you notes, so the licensee stores it safely and agrees with the family who will pick it up. The second session uses its own book or a continuation of the same one, as the family prefers. Leaving the book on the table risks losing pages that cannot be replaced.
  124. The correct answer is A (Set the items out securely and record them for return to the family.): Memorabilia is part of the environment the competency profile expects the licensee to arrange, and items of value are listed so they can be accounted for. When the service ends, the licensee returns everything on the list and records that it went back. Deciding to give an item away is the family's decision, not the licensee's.
  125. The correct answer is C (Move it and reattach the card, keeping the sender's name visible.): The arrangement is repositioned so the family can be seen and greeted, and the card is put back where it can be read. Floral tributes and cards are part of the room the licensee sets up and monitors during the visitation. The card stays with the tribute, and the tribute stays in the room.
  126. The correct answer is A (Send the limousine for the family and bring them to the church.): The competency profile expects the licensee to send vehicle or vehicles for the family as appropriate and to make arrangements for the family and the officiant to reach the right place. Collecting the family keeps the schedule and keeps the family together on the way. Leaving the family to find its own way to the church adds a problem to a morning that should run smoothly.
  127. The correct answer is A (Speak with the guests quietly and move them to other seats.): The guests are greeted politely and walked to good seats elsewhere, so the reserved rows are free without anyone being embarrassed. A public announcement would draw attention to the problem and to the family. Asking the family to sit wherever they can find space ignores the seating the family was promised.
  128. The correct answer is B (Clear the path and steady the carpet before they walk through.): The room is arranged with a clear path for anyone using a wheelchair or walker, so the obstruction and the turned carpet are fixed before the guest moves. The guest keeps her independence and her safety at the same time. Walking beside her without fixing the route leaves the hazard in place for the next person.
  129. The correct answer is A (The number of guests, the arrival time and any dietary needs.): The caterer needs the count, the time the family will return and any food restrictions the family has mentioned. The licensee confirms those details in writing and updates them if the plan changes. Catering is a service the family has arranged for the reception, so the licensee passes the family's information along as the coordinator.
  130. The correct answer is A (The tent, the chairs, the lowering device and the floral tributes.): The licensee arrives ahead of the family and confirms the grave area is ready, with the tent, seating, lowering equipment and flowers in place. Any problem is fixed before the procession arrives, when there is still time. Costs, staff names and road conditions are handled in the office and the planning stage.
  131. The correct answer is C (Replace him with staff or another bearer and brief the team.): A bearer who cannot lift safely is replaced without embarrassment, and the new bearer is shown where to stand and what to do. The competency profile expects the licensee to direct casket bearers in all aspects of their involvement. Telling the family about the change at the church adds a worry on a morning when the family should be free to grieve.
  132. The correct answer is B (Give the family private time and check nothing is left inside.): The family gets the quiet moment they asked for, and before the lid comes down the licensee checks for personal items the family wants to keep. The competency profile expects the casket or container to be closed properly, and it is closed at the time the family chose. The guests are directed away from the moment rather than asked to watch it.
  133. The correct answer is C (Who gives the signal to begin and who closes the casket.): The competency profile expects the licensee to meet with the officiant and review the final details for the funeral, including the entrances, exits and the order of events. Agreeing a hand signal or a nod keeps the service flowing without whispered instructions in front of the family. The fee and the parking are settled before this meeting, and the speakers are the family's business.
  134. The correct answer is A (The arrival time and the person who will bring the casket there.): The licensee confirms the timing of the arrival and who is responsible for the transfer, along with any paperwork the crematorium needs. That agreement keeps the handover quick and keeps the family from waiting. The eulogy and the music are settled with the officiant and the family, not with the crematorium.
  135. The correct answer is D (The arrival time, the parking and the route for the procession.): The licensee confirms when the procession will arrive, where the vehicles will park and how the casket will enter the church. The bearers are told the route and the timing before the morning arrives. A church the funeral home has not served recently holds details the licensee checks in advance rather than guesses at the door.
  136. The correct answer is D (The access, the seating, the washroom and the available floor space.): A service in a private residence is planned around what the house can hold and how people will get in and out. The licensee checks the step, the room, the washroom and the parking, then tells the family what can be done in the space. If the house cannot hold the number expected, the licensee brings the family a workable alternative before the plan is fixed.
  137. The correct answer is B (The hall's rules, the setup and the closing time for the evening.): The licensee confirms the house rules, how the room will be set and when the hall must be returned, and passes those limits to the family and the caterer. A rented space usually carries its own restrictions on catering, alcohol and hours. The number of volunteers and the colour scheme are not the funeral home's decisions.
  138. The correct answer is C (Keep the timing and give the family the quiet cues as agreed.): In a family led service the family holds the ceremony and the licensee keeps time, cues the speakers and watches the room from the back. That support keeps the service on its agreed order without the funeral home taking over. Waiting in the office leaves the family without the help it asked for.
  139. The correct answer is A (The lighting, the temperature, the seating and the casket placement.): The walk through confirms the room is ready for the people about to enter it, with the casket placed properly, the seats set and the air and light comfortable. The licensee also checks the register, the music level and the display one last time. Staff assignments, costs and guest names were settled well before this moment.
  140. The correct answer is C (A search for lost items, and securing the register and donations.): The room is swept for property left behind, and the register, donations and any memorial items are secured for the family. Lost property is logged so it can be returned to the right person. The licensee also confirms who locks up and records anything unusual from the evening in the file.
  141. The correct answer is D (Move the guest into a private room and call for help.): The guest is moved out of the room and medical help is called at once, which protects the guest's dignity and keeps the family's service undisturbed. The licensee stays with the guest, sends someone for help or calls emergency services, and has another person keep the family and the other guests settled. Announcing a call for a doctor across the room would alarm everyone, and waiting for the guest to recover where they fell risks their health.
  142. The correct answer is D (Take her to the quiet room and stay with her discreetly.): The licensee takes her where she is not on display, stays with her and lets her decide when she is ready to go in. A quiet room away from the door is part of the environment the licensee prepares for exactly this reason. Sending her straight to the casket or asking other guests to help out takes the decision away from her.
  143. The correct answer is C (Move the brothers to a private room and speak with them quietly.): The brothers are taken away from the guests, heard out and helped to bring the question to the legal representative rather than settle it in the chapel. The visitation carries on while the licensee handles the matter out of sight. Correcting them in public or leaving the argument in place lets a private dispute become the family's public memory.
  144. The correct answer is D (Speak with the cousin privately and return to the agreed order of service.): The licensee approaches the cousin without a scene, thanks him and explains that the family has asked for the agreed order, which the licensee is there to keep. The officiant is briefed so the service can pick up where it left off. Publicly stopping him or letting the speech run both take the service away from the legal representative's direction.
  145. The correct answer is A (Keep the cameras away from the family and let the spokesperson speak.): The family's direction is carried out quietly, with the family given a private route in and out and all questions directed to the person the family named. The licensee does not give interviews or release details about the service. Reporters are people doing a job, and a plan agreed with the family in advance keeps the day dignified for everyone.
  146. The correct answer is B (Greet him courteously, seat him apart and offer to pass a message.): The staff member follows the plan agreed with the family before the visitation, treating the brother with courtesy while keeping the reserved area for the family. If he asks to speak with his sister, the staff member offers to pass a message rather than deciding for the family. If his conduct becomes disruptive, the licensee speaks with him privately away from the casket and the guests.
  147. The correct answer is D (Release the urn to the daughter and explain this to the uncle.): The competency profile expects cremated remains to be returned in a respectful manner to the appropriate individual, and the direction of the legal representative decides who that is. The licensee explains the position to the uncle calmly and privately, without taking sides in a family dispute. Handing the urn to whoever asks first puts the funeral home in the middle of the disagreement.
  148. The correct answer is B (Explain the cemetery's by-law and review the options with the family.): The cemetery's by-law is a real limit, so the licensee brings it to the family with the choices that go with it, such as a different cemetery or a different container. The family keeps the decision and the funeral home does not promise something the cemetery will not allow. Ordering a container without telling the family spends their money on something they did not choose.
  149. The correct answer is D (Hold the extra guests in another area and check with the family.): The size of the service is the family's decision, so the licensee makes the arriving guests comfortable elsewhere and asks the legal representative how to proceed. The plan can then change with the family's agreement rather than by the funeral home's choice. Admitting everyone changes a private service, and refusing entry on a cold doorstep is not how guests are treated.
  150. The correct answer is B (Leading the congregation in the prayers set out in the programme.): The licensee supports the officiant and keeps the order and rhythm of the service, and the worship itself belongs to the officiant and the family's faith community. Standing where the family, the officiant and the door can be seen, giving quiet signals and placing the bearers are all part of directing the funeral. A licensee who takes over the prayers steps into a role the family did not give the funeral home.
  151. The correct answer is B (Confirming the planned route against construction notices, road closures and school zone times.): A procession route is planned in advance, checking for construction, events, school zones and any other condition that could delay the group. The arrival time is confirmed with the cemetery or crematorium as part of the same plan. Checking the route on the morning of the service leaves no time to change the arrangement.
  152. The correct answer is B (Where to line up, when to start engines and how to follow the vehicle ahead.): Guests are told where to line up, when to start their engines and how to follow the vehicle ahead, together with what to do if they become separated. The competency profile also expects the licensee to remind guests to obey all traffic regulations. A briefing that skips the traffic rules leaves the drivers to guess.
  153. The correct answer is A (Guests must obey signals, stop signs and directions given by a police officer.): A procession does not give anyone the right to ignore a red light, a stop sign or a police direction. The competency profile expects the licensee to organize and direct processions and to remind guests to obey all traffic regulations. Blocking an intersection against the signal endangers the whole group.
  154. The correct answer is C (Follow the directions given by the officers in charge of the escort.): Where a police escort is arranged, the licensee follows the directions of the officers in charge. The officers control the intersections while the funeral home staff keep the group orderly behind them. Guest drivers are told to stay in line and to obey the signals and directions they meet.
  155. The correct answer is B (Increase the following distance and reduce the speed of the group.): In snow, heavy rain or fog, the safe response is a longer following distance and a lower speed. The licensee also watches for the point at which the conditions make the route unsafe. Weather is a planning factor, so the decision is made before the group is committed to the road.
  156. The correct answer is D (Keep a staff member with the vehicle and send help while the coach departs.): The family is the priority, so the coach and the family vehicles leave together while a staff member stays with the disabled vehicle and arranges assistance. The remaining guests are briefed on the route so they can follow at their own pace. Telling the family briefly what has been arranged prevents worry later.
  157. The correct answer is C (Hold the group briefly, call the cemetery and ask the family what they prefer.): A short delay is normal, and the cemetery can usually absorb it when the funeral home calls ahead. The licensee tells the family briefly what is happening and lets them decide when the choice is theirs to make. The call and the decision are recorded in the file.
  158. The correct answer is A (Telephone the cemetery, explain the delay and ask how much flexibility exists.): The cemetery knows its own schedule, so the licensee calls, explains the delay and asks how much flexibility exists before promising anything to the family. If the delay cannot be absorbed, the family decides whether to proceed or move the committal. The decision is recorded in the file.
  159. The correct answer is B (Confirm the receiving cemetery has accepted the transfer and the paperwork is complete.): A transfer to another province involves a receiving cemetery and a longer list of documents, so the arrangements are confirmed before the coach leaves. The competency profile expects the licensee to arrange for and supervise disposition by a third party, such as a shipout. Departing before the receiving end has confirmed the plan risks a family arriving with nowhere to go.
  160. The correct answer is D (The flower car, which is assigned to carry the floral tributes.): The flower car carries the tributes from the church to the graveside so they can be arranged before the family arrives. The lead car guides the group, the family car carries the immediate family and the coach carries the casket. Loading the tributes into the coach would leave no room for the casket and the bearers.
  161. The correct answer is A (Use headlights so the group can stay together in the failing light.): Headlights help the group stay together in low light and make the vehicles easier for others to see. The licensee drives carefully and predictably and does not ask guests to break a traffic rule to keep up. High beams and hazard lights used for the whole trip create glare and confusion.
  162. The correct answer is C (Recommend a shorter return route and check that the relatives arrive safely.): When conditions become unsafe, the licensee considers an alternate route or a shorter procession and tells the family what the road reports say. Elderly relatives driving themselves need the same advice the funeral home follows. Weather does not remove the duty to warn and check.
  163. The correct answer is D (Allow the travel time plus a buffer for traffic delays and parking.): A realistic schedule includes the travel time plus a buffer for traffic, parking and the small delays that happen at every cemetery. Clear instructions to the guests also shorten the time spent forming the procession. A service that runs late then has room to recover.
  164. The correct answer is B (Assign the available licensed colleague so that the coach leaves on time.): The family is waiting and the coach is due to leave, so the licensee fills the missing position with the available licensed colleague and keeps the group on time. The absent driver is contacted afterwards and the reason is recorded. Asking guests to guide themselves through traffic puts the family at risk.
  165. The correct answer is C (That the grave, the lowering device and the tent are ready.): The funeral director coordinates with the cemetery staff so that the site is ready, the equipment is in place and the family has what it needs. The tent, the chairs and the placement of the tributes are checked before the procession arrives. A committal that waits for the site to be prepared is the outcome this check prevents.
  166. The correct answer is A (The lowering device, which supports the casket over the grave.): The lowering device holds the casket in position over the grave during the committal and then lowers it. The vault or grave liner is the outer container placed in the grave, and the coach only transports the casket. The licensee confirms with the cemetery staff that the device is in place before the family arrives.
  167. The correct answer is D (Supervise the correct closure of the outer container before leaving.): The competency profile expects the licensee to supervise the correct closure of the outer container. Cemetery staff often do the physical work, and the licensee stays to confirm that it is done correctly. Leaving the closure entirely to others gives up the supervision the task requires.
  168. The correct answer is B (That the tent, the chairs and the family seating are arranged in time.): The tent, the chairs and the family seating are arranged before the procession arrives so the family can be seated without delay. The licensee also confirms where the tributes will be placed and how the officiant will be supported. A cold afternoon outdoors makes shelter and seating part of the family's comfort.
  169. The correct answer is A (Explain the choices and follow the family's direction for the tributes.): Flowers and other tributes are tracked, and the family decides what happens to them after the service. The choices include delivery to a hospital, a care home, a church or the family home, or respectful disposal. The licensee confirms the direction and returns any cards or ribbons the family wants to keep.
  170. The correct answer is C (Arrange and supervise the interment when the ground opens in spring.): The competency profile expects the licensee to arrange for and supervise a postponed disposition, such as a spring interment or winter storage. The file shows what was agreed, what was paid and what will happen when the ground opens. The family is told the date as soon as the cemetery confirms it.
  171. The correct answer is D (Give the family time at the grave and hold the staff back.): The family is given time to say goodbye, and the staff wait until they are ready. The licensee supports the officiant and keeps the schedule flexible enough for this part of the day. Rushing the family away from the grave is a service failure that no schedule can justify.
  172. The correct answer is A (That the site is tidy and the tributes are removed or placed.): When the family has left, the licensee confirms that the site is left in a respectful condition. The tributes are removed or placed as the family directed, and the equipment is gathered. Anything that needs attention is noted in the file before the staff leave.
  173. The correct answer is C (At the church before the service, with clear instructions and a walk through.): Casket bearers are directed in all aspects of their involvement, so the briefing happens before the service rather than at the grave. Plain instructions on positions, pace and hand placement prevent confusion. The licensee also names the staff member who will signal the bearers during the service.
  174. The correct answer is B (Three on each side of the casket, evenly spaced along the rails.): With six bearers, three stand on each side of the casket and take an even share of the weight along the rails. The licensee positions them before the casket is lifted and checks that the route is clear. An uneven arrangement puts both the bearers and the casket at risk on steps.
  175. The correct answer is D (Set the casket on the lowering device, then step back and seat the family.): The casket is placed on the lowering device, the bearers step back and the family is seated for the committal. The officiant then leads the service with the family in place. The order matters because the family should not be left standing while the bearers settle the casket.
  176. The correct answer is C (Stop, add a staff bearer and continue once the casket is level again.): Safety comes first, so the licensee stops the movement, adds a staff bearer and continues when the casket is level and the team is steady. The change is made without drawing attention to the pallbearer. The incident is noted in the file afterwards.
  177.
