The correct answer is A (A certificate issued by the coroner authorizing the cremation.): A crematorium operator shall not permit the cremation of a dead human body unless a certificate issued by the coroner authorizing the cremation has been received. The same requirement must be stated in the contract for cremation. Without the certificate, the cremation cannot proceed at all.
  178. The correct answer is B (The pacemaker must be taken out by a person the regulation allows.): A crematorium operator shall not permit the cremation of a body that has a pacemaker. The operator may contract out or arrange for the removal, and the regulation limits who may be engaged to do that work. A pacemaker left inside a body creates a serious risk in the cremation chamber.
  179. The correct answer is C (When two years have passed, or sooner if it is safe to cremate.): The radioactive implant rule has a narrow exception. The operator may permit cremation when it is at least two years after the day the body received the implant, or when a lesser time has passed and it is safe to cremate the body. A pacemaker has no matching exception.
  180. The correct answer is D (It may not be cremated because the casket contains prohibited material.): The crematorium operator shall not permit cremation of a body in a casket that has on or in it material made of or containing non-flammable or hazardous material or chlorinated or fibre reinforced plastic, apart from incidental metal. The incidental metal exception does not cover the shell, so this casket is unsuitable for cremation. The family is told which caskets cannot be used for cremation.
  181. The correct answer is B (A Class 1 funeral director acting for a licensed operator or a physician.): Section 52 allows an operator to engage a Class 1 funeral director who is acting on behalf of a holder of a Funeral Establishment Operator Class 1 licence or a Transfer Service Operator Class 1 licence, or a legally qualified medical practitioner. The operator arranges the removal and the file records who performed it. A crematorium operator may also contract out the removal to a person described in section 52.
  182. The correct answer is A (That the identity of the deceased and the casket have been confirmed.): The licensee confirms the identity of the deceased and the casket and hands over the documents the crematorium needs. Confirming identification at the hand off protects against a mix-up later. The cremated remains are then returned according to the family's direction and the regulation.
  183. The correct answer is D (Six years from the date of the cremation to which it relates.): Section 101(5) requires a crematorium operator to retain the coroner's certificate authorizing the cremation for six years from the date of the cremation. The retention schedule is written down so the records are not destroyed early. An inspector may ask to see the certificates for past cremations.
  184. The correct answer is C (That cremation is not permitted with a pacemaker or an unsuitable casket.): Section 125(1) requires the contract to state that a body shall not be cremated if it has a pacemaker, or a radioactive implant outside the narrow exception, or if it is in a casket containing prohibited material. The contract must also state that a certificate issued by the coroner authorizing the cremation must be received by the operator. The family signs with those limits explained.
  185. The correct answer is A (Confirm who is entitled, record the release and deliver the remains respectfully.): The competency profile expects the licensee to return cremated remains in a respectful manner to the appropriate individuals. The licensee confirms who is entitled to receive them and records the release with a signature. The remains are delivered in person rather than left for collection or sent through the mail.
  186. The correct answer is B (The deceased's name, the receipt date, the authorizing person and the scattering details.): Section 28(1) sets out the record an operator prepares whenever cremated remains are received for scattering. It includes the name of the deceased, the date the remains were received, the name and address of the person who authorized the scattering and the date, manner and place of scattering. The record is kept so a future request can be answered.
  187. The correct answer is D (Disclose the record to her without charge, as the regulation requires.): When the purchaser, personal representative or a family member asks for the scattering record, the operator discloses it free of charge. The daughter's request fits that description, so no fee applies. Refusing or delaying the disclosure does not meet the requirement.
  188. The correct answer is C (Keep the two sets separate unless the purchaser's contract allows co-mingling.): Cremated remains are not co-mingled with other cremated remains when in storage or when being scattered unless the operator's contract with the purchaser authorizes it. Storing the two sets together without that authorization breaches the requirement. The operator also keeps a record of where each set of remains is held.
  189. The correct answer is B (Have the remains interred in a cemetery, including a common lot.): Section 53(2) allows the operator to inter unclaimed cremated remains in a cemetery, including in a common lot, when the remains are not claimed within one year from the date of cremation and reasonable efforts to contact the purchaser, personal representative or a family member have been made. The documented attempts are what make that step permissible. The operator keeps the record of the interment.
  190. The correct answer is A (Refund the amount the price list shows as the refundable deposit.): Section 53(3) requires the operator to refund any refundable deposit charged for the interment if the cremated remains are claimed before they are interred. The price list must state the amount of that deposit, so the refund is easy to confirm. The claim is recorded in the file with the date.
  191. The correct answer is C (Its effective date, and the list must be kept up to date.): Section 55(6) requires the price list to be up to date at all times and to indicate its effective date. The list must also include every licensed supply and service that the operator generally provides, so new prices replace the old ones rather than sitting beside them. A stale list leads to charges that do not match the published price.
  192. The correct answer is D (The operator's name and business name, if the two names differ.): Section 119(1) lists the information that must appear in a sign or written advertisement, brochure, price list, contract, letterhead, pamphlet, circular or other written material used to promote the sale of a licensed supply or service. It includes the operator's name, the operator's business name if it differs from the operator's name, and the name of any person who controls the business in the circumstances the section describes. Business cards are the one written item the section excepts.
  193. The correct answer is A (Six years from the date the contract is fully performed or cancelled.): Section 101(1) requires an operator to retain records relating to a contract for six years from the date the contract is fully performed or is cancelled. Destroying a file before that period ends leaves the operator unable to answer a complaint or an inspection. The retention period is written into the records policy so the decision is not left to memory.
  194. The correct answer is B (Repay the difference of $250 to the purchaser within 30 days.): Section 34(1) of the Act prohibits charging more than the price indicated on the price list, and section 34(2) requires the licensee to repay the difference within 30 days when an overcharge happens. A credit note or a later price change does not satisfy that duty. The correction is recorded in the file so the account and the payment agree.
  195. The correct answer is D (The conditions of sale and a full description of the included supplies and services.): Section 119(6) requires an operator who advertises a price to explain all the conditions of sale that relate to the advertised price and to describe fully the supplies and services that the price includes. A package advertisement that leaves out what is included misleads the reader. The same rule applies to the printed notice and to the website.
  196. The correct answer is C (Recover the dates from the bank records and complete the file accurately.): The competency profile expects a licensee to complete accurately all records required by the business and all required financial transactions with their related record keeping. The dates can be recovered from the deposit records, so the file is corrected from a reliable source. Leaving gaps or guessing creates a record that cannot be trusted.
  197. The correct answer is A (Every licensed supply and service that the operator generally provides to families.): Section 55(1) requires the price list to include every licensed supply and service that the operator generally provides. Urns and memorial folders are licensed supplies, so they belong on the list even when no family has asked for them recently. The list must also describe each item clearly and show its price.
  198. The correct answer is B (Remove the charge, correct the statement and record the correction.): The file shows no record of the limousine service, so the charge cannot be supported and the statement is corrected. The family receives the corrected statement in plain language, with the change explained. The correction and the reason for it are recorded in the file.
  199. The correct answer is C (Speak with the employee privately, explain the dress and conduct policy.): The competency profile expects licensees to present a professional image, to follow professional ethics and to abide by policies on dress and conduct. A private conversation that explains the policy and the reason for it corrects the behaviour and protects the funeral home's standing in the community. A public rebuke or a deduction from pay creates a new problem.
  200. The correct answer is D (Accept both, provide accurate information and cooperate with the reporter.): The competency profile expects licensees to serve as a community resource, to make presentations to community groups and to cooperate with the media. The information given about death, grief and the sector is accurate, and questions that call for legal or medical advice are referred to the right professional. Turning the club and the reporter away gives up a chance to inform the public.
  201. The correct answer is B (The owner makes sure the business follows governing legislation and keeps legal knowledge current.): The competency profile places two linked duties on an owner or manager. The business must be operated pursuant to relevant governing legislation, and the owner or manager must maintain current knowledge of applicable laws and codes. Because the law changes, a single review or a wait for the regulator does not discharge either duty.
  202. The correct answer is A (Review it each year and after every significant change in the law.): A practical compliance system collects the licences, renewal dates, records, reporting duties, inspection requirements and policies in one place. Reviewing that list at least once a year, and again after a significant change in the law, keeps it usable. Waiting for a renewal, a family request or an inspection leaves gaps that an inspector or a complaint can expose.
  203. The correct answer is D (Employment and human rights law, health and safety law, privacy, taxation and municipal rules.): The competency profile names the laws governing the sale of funeral services, relevant family and privacy law, human rights and employment law, codes of conduct set by the regulatory body, and municipal, health and safety, labour, taxation and consumer protection requirements. The FBCSA and its regulations are central but not exclusive. A business that follows only the Act can still breach an employment, privacy or safety obligation.
  204. The correct answer is C (Review the change, update the affected policy and train the payroll staff before the date.): An owner or manager must keep the business operating under current law and keep current knowledge of legal change. Reading the change, adjusting the affected policy and training the staff who apply it turns that knowledge into compliant practice. A penalty absorbed by a provider, a hope that the change may not apply or a wait until the effective date leaves the business not compliant in the meantime.
  205. The correct answer is A (Write the policy in plain language, train the staff and review it regularly.): A policy protects people only when the staff know it, understand it and follow it. Plain language, training and regular review are the practices the competency profile supports when it asks an owner or manager to develop and implement policies and procedures. A policy kept in one office, revised once or left to personal habit does not achieve that.
  206. The correct answer is C (Use the approved greeting and avoid promising what the home cannot deliver.): Training for a receptionist is specific to the task, and the chapter describes a script for the initial enquiry together with a list of what must never be promised. A consistent greeting and a limit on promises protect the family from misinformation and protect the business from a commitment it cannot keep. Quoting a final price, promising every request or sending the caller to the embalmer all go beyond that training.
  207. The correct answer is A (A written standard for attire and behaviour, explained to staff at orientation.): The competency profile asks licensees to abide by policies about telephone etiquette, appropriate dress and conduct, and it asks an owner or manager to develop and implement those policies. A standard that is written, explained and applied gives the staff clear guidance. An unwritten expectation, personal preference or a notice confined to one room does not give the staff a clear standard.
  208. The correct answer is D (Review the file, question the staff involved and write down the findings.): A personal effects record is part of the case file, and its absence is exactly the kind of process error a complaint can expose. The first response is to establish what happened and to record the answer. Denying the item, offering money or waiting for a formal complaint does not settle the facts and can turn a family concern into a regulatory complaint.
  209. The correct answer is C (Writing measurable objectives for the business and reviewing the progress toward them.): The competency profile expects an owner or manager to develop, or take part in developing, long term and short term goals and objectives for the business. Objectives that can be measured and reviewed turn a goal into something the staff can act on. Waiting for the regulator, copying a competitor or replacing the plan with invoices does not do that.
  210. The correct answer is B (The contract must be reviewed and signed by a licensed funeral director.): Subsection 32 (3) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires the funeral establishment operator to make sure that a contract negotiated by an unlicensed student enrolled in a funeral director or funeral preplanner education program is reviewed and signed by a licensed funeral director. A later read, a family signature or a filed copy does not satisfy that condition. The duty sits with the operator, so the review must happen before the contract is treated as complete.
  211. The correct answer is D (Count the merchandise and supplies on a set schedule and record the totals.): The competency profile asks an owner or manager to keep an accurate inventory of merchandise and business supplies. A regular count and a written total give the business a reliable figure for ordering and for the record set. Noticing an empty shelf, guessing from old purchase orders or asking at a performance review does not produce an accurate inventory.
  212. The correct answer is A (Review a sample of completed case files each month and record the findings.): Supervision should be visible and supportive, and the chapter describes a supervisor who reviews a sample of files each month to catch small problems early. Recording the findings shows both the review and the fix. Reporting only the files a family questioned, waiting for an inspection or limiting access to the manager all fail to find problems early.
  213. The correct answer is C (To set out the duties, responsibilities and reporting relationship for the position.): The competency profile expects an owner or manager to develop or assist in developing job descriptions for the staff. A description explains what the position does, who the employee answers to and what the role covers. Replacing other documents, promising hours or serving as a licence record is not the purpose of a job description.
  214. The correct answer is B (Record the training in the employee's file with the date and topics covered.): The competency profile expects an owner or manager to provide appropriate training for personnel and to maintain accurate records of that training. A dated record of the topics shows what was taught and when. Telling coworkers, filing the notes with marketing material or waiting for a performance review does not create a usable training record.
  215. The correct answer is A (Deduct the required amounts from wages and remit them as the law requires.): The competency profile lists adherence to legislative requirements about employees, and it names mandatory source deductions from wages as an example. The employer takes those amounts from each pay period and remits them as the law requires. Leaving the decision to the employee, holding the amounts or limiting the deductions to licensed staff does not meet the employer's obligation.
  216. The correct answer is D (Discuss the workload, adjust the schedule and offer the employee support.): The competency profile asks personnel to use time and organization skills, apply strategies that reduce the risk of injury and illness, and apply effective stress management skills. A workload discussion, a schedule adjustment and support address the cause of the change in behaviour. Ignoring it, transferring the employee without discussion or leading with a warning about the file does not resolve the workplace problem.
  217. The correct answer is B (Train the employee on the safe handling rules and the required protective equipment.): The competency profile asks an owner or manager to make health and safety requirements part of the work, naming WHMIS, safe disposal of hazardous waste and universal precautions. Training on safe handling and protective equipment gives the employee the practices the task requires. Handing over labels, asking the supplier to perform the work or storing the materials without instructions does not meet the employer's duty.
  218. The correct answer is C (The price list must always be up to date and must show its effective date.): Subsection 55 (6) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires the price list to be up to date at all times and to indicate its effective date. Two versions in circulation breach that rule because one of them is no longer accurate. Keeping both in use, replacing the list on a family request or following a competitor's prices does not meet the subsection.
  219. The correct answer is A (Provide a copy of the price list without charge to the person who asked.): Subsection 68 (1) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires every operator to provide a copy of the price list without charge to every person who requests it. The request alone creates the duty, so the receptionist should hand over the copy. Mailing it later, waiting for an arrangement booking or describing prices aloud does not satisfy the subsection.
  220. The correct answer is D (Update the website with the current price list and explain the difference to the family.): Subsection 68 (3) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires an operator who uses a website to promote or sell licensed supplies or services to make the current price list available, without charge, in a printable form in a clearly visible place on the site. An outdated page does not meet that rule and leaves families with wrong information. Removing all prices, keeping the old page or asking for a signed statement does not fix the practice.
  221. The correct answer is B (Six years from the date the replaced price list was last in effect.): Subsection 101 (2) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires an operator to retain a price list for six years from the date the price list was last in effect. The clock follows the end of the list's life, not the date it was printed or replaced. Shorter or longer periods chosen for convenience do not meet the subsection.
  222. The correct answer is D (Repay the $300 difference to the purchaser within 30 days of the overcharge.): Subsection 34 (1) of the Act prohibits a licensee from charging, collecting or receiving more than the price indicated on the price list, and subsection 34 (2) requires the difference to be repaid to the purchaser within 30 days. The $300 is the amount over the listed price. Applying it to a future account, holding the money or waiting for a written complaint does not satisfy subsection 34 (2).
  223. The correct answer is A (Produce a readable hard copy of the records when it is needed.): Subsection 97 (2) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires an operator that does not keep records in hard copy to make sure that a hard copy of the records can be readily produced in an easily understandable form. That rule keeps the records usable during an inspection or an investigation. Storing a second copy elsewhere, waiting a year to print the file or handing over a password does not meet the subsection.
  224. The correct answer is C (The advance money is trust money and cannot be used for the business's operating costs.): Money received in advance for licensed supplies or services is prepaid trust money under subsection 78 (1) of Ontario Regulation 30/11, and subsection 78 (3) requires the operator to keep it in the trust account or fund until the regulation permits a payout. The only early retention the regulation allows is a limited amount that may be elected on or after the 30th day after the contract was made, based on what the operator could keep if the purchaser cancelled. Using the money for operating costs is not a permitted payout, and a promise to repay it or a written note does not change that.
  225. The correct answer is D (Not later than the 35th day after the money is received.): Subsection 78 (2) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires an operator to deposit prepaid trust money into an individual trust account or a pooled trust fund not later than the 35th day after the day the operator receives it. The other periods in the options do not appear in the subsection. Where a contract provides for the supplies or services within 30 days, subsection 78 (4) sets the same 35th day limit for the deposit.
  226. The correct answer is B (Listen to the concern, review the file and correct any error that is found.): A family concern that is answered openly and corrected early is far less likely to become a regulatory complaint. Under section 66 of the Act, the registrar may receive complaints, make inquiries and attempt to mediate or resolve them, so the funeral home benefits from resolving the matter itself. Explaining how to complain, trading a discount for silence or refusing to revisit the arrangements does not correct the problem.
  227. The correct answer is C (Within 15 days after the change, in the form and manner the registrar specifies.): Subsection 103 (1) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 lists the changes an operator must report, and the list includes the person in charge of day to day operations at a location. Subsection 103 (2) requires the notice to be given within 15 days after the change occurs, in the form and manner specified by the registrar. Waiting for a renewal, an annual report or an inspection does not meet the deadline.
  228. The correct answer is A (Records must be kept so each business or location can be identified and compiled separately.): Subsection 97 (1) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires an operator to keep records in a manner that lets the information for each licensed business or location be readily identified and compiled separately. A single combined system that cannot separate the two locations fails that requirement, and an inspector who asks for one location's records cannot be answered properly. Destroying records early, storing them in the preparation room or seeking registrar approval before storage is not the requirement at issue.
  229. The correct answer is D (Written notice of the storage location must be given to the registrar.): Subsection 98 (1) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires an operator to keep records at the operator's principal place of business in Ontario. Subsection 98 (2) allows another location in Ontario if the operator gives the registrar written notice of where the records are kept. A duplicate copy, a landlord consent or an inspector's approval is not what the regulation requires.
  230. The correct answer is B (The certificate must be kept for six years from the date of the cremation.): Subsection 101 (5) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires a crematorium operator to retain the coroner's certificate authorizing a cremation for six years from the date of the cremation. The period runs from the cremation itself, not from the claim of the remains or a licence renewal. A two year period would be shorter than the subsection requires.
  231. The correct answer is A (They must be delivered to the nearest municipal archive or another public archive.): Subsection 102 (1) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires an operator that ceases to operate to arrange for records that had to be made available to the public to be delivered to the nearest municipal archive, an archive of a religious organization or another public archive. If no such archive accepts them, they go to the Archives of Ontario. Shredding them, sending them to the registrar or handing them to a family does not meet the subsection.
  232. The correct answer is C (They must be retained as long as the operator remains licensed for the business.): Subsection 101 (4) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires an operator to retain records that the Act requires it to make available to the public for as long as the operator continues to be licensed for the business to which the records relate. The general six year period in the other subsections does not shorten that duty. Age, storage space and the last public request do not change the rule.
  233. The correct answer is B (Notify the registrar of the arrangements, including the address where the records are kept.): Subsection 102 (1) requires an operator that ceases to operate to arrange for its public records to go to an archive and for its other records to be stored securely and made available for inspections or investigations. Subsection 102 (2) requires the operator to notify the registrar of the details of those arrangements, including the address where the records are located, as soon as the arrangements are made or changed. Waiting for an inspector, delaying a year or reporting only the number of boxes does not meet that duty.
  234. The correct answer is D (The operator is exempt from those obligations because the buyer is licensed to operate.): Subsection 102 (3) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 exempts an operator that ceases to operate from the record delivery and notice obligations if the operator is selling or transferring the business to another person who has become licensed to operate it. The exemption exists because a licensed buyer continues the business and keeps the records in use. Sending the records to an archive, keeping them for six years in the old building or requiring a personal licence class is not what the subsection provides.
  235. The correct answer is A (Obtain every permit and approval required by law and keep the evidence available.): Section 23 of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires that all permits and approvals required by law be obtained for each location of each of the operator's businesses, and that the permits and evidence of the approvals be kept available. The duty applies to a new location as soon as the business operates from it. Waiting for an inspection, posting numbers instead of keeping the documents or storing the evidence at another site does not meet the section.
  236. The correct answer is C (An operator must not operate from or invite contracts at an unlisted location.): Subsection 21 (1) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 prohibits an operator from operating from a location other than one identified on the licence, and subsection 21 (2) prohibits inviting the public to enter into contracts at another location. Subsection 21 (3) permits a contract to be entered into at a location requested by the purchaser, which is a narrow exception. Telling a municipality, attending with a licensed funeral director or setting a low contract value does not create a lawful business location.
  237. The correct answer is D (The operator must obtain the consent of the registrar for the arrangement.): Subsection 22 (3) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires an operator of a funeral establishment or transfer service to avoid placing one person in charge of the day to day operations of more than one business, or of more than one business location, unless the operator has the consent of the registrar. Subsection 22 (4) lists the factors the registrar may weigh, including geographic separation, business volume and compliance history. Sharing a municipality, hiring a second manager later or asking the staff to approve the plan does not replace the registrar's consent.
  238. The correct answer is B (Keep a schedule naming each task, its frequency, the person responsible and the record.): The competency profile asks an owner or manager to provide effective general maintenance for facilities and equipment, including cleaning and maintenance of vehicles and timely cleaning and maintenance of equipment. A schedule with a named task, frequency, responsible person and record shows that the work was planned and completed. Relying on memory, waiting for a family comment or recording the work only when an inspector asks does not create that evidence.
  239. The correct answer is C (The person must have completed the education required as a result of section 42.): Subsection 22 (1) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires an operator to have a person in charge of the day to day operations of each business location identified on the licence, and that person must have completed the education required as a result of section 42. The requirement is tied to the role, not to a period of service or a distance from the site. Years of service, a business licence held by someone else or a short commute does not satisfy that subsection.
  240. The correct answer is A (Personal and business liability coverage, plus third party liability coverage for claims.): The competency profile expects an owner or manager to evaluate and obtain appropriate insurance coverage, and it gives personal and business liability and third party liability as examples. Coverage is part of managing risk as the business changes. Staff health and dental plans, collision coverage alone or life policies sold to families are not the coverages the profile names.
  241. The correct answer is D (Explain the scope limit and arrange for a Class 1 licensee to embalm.): Embalming is the task that separates Funeral Director Class 1 from every other personal licence class. A Class 2 licensee may enter into contracts and arrange and direct funeral rites and ceremonies, but may not provide embalming services. A family request, a signature on a waiver or a note in the file cannot expand the scope the licence allows.
  242. The correct answer is B (Acknowledge the request, explain the limits of the role and refer to a counsellor.): The competency profile asks a licensee to assess the emotional needs of the people served while recognizing the limits of the scope of practice, and to make people aware of counselling or refer them to an appropriate health care professional when the need is beyond that scope. Acknowledging the request and referring it protects the family. Promising free counselling, providing sessions without a qualification or telling a grieving family to wait does not.
  243. The correct answer is A (Refuse to approve the advertisement because the statement would mislead the public.): Section 27 of the Act prohibits a licensee from making a false, misleading or deceptive statement in any advertisement, circular, pamphlet or similar material relating to the sale or provision of licensed supplies or services. A slogan that suggests an award the business never received is misleading, and approving it exposes the licensee. Smaller type, a claim that slogans are exempt or a request for the newspaper to check the award does not make the statement true.
  244. The correct answer is C (Decline to discuss the case and explain that the information stays confidential.): The competency profile asks a licensee to maintain confidentiality of information at all times. Details about a death and a family's account belong to the family and the business, not to a curious neighbour who happens to be a customer. Sharing part of the information, confirming a payment or relying on a neighbour's promise of privacy all breach that duty.
  245. The correct answer is B (A licence is a privilege that carries a duty to protect the people served.): The competency profile asks a licensee to accept professional responsibility to protect the dignity and interests of those served, and it describes professional accountability as relying on conscientious, self-disciplining and responsible members. A licence gives the holder the legal right to do work that others may not do, and that right comes with obligations to the public. It is not a private asset, a reward for passing an exam or a document that can be lent to someone else.
  246. The correct answer is D (Disclose the affiliation and the payment clearly to families before any recommendation.): The competency profile asks a licensee to disclose clearly, concisely and accurately information about affiliations with other organizations, commissions and other benefits as appropriate. A family deciding on a monument company is entitled to know that the person making the recommendation is paid for it. Stopping the recommendation while keeping the payments, keeping the arrangement private or disclosing only on request does not give the family what it needs to judge the advice.
  247. The correct answer is C (Decline to repeat the claim and describe the competing home fairly and accurately.): The competency profile asks a licensee to relate professionally and positively to other professionals and organizations connected to the funeral sector and to maintain a professional relationship with other funeral sector providers. Repeating or enlarging an untrue claim damages a colleague and misleads the audience. Agreeing with the colleague, adding a stronger story or leaving the claim uncorrected does not meet the professional standard.
  248. The correct answer is A (Prevent the coworker from driving and report the concern to the supervisor.): A licensee is expected to conduct themselves in a manner consistent with accepted standards of practice, and the competency profile asks personnel to apply strategies that reduce the risk of injury or illness to themselves and other team members. A coworker who may be impaired should not drive the funeral coach, and the concern belongs with a supervisor. Allowing the trip, driving silently in place of the coworker or asking a third employee to watch does not address the risk.
  249. The correct answer is D (The registrar may require the licensee to complete the educational requirements again.): Subsection 42 (7) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 allows the registrar to require an applicant for a licence or a licensee to complete again any of the educational requirements, or other specified requirements, if there has been a period during which the applicant did not carry on the activities authorized by the licence. That power keeps a returning licensee current with the work. Restricting sales, imposing unpaid work or adding an examination at every renewal is not what the subsection provides.
  250. The correct answer is B (Report the conduct through the appropriate channel and record what was observed.): The competency profile asks a licensee to recognize professional misconduct and respond appropriately, consistent with the reporting requirements that apply in the setting, and to act so that any adverse effects are reduced. Writing false entries in a preparation record is dishonest, and it corrupts a record that an inspector or a family may rely on. Keeping quiet, quietly correcting the entries alone or waiting to see whether it happens again does not meet that duty.

Practice Test 2 Answer Key

  1. The correct answer is D (Its effective date, which must be shown on the current version of the list.): Ontario Regulation 30/11 section 55(6) requires the price list to always be up to date and to indicate its effective date. A list without an effective date cannot show which prices are in force. The other details may be useful to the business, but they are not required on the price list.
  2. The correct answer is C (Every licensed supply and service that the operator generally provides to families.): Section 55(1) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires the price list to include every licensed supply and service that the operator generally provides. The list is built around what the business offers, not around sales figures or cost. A family is entitled to see the price of anything the operator normally sells.
  3. The correct answer is D (A copy must be given without charge to every person who requests one.): Section 68(1) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires every operator to provide a copy of the price list without charge to every person who requests it. The caller does not have to be a client or a prospective client. The rule exists so that a person can compare prices before deciding anything.
  4. The correct answer is A (Plain language, printed in legible type of at least 10 point.): Section 54 of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires the price list to be written in plain language and legibly printed in 10 point type or larger. Plain language matters because the list is read by grieving people, not by lawyers. Larger type alone does not fix wording that a family cannot follow.
  5. The correct answer is C (No licensee may charge more than the price listed for that service.): Section 34(1) of the Funeral, Burial and Cremation Services Act, 2002 prohibits a licensee from charging, collecting or receiving more than the price on the price list for a licensed supply or service. The day of the week does not change the rule. If the operator wants a weekend price, the price list itself must show that price.
  6. The correct answer is D (Within 30 days after the amount was charged or the money was received.): Section 34(2) of the Act requires a licensee who charges more than the listed price to repay the difference to the purchaser within 30 days. The clock is set by the overcharge, not by the family complaint. A refund policy that delays repayment beyond the 30 days breaks the Act.
  7. The correct answer is C (Not later than the 35th day after the operator receives the money.): Section 78(2) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires prepaid trust money to be deposited no later than the 35th day after the operator receives it. The deposit goes into an individual trust account or a pooled trust fund. Until that deposit is made, the money is still trust money and must be kept separate from business money.
  8. The correct answer is B (Trust money must be held separate and apart from every other kind of money.): Section 76(1) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires an operator to hold all trust money separate and apart from all money other than trust money. Temporary use is still use. Trust money exists for the beneficiary named in the contract, not for the cash needs of the business.
  9. The correct answer is A (Whether the supply is available for rent, and the rental terms offered.): Section 55(3) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires the price list to indicate whether a particular supply is available for rent. A family comparing a rental casket with a purchase needs that information on the list. The identity of past renters is private business and does not belong there.
  10. The correct answer is C (The name and address of the insurer and the policy number if known.): Section 124(1)(a) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires an insurance-funded contract to contain the name, address and telephone number of the insurer or issuer and the number of the insurance or annuity contract if it is known. This lets the operator and the family identify the funding policy later. The operator must also attach the policy and the designating or assigning document if they are available.
  11. The correct answer is B (Records of all money received or disbursed, with the details of each account.): Section 99 of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires an operator to keep records of all money received or disbursed, including details of each account with a depositary. The requirement covers the whole money flow, not only trust deposits or completed sales. Those records are what let an inspector follow a single transaction from the family to the bank.
  12. The correct answer is B (The operator must refund the excess to the person entitled under the contract.): Section 133 of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires an operator who receives insurance or annuity proceeds to refund any amount that exceeds what is payable for the supplies and services funded out of those proceeds. The money belongs to the family or the person named in the contract. Moving it to another account or another contract is not a permitted use.
  13. The correct answer is A (Provide input on the pricing for the owner to consider and approve.): The competency profile expects an owner or manager to develop or provide input to budgets, pricing and price lists, and a licensee supports that work. The licensee brings real information about costs and what local families ask for. The pricing decision and the price list remain the operator's responsibility, and the package must be on the list before it is sold.
  14. The correct answer is D (Do not collect the increase because the money was paid before the service.): Section 38 of the Act says that if money is paid under a contract in advance and the price of the supplies or services rises before they are provided, the operator shall not charge the purchaser any additional amount for the increase. The prepaid contract fixes the price for what it covers. A later price list applies to new contracts, not to money already paid.
  15. The correct answer is B (Give the price information and offer to send her the price list.): Section 68(2)(b) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires information about prices for licensed supplies and services to be provided to persons making inquiries in person, by telephone or in writing. Section 68(1) also requires a copy of the price list without charge to anyone who requests it. A caller comparing homes is exactly the person the rule is meant to protect.
  16. The correct answer is C (The current price list, showing its effective date, with the price quoted from it.): Section 55(6) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires the price list to always be up to date and to show its effective date. A written inquiry must be answered with price information under section 68(2)(b). Quoting an old saved version would mislead the family and could lead to a charge that does not match the list in force.
  17. The correct answer is C (The contract must show the total price and the discount allowed to the purchaser.): Section 121(3)10 of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires the contract to state the total price, including taxes and any payments the purchaser must reimburse, less any discount allowed to the purchaser. Charging less than the listed price is allowed because section 34 of the Act only prohibits charging more. Changing the price list to match one private discount would make the public list inaccurate.
  18. The correct answer is A (The payment schedule, the amount of each payment and the financing charges applied.): Section 121(3)11 and 12 of Ontario Regulation 30/11 require the contract to set out when payments are due, how each payment is calculated, the financing charges and the interest or other amounts charged on late or deficient payments. The purchaser also needs to know the rights and duties that apply if the contract is cancelled for non-payment. Those terms belong in the contract itself, not in an office file.
  19. The correct answer is D (Record the waived charge on the contract and leave the trust money untouched.): An operator may reduce or waive its own charge, and the contract should show the arrangement accurately. Trust money is held for the purchaser and can be paid out only as the licensed supplies and services are provided under the contract. Using one family's trust money to cover another contract, or marking a waived charge as paid, breaks the trust rules and the record.
  20. The correct answer is A (Reverse the payment in the records and tell the family about the balance.): Section 99(1) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires records of all money received or disbursed. A cheque that the bank returns has not produced money received, so the record must show the reversal and the account must show the balance owing. The family should be told about the problem privately and given a chance to pay.
  21. The correct answer is B (Record the deposit as unpaid and speak with the family privately about another method.): The transaction did not produce money, so the deposit record must show it as unpaid. Section 99 of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires accurate records of all money received or disbursed, and section 30 of the Act prohibits falsifying or misleading entries in a document relating to the sale of licensed supplies or services. The conversation about payment should be private and respectful.
  22. The correct answer is B (Take no repossession action without leave of the court, because two thirds is paid.): Section 129(1) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 prohibits an operator from taking action to repossess a licensed supply on a default in payment if at least two thirds of the purchase price has been paid, unless a judge of the Superior Court of Justice grants leave. The family has paid well past that point. The operator may pursue the unpaid balance through lawful means, but not by self-help repossession.
  23. The correct answer is B (Explain the less expensive options available and possible sources of financial assistance.): The competency profile expects a licensee to identify the purchaser's financial parameters in a respectful manner, explain payment policies clearly and discuss potential sources of financial assistance where appropriate. The licensee also presents the less expensive options that fit what the family can manage. Promising a full package, or promising to carry the whole cost, is not a decision the licensee can make on the operator's behalf.
  24. The correct answer is A (That the operator is named as beneficiary or the proceeds are assigned to it.): Section 124(2)3 of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires the document designating the operator as beneficiary or assigning the proceeds to be attached to the contract if it is available. Section 132(5) prohibits an operator from agreeing to be named as beneficiary or assignee unless a contract for the supplies or services has been entered into. Until the assignment or designation is confirmed, the licensee cannot treat the policy as money paid under the contract.
  25. The correct answer is C (Review the contract with the family and explain in detail what it provides.): The contract must set out all the supplies and services to be provided and the price of each one, and the operator is bound by what that contract says. If the operator substitutes a different supply or service, section 128 of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires the purchaser to be told about the substitution and its comparative value, and the price must not increase because of it. A statement made by an insurance agent who is not acting for the operator does not change the contract.
  26. The correct answer is A (Nothing, because no fee may be charged for advice about another contract.): Section 24(4) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 prohibits an operator from charging for providing advice about, or for arranging or negotiating, a contract for the purchase of licensed supplies or services from the operator or from another operator. Section 24(1) also prohibits a fee connected to a licensed supply or service that was not purchased from the operator. The only exception is a design service for a customized supply requested by the purchaser.
  27. The correct answer is D (Decline the cash, because a licensee may not accept payment for insurance in cash.): Section 132(3) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 prohibits a licensee from accepting any payment for an annuity, insurance contract or group insurance that is in cash or made payable to the licensee. Sharing the money or passing it to a family as a discount does not change the nature of the payment. Section 132(1) also restricts the use of the operator's name by a person soliciting insurance from a vulnerable person.
  28. The correct answer is C (Disclose the benefit and name the person who pays it in the contract.): Section 121(3)9 of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires the contract to set out the consideration and benefits, if any, that are due to the operator or another person because of recommendations made to the purchaser about supplies or services for which the purchaser will contract with a third party, and to name every person from whom that benefit is due. The competency profile also expects a licensee to disclose affiliations and benefits. An undisclosed rebate that shapes a recommendation is a conflict of interest.
  29. The correct answer is D (Give the family balanced information and disclose the arrangement in the contract.): Section 121(3)9 of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires the contract to disclose consideration and benefits due to the operator because of recommendations made to the purchaser, and to name the person who pays them. The competency profile also expects a licensee to disclose affiliations, commissions or other benefits as appropriate. The family is entitled to information that serves its own needs rather than a single retailer's arrangement with the home.
  30. The correct answer is A (Confirm the family's consent in writing before publishing the note and photograph.): The competency profile requires a licensee to maintain confidentiality of information at all times. A general thank you note does not cover a public website that would link a named family to a death and the services used. The licensee should confirm the exact use in writing, including the name and any photograph, before anything is published.
  31. The correct answer is B (An explanation of the conditions of sale and a full description of what is included.): Section 119(6) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires an operator who advertises a price to include an explanation of all the conditions of sale that relate to that price and a full description of the supplies and services included. Section 68(3) also requires the price list to be available without charge in a printable form in a clearly visible place on the website. A headline price without the conditions can mislead a family about what it will actually pay.
  32. The correct answer is B (Give general information about funerals and decline to confirm details about this family.): The competency profile expects a licensee to cooperate with the media and serve as a community resource about death and the funeral sector, and it also requires confidentiality of information at all times. General information about funeral practice may be given. Details about a particular family, including the funeral date and the choice of cremation, cannot be confirmed without proper authority.
  33. The correct answer is D (Refuse the transfer, record the request and report the matter as required.): Section 76(1) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires trust money to be held separate and apart from all other money, and section 78 requires it to stay in the trust account or fund until the regulation permits or requires a payout. Using trust money for operating costs takes money that belongs to purchasers. The competency profile also expects a licensee to recognize professional misconduct and respond appropriately, which includes documenting the request and reporting it as the situation requires.
  34. The correct answer is A (Arrange for the remains to be released and treat the balance as a debt.): Cremated remains are held for the person entitled to receive them under the contract and are not security for an unpaid account. The licensee should follow the contract and the direction of the person entitled to the remains, and should raise the owner's instruction with the operator. The outstanding balance is a debt that the business may pursue through lawful means, such as a civil claim.
  35. The correct answer is A (Explain the limit and refer the family to a qualified counselling service.): The competency profile expects a licensee to communicate the limits of the scope of practice and the services available, and to make a person aware of counselling or refer to an appropriate health care professional where the need is beyond that scope. A contract must set out the supplies and services that will actually be provided under it. Writing counselling into the contract would promise something the operator will not deliver.
  36. The correct answer is D (The instruction is wrong because tied selling is not permitted in these circumstances.): Section 38.1 of the Act prohibits an operator from requiring a purchaser to buy other supplies or services from the operator as a condition of buying certain licensed supplies or services, unless the operator does so in the prescribed circumstances. The permitted combinations in section 70 of Ontario Regulation 30/11 cover things such as staff services, vehicles and facilities, and they do not include caskets. Section 24(1) also prohibits a fee connected to a supply that the family did not buy from the operator.
  37. The correct answer is C (Remove the claim or correct it so the website is not misleading to families.): Section 27 of the Act prohibits a licensee from making a false, misleading or deceptive statement in advertising or material published relating to the sale or provision of licensed supplies or services. Section 28 allows the registrar to order the use of the material to stop and to require a retraction or correction of equal prominence. A small note does not fix a headline claim that is not true.
  38. The correct answer is C (Decline to discuss the family's arrangement and direct the neighbour elsewhere.): The competency profile requires a licensee to maintain confidentiality of information at all times. In a small community, a licensee will often meet people who know the family, and that familiarity does not create a right to information. The family decides what becomes public, and the licensee should say nothing about the arrangement.
  39. The correct answer is C (The casket must stay priced separately and cannot be buried in the staff charge.): Section 24(3) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 prohibits an operator from including in a charge for professional and staff services an amount for a supply or service that the regulation requires to be priced separately on the price list. Caskets are among the supplies that must appear with their own prices. Adding one standard casket to the staff charge would hide a price that the family is entitled to see.
  40. The correct answer is D (Repay the difference to the family within 30 days and correct the file.): Section 34(2) of the Act requires a licensee who charges more than the price on the price list to repay the difference to the purchaser within 30 days. The duty does not depend on the family noticing or complaining. Raising the price list after the fact would not cure the overcharge and would leave the public list inaccurate.
  41. The correct answer is D (Complying with the code of ethics is a condition of every licence issued.): Section 62 of the Act makes it a condition of every licence issued under the Act that the licensee comply with the code of ethics established by the Minister. The condition is built into the licence itself, so it applies from the first day and to every class. A complaint or a discipline hearing is not what brings the code into force.
  42. The correct answer is A (A fine of $25,000, which is the maximum the committee may impose.): Section 64(2)3 of the Act allows a discipline committee to impose a fine of up to $25,000 or a lesser amount that may be prescribed, payable by the licensee. The committee may also order a licensee to attend a specified educational program, require an operator to fund educational programs, or fix costs. The Act sets the ceiling, so the committee cannot impose the larger amounts named in the other choices.
  43. The correct answer is A (The licensee must carry the licence and produce it on request while conducting business.): Section 117(2) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires an individual licensed under the Act to carry the licence on his or her person whenever conducting business relating to the sale or provision of a licensed supply or service and to produce it on request. A business card is not a licence. Leaving the wallet card at the office is not consistent with the rule.
  44. The correct answer is B (Record the deposit as unpaid and explain the shortfall to the owner in writing.): Section 30 of the Act prohibits a licensee from falsifying, assisting in falsifying or inducing another person to falsify any information or document relating to the sale or provision of licensed supplies or services, and from making a misleading statement in such a document. Section 31 prohibits furnishing false or misleading information. A promise to pay is not a payment, and the record must show what actually happened.
  45. The correct answer is D (Explain that embalming is not available here and what the home may provide.): Section 34(3) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 provides that a Funeral Establishment Operator Class 2 shall not offer to sell, sell or provide a transfer service or any funeral services other than maintaining premises to be used for the temporary placement of human remains so that persons may attend and pay their respects. The limit belongs to the operator licence, so it does not disappear when a Class 1 licensee is available. The licensee explains the services that the home may lawfully provide.
  46. The correct answer is B (Have the post removed and explain the confidentiality rule to the employee.): The competency profile requires a licensee to maintain confidentiality of information at all times, and an employer is expected to train employees in their responsibilities and supervise them appropriately. A post that names a family and shows the tribute discloses private information to anyone who can see it. Making the account private later does not undo the disclosure that has already happened.
  47. The correct answer is C (Ask the family about their customs and arrange a referral if needed.): The competency profile expects a licensee to take part in ongoing professional development, to stay current in beliefs and practices relating to death in multicultural communities, and to ask questions rather than assume that one family's custom applies to another. Where the request goes beyond the licensee's knowledge, the family should be referred appropriately. Copying the last family's customs, or handing the questions to an untrained coworker, does not meet the standard.
  48. The correct answer is C (Decline the request, because the licence does not permit being present at a visitation.): A Transfer Service Sales Representative may not arrange, co-ordinate, conduct, participate in or be present at a visitation, funeral or other rite or ceremony at which human remains are present, except for scattering cremated remains, and section 37(3) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 sets that limit for a transfer service operator. Standing at the door during a visitation is presence at the rite. The representative should explain the limit and let a funeral director take that duty.
  49. The correct answer is B (It takes advantage of a grieving person and may be the subject of a complaint.): The competency profile expects a licensee to accept professional responsibility to protect the dignity and interests of the people served and to conduct arrangements in an ethical, supportive and sensitive manner. The licensee is also expected to identify the purchaser's financial parameters in a respectful way. Repeated pressure that plays on a widow's grief is professional misconduct, and the registrar may receive a complaint and refer the matter to a discipline committee.
  50. The correct answer is A (Report the conduct to the operator and the regulator as the situation requires.): The competency profile expects a licensee to recognize professional misconduct and respond appropriately, consistent with reporting requirements and accepted practice, so that any harm is addressed. Section 62 of the Act makes compliance with the code of ethics a condition of every licence, and section 66 allows the registrar to receive complaints and refer a matter to a discipline committee. Quiet correction between colleagues does not address the families who were misled.
  51. The correct answer is A (The date, the time and the name of the person who gave permission.): Permission may be oral or written, but the record should show the date, the time and the name of the person who gave it. The address, physician and casket details belong to other parts of the file. Recording the permission protects the licensee and the family when questions arise later.
  52. The correct answer is C (At the point of transfer and again at the point of receipt.): The competency profile requires identification to be verified at the point of transfer and at receipt. Checking only at one end leaves room for a mix-up in between. The second check confirms the body received is the body released.
  53. The correct answer is B (Removing personal effects, placing the body in a casket and transporting the body.): Section 37(2) allows a Transfer Service Operator Class 2 employee to have contact with the body only to remove personal effects, place the body in a casket and transport it. Washing, setting the features and applying disinfectant externally are within the Transfer Service Operator Class 1 scope, not this class. Arterial embalming and restoration are outside both transfer service classes.
  54. The correct answer is D (Washing, applying disinfectant externally, setting the features and casketing.): Section 36(4) permits a Transfer Service Operator Class 1 to place a body in a casket, wash it, apply disinfectant externally and set the features. Arterial embalming and restoring are excluded from that licence class. Cremation and interment are different licensed activities, and a coroner's certificate is issued by the coroner.
  55. The correct answer is B (May offer embalming services only while acting for a Class 1 establishment operator.): Section 40 permits a Funeral Director Class 2 to offer or sell embalming services only when acting on behalf of a Funeral Establishment Operator Class 1, and it does not permit the director to provide those services. The Class 2 licence covers non-embalming care such as washing, disinfecting externally and casketing. Supervision, in-house training and family consent do not widen the scope of the licence.
  56. The correct answer is A (Transport is limited to 72 hours after death or removal from refrigeration.): Section 35(4) prohibits a transfer service operator from transporting a body that has not been arterially embalmed later than 72 hours after death or removal from refrigeration. The 24, 48 and 96 hour figures do not appear in that condition. Refrigeration pauses the clock until the body is removed from it.
  57. The correct answer is D (The interior must not be visible from any public area when doors are closed.): Section 47 requires the interior of a holding room to be hidden from any public area while the doors are closed. Locking, door material and the direction the door opens are not part of that paragraph. Privacy from public view protects the dignity of the deceased and the family.
  58. The correct answer is C (Mechanical ventilation that leads directly to the outside of the building.): Section 47 requires mechanical ventilation that discharges directly to the outside of the building. A fan that recirculates air, a window to a hallway and a shared unit do not meet that requirement. Direct outside ventilation reduces odour and airborne contamination in the room.
  59. The correct answer is A (It must carry enough personal protective equipment for each person handling the body.): Section 51 requires a sufficient supply of personal protective equipment and clothing for every person in the vehicle who handles the body. The other items are not required by that section. Planning for each handler prevents staff from working without barriers when a case changes.
  60. The correct answer is B (Disinfect it in a timely way and before it is used for another body.): The competency profile requires stretchers, equipment and facilities to be disinfected in a timely manner and before use on another body. A rinse, a dry wipe or a delayed pad change does not disinfect the equipment. Disinfecting between cases protects the next deceased person and the staff who handle the stretcher.
  61. The correct answer is D (A Funeral Director Class 1 acting on behalf of a Class 1 operator.): Section 52 permits a Funeral Director Class 1 acting on behalf of a Funeral Establishment Operator Class 1 or a Transfer Service Operator Class 1, or a legally qualified medical practitioner, to remove a pacemaker or radioactive implant. A Funeral Director Class 2 does not qualify under that section. In-house training or peer direction does not replace the licence the section names.
  62. The correct answer is C (Sell or offer to sell the funeral services the operator is authorized to sell.): Section 5(3) permits a sales representative of a Transfer Service Operator Class 1 to sell or offer to sell the funeral services that the operator is authorized to sell. The representative licence is about selling, not about embalming, managing the business or removing implants. Those tasks belong to other licence classes and conditions.
  63. The correct answer is B (A metal identification tag with a personal identifier and the operator's name.): Section 186(3) requires a metal identification tag to accompany the body and to be placed in the container with the cremated remains until they are released. Section 186(4) requires the tag to hold a personal identifier and the crematorium operator's name. A receipt, photograph or contract copy does not satisfy that identification rule.
  64. The correct answer is A (Obtain a certificate of inspection issued within 180 days before use.): Section 49 requires the operator to give the registrar plans or drawings, obtain a certificate of inspection from a medical officer of health, public health inspector or designated person, and provide the certificate to the registrar. The inspection must occur within 180 days before the room is used. Fire department notice, newspaper notice and a blessing are not part of that section.
  65. The correct answer is C (Use it only as a holding room for dead human bodies.): Section 47 states that a holding room shall be used only as a holding room unless it is also an embalming room. Using the space as an office, storage area or lunchroom would breach that condition. Keeping the room for its licensed purpose also protects the dignity and security of the bodies held there.
  66. The correct answer is D (The address, the location of the body, the contact person and any hazards.): The short call before leaving should confirm the address, the location of the body within the building, the contact person, whether a coroner or police are involved, and any infection control or safety concerns. Pricing, casket choice and pallbearer details belong to the arrangement meeting. Good information prevents the team from arriving without the right equipment or staff.
  67. The correct answer is B (Call the funeral home to confirm the new details before acting.): The chapter directs the team to call before acting when the situation on arrival differs from the description. The funeral home can confirm the police role, send the right equipment and adjust the plan. Moving the body without that check risks a legal or safety error.
  68. The correct answer is D (Wait until the coroner's certificate authorizing the cremation is received.): Section 31(2)(a) prohibits a crematorium operator from permitting a cremation unless the operator has received a certificate issued by the coroner authorizing the cremation. A signed contract or a family request does not replace that certificate. The operator waits for the document before starting.
  69. The correct answer is A (Confirm the morgue's release requirements and who will sign out the body.): A hospital or long term care home has its own release process that must be followed before a body leaves the building. The director confirms who releases the body, what documentation is required and when the release can happen. Sending the team first or asking the family to collect the body bypasses that process.
  70. The correct answer is C (Stop and resolve the difference with the facility before moving anything.): Identification must be verified at the point of transfer, and a difference between the band and the file stops the transfer until it is resolved. Accepting an explanation, photographing the band for later or changing the band bypasses that check. An identification error can lead to the wrong body being released or prepared.
  71. The correct answer is A (List, label and store the items so they cannot be mixed with another case.): The competency profile requires the licensee to secure, label and record personal effects as part of the transfer. Listing and storing the items so they cannot be mixed with another case meets that standard. Handing the items back, pocketing them or leaving them in an unrecorded bag creates a risk of loss and dispute.
  72. The correct answer is B (Call for a second staff member and plan the route before lifting.): The chapter directs the licensee to ask for help when the load, the space or the stairs make a solo lift unsafe. Calling a second staff member and planning the route protects both the worker and the deceased. A quick lift, a drag or an untrained family helper increases the risk of a fall or injury.
  73. The correct answer is C (Keep the back straight, bend at the knees and keep the load close.): Safe handling means keeping the back straight, bending at the knees and keeping the load close to the body while avoiding a twist. The other options describe the movements that cause back injury. Correct technique protects the worker and keeps the body under control.
  74. The correct answer is D (Agree on clear commands and say them before every lift.): The chapter directs partners to communicate before every lift with clear words such as ready, lift and step. Agreeing in advance and speaking each command keeps both workers moving together. Silent cues or letting one person decide on the spot can cause a sudden shift in the load.
  75. The correct answer is C (A rushed lift can injure the worker and harm the dignity of the deceased.): The chapter states that a fall injures the worker and harms the dignity of the deceased, so the team resists the pressure to rush. A family may be present, and the director explains what will happen while keeping them at a safe distance. Slowing down does not mean stopping the work.
  76. The correct answer is B (Bring a bariatric cot and assign two or three staff members.): A body larger than the equipment rating calls for a bariatric cot and more staff. Using a standard cot, joining two cots or asking the family to help puts everyone at risk. The chapter treats the bariatric case as one that needs extra planning before the team leaves.
  77. The correct answer is A (Confirm that the cot is locked before transferring any weight.): The chapter directs the licensee to check that equipment is locked before transferring weight onto it. Sliding the body first or testing the cot with a colleague risks a collapse. Using the floor instead of a locked cot creates a separate lifting hazard.
  78. The correct answer is D (Report the injury so the workplace can fix the cause.): The chapter directs licensees to report any injury or near miss so the workplace can fix the cause. Waiting, keeping a private note or telling only a co-worker leaves the hazard in place. Reporting also protects the licensee if the injury affects later shifts.
  79. The correct answer is B (The disinfectant must be used at the specified concentration and contact time.): The chapter states that the disinfectant must be used at the concentration and contact time the manufacturer specifies. A quick wipe with the wrong product gives false confidence. Using a stronger mix also does not replace the required contact time.
  80. The correct answer is C (The vehicle must keep the body from public view during transport.): Section 51 states that the vehicle must be capable of transporting the body in a respectful and dignified manner and of keeping the body from public view. Signage, a partition and route choice are not part of that requirement. Keeping the body from view protects the family and the public.
  81. The correct answer is A (Equipment to clean, sanitize and disinfect bodies, but not to arterially embalm them.): Section 47 requires a holding room to contain the materials and equipment needed to prepare bodies, including cleaning, sanitizing and disinfecting them, but not equipment for arterial embalming or restoring. Embalming equipment belongs in an embalming room under section 48. Cremation equipment and autopsy facilities are outside the holding room standards.
  82. The correct answer is D (It must be on premises the Class 1 licence identifies as business premises.): Section 48 requires an embalming room to be located in premises that a Funeral Establishment Operator Class 1 licence identifies as business premises of the funeral establishment. Distance from the holding room, public access and municipal boundaries are not the test. The registrar must be able to connect the room to the licensed premises.
  83. The correct answer is D (The condition of the container and any hazards it may present.): The chapter states that a body received from elsewhere must be checked on arrival for identification, container condition and any hazards. Identification is already confirmed, so the remaining check covers the container and any hazard it presents. Colour, airline, weight, cost, brand and warranty do not bear on the safety of the body or the staff.
  84. The correct answer is B (The required documentation and a container suitable for transport.): The chapter states that a body being shipped out of the province or country needs documentation and a suitable container. It does not require a notarized family letter, a police escort or a metal container. The director confirms the rules of the destination jurisdiction before the body leaves.
  85. The correct answer is C (The deceased, the time, the place and the receiving person.): The chapter states that the written confirmation names the deceased, the date and time, the place of transfer, the receiving location and the person who will receive the body. Casket, funeral date and vehicle details are not part of that confirmation. Written terms prevent a body from becoming unaccounted for.
  86. The correct answer is A (The body may go to a medical school instead of a funeral home.): The chapter states that a body donated for anatomical study may go to a medical school rather than to a funeral home. The chapter does not require embalming, cremation or a court order for that transfer. The director confirms the school's own acceptance and documentation requirements.
  87. The correct answer is D (From the place removal was first requested to a cemetery, crematorium or holding room.): Section 37(4) permits a Transfer Service Operator Class 2 to transport a body only from the place where removal was first requested directly to a cemetery, crematorium or holding room and then to a cemetery or crematorium. A family home viewing or a second funeral home is outside that route. A Transfer Service Operator Class 2 does not arrange visitations or ceremonies.
  88. The correct answer is A (Retain them until they are claimed or interred in a cemetery.): Section 53(1) requires an operator holding unclaimed cremated remains to retain them until they are claimed or interred in a cemetery, whichever happens first. After one year, and after reasonable efforts to contact the family, the operator may have the remains interred in a cemetery. Scattering, police storage and general storage are not options under that section.
  89. The correct answer is B (The name of the person who performed each identification check.): The chapter directs the licensee to record who performed each identification check. That entry allows the funeral home to answer later questions about the body's identity and custody. Weather, the requesting family member and the distance driven do not document the identification step.
  90. The correct answer is C (The times, places and names of the people who released and received the body.): A clear chain of custody records the times, places and names of the people who released and received the body at each stage. Prices, fuel receipts and family preferences do not show who controlled the body. The record allows the funeral home to answer a question about the body's location at any point.
  91. The correct answer is A (Thank the son, explain the safety steps and keep him at a safe distance.): The chapter directs the licensee to explain what will happen, keep the family at a safe distance and allow a moment of prayer or silence if they wish. Accepting untrained help on a narrow stairway risks a fall, while sending the son away ignores his need to stay close. The best response protects both safety and the family's grief.
  92. The correct answer is D (Cover the body and the window before completing the paperwork.): The chapter says that when a body is left in view, the answer covers it.
