Section 31(3) lets a crematorium operator arrange the removal through a person described in section 52. Class 2 directors, preplanners and drivers are not on that list.
  12. The correct answer is D (The heat can burst the battery and damage the retort or workers): Section 31(2)(b) prohibits a crematorium operator from cremating a body that has a pacemaker or a radioactive implant, and section 27 prohibits a licensee from presenting such a body for cremation. The rule exists because the sealed battery in the device can rupture under the heat of the retort, damaging equipment and putting workers at risk. Removing the device first lets the cremation go ahead safely.
  13. The correct answer is C (Yes, because more than two years have passed since the implant): Section 31(2.1) says the prohibition on cremating a body with a radioactive implant does not apply if at least two years have passed since the body received the implant, or if a lesser time has passed and it is safe to cremate the body. Three years have passed, so the implant does not block the cremation. The coroner's certificate is a separate requirement that is already met.
  14. The correct answer is B (Product labels, safety data sheets and documented worker training): WHMIS gives workers information about hazardous products through the label on the container, the safety data sheet and training. The funeral home keeps the data sheets current and accessible and trains every person who works with the products. Storing products in their original labelled containers supports the same system.
  15. The correct answer is D (A smooth, non-absorbent finish that can be washed and disinfected often): Sections 47 and 48 require the room, all of its surfaces and the equipment used in it to be capable of being easily cleaned, sanitized and disinfected, and section 50 requires the room to be kept in that condition. A smooth, non-absorbent finish can be washed and then disinfected, while a textured or absorbent finish traps soil and moisture. Carpet in a preparation area cannot be disinfected reliably.
  16. The correct answer is A (Washing both hands with soap and running water before continuing the work): Soap and water are used when the hands are visibly soiled or after contact with a body, and an alcohol-based hand rub is used when the hands are not visibly soiled. A rub cannot lift blood and tissue from the skin, and rinsing alone does not clean the hands. Gloves are never washed and reused in place of hand hygiene.
  17. The correct answer is B (Remove the soiled gloves, clean both hands and then leave the room): Contaminated gloves must not touch a telephone, a door handle or a face, and they come off before the licensee leaves the contaminated area. Hand hygiene follows immediately after removal. Leaving gloves on, pulling them partway down or wiping them on the gown keeps the contamination on the hands and spreads it to the next surface touched.
  18. The correct answer is C (The sealed goggles, because the eyes need protection from an angled splash): Eye protection should cover the sides of the eyes, because a splash can enter from an angle rather than straight ahead. A cloth mask is not eye protection, and a shield worn up on the forehead is not in place when the splash happens. The goggles that fit over prescription glasses give the coverage this task needs.
  19. The correct answer is A (The licensee must be fit tested and trained before wearing that respirator): A simple mask reduces droplet exposure but does not protect the wearer from fine particles or vapours, so a respirator is used where a risk assessment calls for one. The wearer must be fit tested and trained, because a respirator that does not seal gives false protection. Suspected tuberculosis is handled with airborne precautions in addition to the usual gloves, gown and eye protection.
  20. The correct answer is B (A fit-tested respirator worn with the usual gloves, gown and eye protection): Airborne precautions are used for organisms carried in small particles that stay suspended in the air and travel beyond close contact. The key addition is a fit-tested respirator, worn with the gloves, gown and eye protection that routine practice already requires. A plain mask, extra gloves or an apron do not filter those fine particles.
  21. The correct answer is D (Influenza, which travels in large droplets during close contact): Droplet transmission happens when a cough, a splash or a procedure creates droplets that reach the eyes, nose or mouth of a person nearby, and influenza spreads that way. Tuberculosis travels in fine particles that stay suspended in the air and calls for airborne precautions. Hepatitis B is bloodborne, and norovirus spreads through contaminated food and water.
  22. The correct answer is C (Leave the pouch sealed until the body is inside the preparation room): A sealed pouch is a barrier that keeps contamination contained, so it stays closed until the body is in a controlled area where the preparation is planned under additional precautions. A used pouch is contaminated waste and is never reused for another body. Opening or cutting the pouch in an open area spreads contamination to the doorway and the transfer route.
  23. The correct answer is D (Replace the full sharps container with an empty one before discarding the needle): A sharps container is replaced before it is overfilled, and nothing is ever pushed down inside it by hand. Sharps go into a rigid, puncture-resistant container and never into ordinary garbage, where they can injure anyone who handles the bag. The lid is closed and the full container goes out through the proper waste stream.
  24. The correct answer is A (Remove the glove, let the wound bleed freely, then wash with soap): The glove comes off, the wound is allowed to bleed freely for a moment and then washed with soap and water, and a waterproof dressing is applied. Bleach and hard squeezing are not part of the first aid for a cut. The licensee reports the injury at once and seeks medical assessment, because the risk from a bloodborne infection is time sensitive.
  25. The correct answer is C (Report the exposure at once and arrange medical assessment the same day): A puncture injury from a contaminated sharp is reported immediately, and the exposed worker is assessed by a medical professional the same day, because the decision about post-exposure care is time sensitive. Waiting for symptoms, or finishing the case first, delays that assessment. The family is not the right source for the deceased person's infection status.
  26. The correct answer is B (Leave the product wet on the table for the full labelled time): Equipment and surfaces are cleaned and disinfected using the manufacturer's dilution and contact time, which means the product stays wet on the surface for the full labelled period. A quick wipe kills fewer organisms and gives a false sense of safety. A stronger mix is not a substitute for the contact time the label requires.
  27. The correct answer is D (The wheels, the handles, the straps, the buckles and the drain bottles): The parts of equipment that are easy to miss are the wheels, handles, straps, buckles, drain bottles, hoses and the undersides of tables, and those are the surfaces that carry contamination from one body to the next. Equipment used in a contaminated area is cleaned and disinfected after use and before it is used for another body. Walls and storage furniture follow the room cleaning schedule rather than being cleaned between every case.
  28. The correct answer is C (In their original labelled containers in a secure, ventilated area): Chemicals are stored in their original labelled containers, in a secure and ventilated area away from food and personal items. The safety data sheets are kept current and accessible, and every person who works with the products is trained. Moving chemicals into unmarked bottles breaks the label part of the WHMIS system.
  29. The correct answer is A (Follow the label and never combine products it does not name): Products are never mixed unless the label tells the user to mix them, because some combinations release toxic gas. The correct dilution and the contact time come from the label and the safety data sheet. A small test batch or a mask does not make an unknown mixture safe.
  30. The correct answer is B (Flush the eye with clean water right away and get medical help): A chemical splash in the eye is flushed with water at once, and medical help is arranged, because the damage grows with the time the chemical spends on the eye. The safety data sheet is read afterwards for the first aid detail and the follow up. Closing the eye, covering it dry or continuing the task delays the flush.
  31. The correct answer is D (Remove the contaminated clothing and wash the skin with water): When a chemical contacts the skin, the contaminated clothing comes off and the area is washed with water, and the safety data sheet is checked for further first aid. Wet clothing holds the product against the skin and lets the exposure continue. Finishing the injection, blotting the cloth or drinking water does not stop the chemical from acting.
  32. The correct answer is A (Move the licensee into fresh air and call for medical help): A person overcome by chemical fumes is moved into fresh air and help is called, and the room is then checked before anyone works in it again. Water, rest and a restart of the fan do not remove the exposure. If the exposure was serious, the worker is assessed by a medical professional.
  33. The correct answer is C (Use the spill kit to contain and clean the product safely): A chemical spill is handled with the spill kit, which contains the absorbent material, the protective equipment and the bags needed to contain the product for proper disposal. Staff must know where the kit is kept and how to use it before a spill happens. Rinsing the product into the environment or leaving it to dry spreads the hazard and is not an approved method of disposal.
  34. The correct answer is B (The dressings, gloves, gown and drapes, because all of them are contaminated): Pathological waste includes tissue, organs, blood-soaked materials and other items that may carry infectious organisms, and contaminated waste does not go into ordinary garbage. Gloves, gowns and disposable drapes used in the preparation are contaminated and follow the same waste stream. Reusable linen is handled as contaminated laundry rather than placed in the general bin.
  35. The correct answer is D (A record of the disposal that shows what was disposed of and how): A record of the disposal allows the funeral home to show what happened to pathological waste, including which waste went out and by what method. Contaminated waste must not go into ordinary garbage, and used chemicals follow the safety data sheet and local rules. Family consent and room temperature do not document the disposal of the waste.
  36. The correct answer is A (It may be used only for holding unless it is also an embalming room): Section 47 states that a holding room is used only as a holding room unless it is also an embalming room, and section 48 says the same for an embalming room. The equipment list for a holding room does not include arterial embalming or restoration, so an injection cannot be moved there for convenience. A room that is used outside its permitted purpose puts the operator's licence conditions at risk.
  37. The correct answer is C (Give the registrar plans or drawings that show enough detail of the room): Section 49 sets the order. The operator first gives the registrar plans or drawings, with any other information needed, so that the registrar can assess whether the room complies with section 47 or 48. The operator then obtains a certificate of inspection, and then provides that certificate to the registrar. A municipal building permit does not replace any of those steps.
  38. The correct answer is B (It is too old, because it must be issued within 180 days before use): Section 49 requires the certificate of inspection to state that the room was inspected within 180 days before the room is used and that it is suitable for the purpose. A certificate issued nine months before the planned first use is outside that window, so the room cannot be used on the strength of it. A fresh inspection and certificate must be obtained and provided to the registrar.
  39. The correct answer is C (A supply of protective equipment for each person who handles the body): Section 51 requires the vehicle to transport the body respectfully and out of public view, to contain a sufficient supply of personal protective equipment and clothing for each person in the vehicle who handles the body, and to be in a clean and sanitary condition. A stretcher, a fire extinguisher and a locked compartment are not items the section requires. Protective equipment belongs in the vehicle so that it is available at the scene.
  40. The correct answer is D (The eyewash station, the safety shower, the spill kit and the first aid box): Staff are shown where the eyewash station, the safety shower, the spill kit and the first aid kit are kept, and they are shown how to use them. A chemical splash in the eye is flushed with water at once, and a spill is contained with the kit before the product reaches a drain. Knowing the route to that equipment before an incident is part of preparation room safety.
  41. The correct answer is A (Proceed with the preparation and viewing using universal and additional precautions): Universal precautions apply to every body, and a case with a known infection adds the precautions that match the route of spread, which for blood and body fluids means strict contact precautions. An open casket viewing can usually go ahead when the preparation is planned properly and the equipment and surfaces are disinfected afterwards. An infectious disease does not by itself require refusing the service, closing the casket or waiting for a letter from the family.
  42. The correct answer is B (Report the injury and seek medical advice without naming any possible virus): A death record that does not mention an infection does not prove that the body was free of one, which is why universal precautions are used for every body. The exposed licensee reports the injury and is assessed by a medical professional, who decides what testing or treatment is appropriate, and that process protects the confidentiality of the deceased person. Telling the family a guess, or asking them for medical details, is not supported by the record.
  43. The correct answer is D (Wait for the repair, or use another room that is an approved embalming room): Sections 47 and 48 require mechanical ventilation directly to the outside of the building, and an embalming room cannot be used for arterial embalming until that ventilation is working again. Formaldehyde vapour irritates the eyes, nose and throat and is a serious long term hazard, and a mask does not remove vapour from the air. Moving the work into a holding room is not allowed, because arterial embalming is not part of that room's permitted use.
  44. The correct answer is B (Cover the cut with a waterproof dressing before putting on gloves): Broken skin is covered with a waterproof dressing before gloves go on, because a cut is a portal of entry for organisms from the body or from contaminated equipment. Double gloving does not seal the wound, and hand cream does not close it. A licensee with a covered cut can work as long as the dressing stays intact and is replaced when it becomes soiled.
  45. The correct answer is C (Clean the dried soil from the whole table first, then disinfect the surface): Cleaning removes the visible soil that would shield organisms and use up the disinfectant, and disinfection comes after cleaning, at the labelled dilution and for the labelled contact time. Wiping a soiled surface twice with the same cloth moves the contamination around. A stronger mix and a note in the record do not clean or disinfect the table.
  46. The correct answer is D (Contain the spill and ventilate the room before doing anything else): A chemical spill is handled with the spill kit, and the room is ventilated before the work continues, because the vapour is the immediate hazard to everyone present. The waste is disposed of as the safety data sheet and local rules require rather than rinsed into a drain or placed in ordinary garbage. The body and the schedule wait until the spill is contained.
  47. The correct answer is A (The licence must be amended to identify the annex as business premises): Section 48(1) says an embalming room must be located in premises that the Funeral Establishment Operator Class 1 licence indicates are business premises of the funeral establishment. The room was also constructed or altered recently, so section 49 adds the requirement to give the registrar plans or drawings, obtain a certificate of inspection issued within 180 days before use, and provide that certificate to the registrar. Municipal zoning and neighbour notice are separate matters.
  48. The correct answer is C (Record the near miss, review the process and change the blade routine): Every injury and near miss is reported, and a pattern that repeats is a signal that the process itself needs to change. The team reviews the incident, trains on the fix and keeps the record, which protects the worker and shows that the risk was addressed. A reminder to be careful and a new counter position do not remove the cause.
  49. The correct answer is B (The remains must be dealt with in accordance with that Act): Section 162(17) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 provides that where a medical officer of health determines that remains are those of a person who died of a communicable disease within the meaning of the Health Protection and Promotion Act, the remains shall be dealt with in accordance with that Act. The funeral home follows the public health direction that applies to the case rather than its usual routine. Cremation timing, embalming and the burial permit are not the rule this provision states.
  50. The correct answer is A (Hold the release, arrange an eligible removal and reschedule the cremation): Section 27 prohibits a licensee from presenting a body for cremation that could not be cremated by reason of section 31(2)(b), and section 52 limits who may remove a pacemaker to a Funeral Director Class 1 acting for a Class 1 operator or a legally qualified medical practitioner. The cremation waits until an eligible person removes the device. A family signature cannot make the cremation lawful, and a licensee who is not on the section 52 list cannot do the removal.
  51. The correct answer is B (Religious rites relating to the washing of a body are not embalming.): The definition in the Funeral, Burial and Cremation Services Act, 2002 covers preserving and disinfecting all or part of a dead human body by any means other than refrigeration, and it excludes religious rites relating to the washing of a body. The family's traditional washing is therefore not the practice of embalming that the Act licences. No registrar approval is needed for it, and it does not depend on an arterial injection.
  52. The correct answer is D (In the intestines, and then it spreads through the body.): Decomposition is the breakdown of tissues by the body's own enzymes and by bacteria, and it begins in the intestines before it spreads. Gas, odour, colour change, skin slippage and tissue breakdown are signs that a case is advancing. The lungs, the brain and the liver are not the starting point that the case analysis material describes.
  53. The correct answer is A (The solution strength, the distribution, the condition and the storage.): Preservation is not permanent, and how long it lasts depends on the strength of the solution, the completeness of distribution, the condition of the body and the conditions of storage. Embalming slows decomposition rather than stopping it, which is why a longer viewing asks more of the plan. Casket cost, licence class, room size and the arrangement conference have no effect on how long the appearance holds.
  54. The correct answer is C (Jewellery can trap contamination and long nails can harbour organisms.): The infection control material directs staff to remove jewellery that could trap contamination and to keep fingernails short and free of chipped polish that can harbour organisms. Gloves are worn as well, but they do not replace those habits. Turning the practice into a matter of appearance or a daily supervisor check misses the point, which is reducing what the hands can carry into a case.
  55. The correct answer is B (Agent, reservoir, portal of exit, transmission, portal of entry, host.): The chain of infection runs from the infectious agent through its reservoir, a portal of exit, a mode of transmission, a portal of entry and finally a susceptible host. Breaking any one of those links stops the spread, which is the aim of hand hygiene, barriers and disinfection. The other lists either change the order or name modes of transmission as if they were links in the chain.
  56. The correct answer is A (Contact and droplet, through hands, equipment and splashes.): Contact transmission happens when contaminated hands, gloves, equipment or surfaces touch broken skin or mucous membranes. Droplet transmission happens when a cough, a splash or a procedure sends droplets to the eyes, nose or mouth. Airborne, vector and vehicle routes exist, but they are not the modes that funeral service staff meet most often.
  57. The correct answer is D (Pressure does not replace a steady rate of flow through the vessels.): Pressure pushes the solution into the arterial system while the rate of flow controls how quickly it moves. High pressure with a slow rate does not improve distribution, and it can damage fragile vessels. The licensee should look at the drainage, the position of the tube and the rate of flow instead of raising the pressure setting.
  58. The correct answer is C (Store the equipment where it is easy to reach and keep it stocked.): Protective equipment is useful only when it is the right equipment, worn correctly and changed when it becomes contaminated. Keeping it within reach, stocking the sizes and replacing damaged or expired items removes the reason for going without it. Locking it away, keeping one box or waiting for an annual review leaves staff to improvise during a case.
  59. The correct answer is A (Treat a tray as contaminated once it has touched the body.): Concurrent disinfection means disinfecting as the work proceeds instead of leaving everything until the end. Trays, tables, hoses, drain bottles and equipment are treated as contaminated after contact with a body, solutions are changed when they become soiled and gloves are changed between tasks. Saving the room for one final cleaning, wiping with a dry cloth or soaking used instruments allows contamination to spread during the case.
  60. The correct answer is B (It slows decomposition, disinfects the body and restores a natural look.): Embalming temporarily preserves the body so family and friends can gather, sanitises it to reduce the risk of transmitting organisms, and restores a natural appearance that helps the bereaved recognise the person they lost. It also allows time for transport or for a service to be delayed. It does not stop decomposition for years, remove the need for care of the body or take the place of identification.
  61. The correct answer is D (A holding room that meets the requirements of section 47.): Section 33 (2) requires a Funeral Establishment Operator Class 1 to make sure there is a holding room on the funeral establishment premises that meets the requirements of section 47. That duty applies whether or not the operator offers embalming. A crematorium, a cemetery or a chapel of a particular size is not part of this licence condition.
  62. The correct answer is C (Inspect the casket, adjust the bedding, place the body, add the photograph.): The casketing work starts by obtaining and inspecting the selected casket or container, centring it on the carriage or bier and adjusting the interior bedding before the body is placed. The body is then positioned and personal effects are added as directed by the legal representative. Dressing and feature setting belong to an earlier stage, and the casket is centred before the body goes in.
  63. The correct answer is A (The instructions received, the products used and who performed the work.): The preparation report should show the instructions received, the preparation performed, the products used, any tissue damage or unusual condition, the personal effects handled and the person who performed the work. It is a legal record, so it is written when the work is done rather than from memory later. Contact details, prices and visitor lists belong in other parts of the file.
  64. The correct answer is D (The cot, straps, slide sheet, body cover, gloves and disinfectant.): The pre-shift check covers the cot, the straps, the slide sheet, the body bag or cover, personal protective equipment, a sharps container, the disinfectant and the condition of the vehicle itself. Having a complete set for each team, or a clear process for returning and disinfecting one set between calls, keeps the removals running. Casket stock, office documents and cemetery tools belong to other parts of the business.
  65. The correct answer is B (Match the identification tag on the body to the case record.): The competency profile requires the licensee to verify the identification tags attached to the body before carrying out any procedures. A signed authorization and a facility band are useful, but the tag on the body must still be matched to the funeral home's own record. Payment, a written cause of death and a burial permit are separate matters that do not replace that check.
  66. The correct answer is C (Assess the body and contact the sending home and the arranger.): The body is not in the condition the sending home described, so the licensee stops and resolves the difference before anyone views it. The sending home and the person who arranged the funeral need to know what was found, and the condition on arrival is recorded. Covering the problem with cosmetics or simply closing the casket leaves the family and the file without an answer.
  67. The correct answer is A (Apply the additional precautions now and tell the supervisor at once.): The licensee treats the hazard as real as soon as it is known and raises the level of protection before any further handling. The supervisor and everyone who will touch the body need that information, and the details are confirmed with the hospital and recorded. Routine care, a return of the body or an unexplained wait leaves staff and the family's timeline at risk.
  68. The correct answer is B (Explain what the stiffness allows and position the hands gently.): Rigor mortis fixes the muscles and limits how far a limb can be moved, so the licensee tells the family what is possible and works with the condition rather than forcing the joint. Gentle positioning with support, followed by a record of what was agreed and done, meets the family's wish as far as it can be met. Forcing the elbow, promising a pose that cannot be held or refusing to try are all poorer answers.
  69. The correct answer is D (Confirm the receiving jurisdiction's rules and the container required.): A body shipped out of the province needs the documentation that the receiving jurisdiction and Ontario require, and a container that suits the journey. The licensee confirms those points with the receiving home before giving the family a timeline. Booking first, changing the family's decision or leaving the question to the airline does not settle the requirements.
  70. The correct answer is C (Explain the exposure risk, supply protective equipment and supervise the washing.): The family's religious practice is honoured, and the work is made as safe as the condition allows with barriers, hand hygiene and the additional precautions the infection requires. The licensee explains the risk in plain language and stays involved so that the washing follows the funeral home's infection control practice. A silent risk, a refusal or a signed waiver does not protect the relatives.
  71. The correct answer is A (Explain how the body will change and offer refrigeration instead.): The licensee tells the family what warmth and time will do to the body and offers a plan that protects the appearance, such as transfer to refrigerated care. The family's decision is then recorded. Agreeing without that conversation, removing the body without permission or leaving a note about room temperature does not meet the duty to advise and protect.
  72. The correct answer is C (Contact the nursing home and the transfer team about the difference.): The personal effects record must show each item received, where it came from, who received it, the date and the condition of the item, so a difference between the body and the paperwork is resolved at once. The licensee asks the facility and the transfer team what happened to the watch and the wallet and records the answer. Replacing the items, waiting for the family to notice or treating the transfer sheet as a receipt leaves the record and the family without an answer.
  73. The correct answer is B (Tell the arranger and discuss the options with the family.): A condition found partway through the work changes the plan, so the licensee reports it and brings the family into the decision while there is still time to act. The options may include extra work, a different viewing arrangement or help from an experienced licensee. Hiding the area, delaying the news or cancelling without an explanation takes the choice away from the family.
  74. The correct answer is D (What was found, what was provided and what the family was told.): The file should show the problem, the replacement that was supplied and the conversation with the family, so the record answers any question that comes later. Reporting the problem to the supplier may also be needed, but that is not what completes the case record. The cost, the delivery time and the name of the person who moved the casket do not describe the change.
  75. The correct answer is A (The reason the solution strength and technique were chosen.): The case analysis drives the chemistry and the technique, and the record should show why those choices were made for this body. That entry explains the work to a manager, a colleague or an inspector and guides the next similar case. Supplier details, arrival times and staff numbers are administrative facts that do not explain the preparation.
  76. The correct answer is B (Stop, put on protection and look for a sharp object safely.): A hard object in clothing may be a needle, a blade or another sharp, so the licensee stops and checks the pocket with protection and a tool rather than bare fingers. Anything sharp goes straight into a rigid sharps container, and the hazard is recorded for the rest of the case. Passing the task to a colleague, shaking the object loose or handling it quickly does not remove the risk.
  77. The correct answer is C (What the chemical is and how it must be handled.): Contamination of unknown origin may require guidance from the coroner or a health authority before the body is prepared or viewed. The licensee identifies the hazard and the protective measures first, because the residue may affect the staff, the preparation room and the family. Cost, cosmetics and delivery schedules are settled after that question is answered.
  78. The correct answer is A (Stop and make sure the coroner has been notified.): A sudden death with no attending physician and unexplained marks on the body is a matter for the coroner, who controls the body in a coroner's case. The licensee stops, confirms that the coroner has been notified and takes no further step until direction is given. Recording the marks, questioning the family or starting the bathing step interferes with an investigation.
  79. The correct answer is D (Report the concern to the registrar, who may investigate.): The registrar may receive complaints about conduct that may contravene a requirement under the Act, gather information and take action, and inspectors have the power to examine premises and records. Reporting the concern puts the question in front of the body that can act on it. A private warning, a wait for an application or informal advice to families leaves the alleged unlicensed practice unchecked.
  80. The correct answer is B (Stop, discard the mixture and confirm the correct product.): Cavity fluid is far stronger than arterial fluid and is not made for injection through the vessels, so the work stops before any of the mixture enters the body. The licensee discards it, confirms the product and starts again with the fluid the case analysis calls for. Diluting the wrong product, watching for a reaction or documenting the substitution still risks damage to the body and the staff.
  81. The correct answer is C (Identification and permission, case analysis, preparation, final inspection.): The work starts from permission and identity, then the case analysis sets the plan, then the preparation is carried out and the result is checked before the family sees the body. The final inspection happens last, in the room and the lighting the family will use. Starting with the preparation, checking identity late or inspecting before the work is done leaves the plan and the family's expectations unmet.
  82. The correct answer is A (Explain the timing, guide them to the waiting area and finish the checks.): The licensee protects the family from seeing a body that is not yet ready, explains what is still being done and settles them comfortably while the work and the final inspection are completed. The doors stay closed until the body, the casket and the room are ready. Allowing the family in early, sending them outside or showing an unfinished face from the doorway all expose them to a moment they did not ask to remember.
  83. The correct answer is C (Ask the co-worker who performed the work to complete the case report.): The preparation report is a legal record of the work that names the person who performed it, describes the condition found and lists the products used. A licensee who did not do the work cannot describe those details accurately, and signing anyway misstates the record. The report belongs to the person who did the work, and the licensee should not take on that responsibility.
  84. The correct answer is B (Confirm the decision with the person who has authority.): Preparation begins only after the instructions and the authorization are verified with the person who made the arrangements or the legal representative, and the choices are recorded. Bathing may look harmless, but starting any procedure before the decision is confirmed sets the case on a path the family has not approved. Waiting passively or beginning embalming takes the decision away from the family.
  85. The correct answer is A (Remove it and dispose of it as a hazardous item.): A medication patch is handled as a hazardous material, because some patches hold a drug that can harm anyone who handles the body, so the patch is removed with protection and disposed of as the safety data sheet and local rules require. The removal and the disposal are recorded. Leaving the patch on, washing over it or asking the family to handle it leaves a hazard on the body and places the risk on the wrong person.
  86. The correct answer is D (Report the finding and adjust the plan and the family's expectations.): An unrecorded autopsy changes the preparation plan, because the vessels were cut, the organs were returned to the cavity and the cavity needs thorough aspiration and treatment. The licensee reports the finding, adjusts the plan and tells the family what the change means for the viewing. Proceeding as a routine case or asking the family to confirm the autopsy does not get the plan or the record right.
  87. The correct answer is A (Ask the co-worker to put on the required barriers now.): Universal precautions apply to every body and to every person who handles it, and the competency profile expects a licensee to encourage co-workers to follow them. The licensee speaks up before the task begins and reports the matter if the co-worker refuses. Silence, a note after the fact or doing the work alone leaves the co-worker exposed and the practice unchanged.
  88. The correct answer is B (Record the concern, review the file and report it to the supervisor.): The preparation report, the case analysis and the family's instructions are the evidence needed to answer the question, and the supervisor needs to know about the complaint. The licensee records what the family said and what the file shows. Refusing to discuss it, settling it with money or dismissing the family's account leaves the concern unresolved and unrecorded.
  89. The correct answer is D (The condition of the body and the hazard that was found.): The handover and the transfer record must carry the condition of the body and any hazard, such as a suspected infection or a wound, so that the preparation staff can choose barriers and a technique before the work starts. The permission and identification checks are recorded as well. Route details, the name of the person who gave permission and travel times do not prepare the room or protect the staff.
  90. The correct answer is C (The name on the paperwork, the tag and the casket plate.): The last check before the body leaves the building matches the identification attached to the body with the case paperwork and the name plate on the casket. That check protects against the one error no apology can repair, which is releasing the wrong body. Casket colour, delivery schedules and weights do not identify the person inside.
  91. The correct answer is B (Stop, tell the supervisor and correct the plan for the case.): The licensee stops, works out how much weak solution has entered the body and corrects the plan with the fluid and technique the case now needs, checking the tissues as the work continues. The supervisor and the person who arranged the funeral are told what happened and what the result will be. Finishing with the same weak mixture, guessing at a stronger one or abandoning the family without a plan are all poorer answers.
  92. The correct answer is A (A Class 1 director must be engaged to perform the embalming.): Only a licensed funeral director acting on behalf of a Class 1 establishment operator may perform arterial embalming, and a Funeral Director Class 2 is expressly prohibited from providing the service. An unlicensed assistant may not perform the work either, whether or not someone stays in the room. The licensee arranges for a Class 1 funeral director and adjusts the family's timeline if that is needed.
  93. The correct answer is C (Record the exposure as a hazard for the staff who handle the body.): Health information about the deceased is shared with the staff who need it to choose the right barriers and technique, and the hazard is recorded on the case file. The licensee explains that this is done to protect the people who will handle the body rather than to embarrass the family. Routine barriers alone, or passing the news to relatives or a doctor, leaves the staff without the information they need.
  94. The correct answer is D (Explain what the instruction means, then follow the family's direction.): The licensee asks the family to explain their requirement and then follows it, records it and plans a viewing that suits the condition of the body. The family is told what they will see and what the limits will be, and the family decides with that information. Substituting the funeral home's routine, refusing the viewing or acting without the family's knowledge all disregard the recorded instruction.
  95. The correct answer is B (Report the removal to the supervisor and the operator at once.): Section 52 permits only a Funeral Director Class 1 acting on behalf of a Class 1 operator, or a legally qualified medical practitioner, to be engaged for this removal. An unlicensed assistant is not an eligible person, and the operator is responsible for who is engaged for the task. The licensee reports the matter immediately so the operator can deal with the cremation, the record and the regulator, rather than recording it quietly and carrying on.
  96. The correct answer is A (Assess the change, correct what is needed, then record it.): The licensee monitors the body until the time of disposition and deals with changes as they appear, so the mouth and any seepage are checked and corrected before the doors open again. What was found and what was done are entered on the file. Waiting, refusing or closing the casket without a word takes the family's wish out of the decision.
  97. The correct answer is C (Assess the body, obtain the missing record and tell the supervisor.): The licensee needs to know what solution, vessels and treatments were used before deciding what the case still requires, so the record is obtained from the sending home. The condition of the body is assessed, the supervisor is told and the family is given honest information about the plan and the timeline. Restarting blindly, presenting the body unfinished or asking for an apology does not establish what has been done or what remains.
  98. The correct answer is D (The arterial embalming must come from a Class 1 establishment operator.): Only a Funeral Establishment Operator Class 1 is authorized to offer arterial embalming, and a Funeral Director Class 1 must act on behalf of such an operator. The transfer service may transport the body and, through a funeral director, may wash and disinfect it and set the features, but it may not arrange or conduct the visitation. The Class 2 operator provides premises where people may attend and pay their respects, and nothing more.
  99. The correct answer is B (Stop and arrange for an eligible person to remove it.): A body that still has a pacemaker or a radioactive implant must not be presented for cremation, apart from the limited exception for an implant that was received at least two years earlier or that is safe to cremate sooner. Only a Funeral Director Class 1 acting for a Class 1 operator, or a legally qualified medical practitioner, may be engaged to carry out such a removal. The licensee stops, has the device identified and arranges an eligible removal before the body is released.
  100. The correct answer is A (Set out what each preparation option will produce and record the choice.): The licensee explains what arterial embalming, non-embalming preparation with refrigeration and a closed or limited viewing would each give this family, and the family decides with that information. The conversation and the decision are written on the file before the work starts. Embalming without the family's direction, promising an appearance the condition cannot support or refusing the viewing all take the decision away from the people who hold it.
  101. The correct answer is C (The cause of death recorded on the medical certificate and the past medical history): The cause of death and the medical history are intrinsic factors because they belong to the body and its health record. Room temperature, viewing length and travel time are extrinsic factors that come from the surroundings and the arrangements. The case analysis sorts the two groups so that fluid strength, injection site and drainage plan can be chosen for this body.
  102. The correct answer is C (A gentler solution with a humectant, moderate pressure and close monitoring of tissues): Thin and dry tissue in an elderly person is fragile, so a gentler solution with a humectant protects the skin and helps hold moisture. Moderate pressure with close monitoring keeps the tissues from swelling or tearing during injection. A short visitation lowers the risk of change, so maximum pressure and a strong solution are not needed.
  103. The correct answer is D (That finding helps estimate time since death and guides how the body is positioned): Stiffness of the limbs and jaw is rigor mortis, and how far it has progressed helps the licensee estimate the time since death. The same finding tells the licensee what to expect when the body is moved and positioned for preparation. Cooling of the body is algor mortis, and pooling of blood in the lowest parts is livor mortis.
  104. The correct answer is A (Plan extra treatment of the affected areas and avoid added pressure there): Fixed livor mortis marks where blood has settled under gravity, and in this case it has settled across the face and neck. The appearance can be improved with careful topical and hypodermic treatment, and added pressure that pushes more blood into the area is avoided. Reducing the solution strength, waiting for the colour to clear, or draining before injection does not correct the staining.
  105. The correct answer is A (Allow the body to return toward room temperature and follow the fluid distribution closely): Refrigeration delays decomposition, but cold tissues accept arterial solution unevenly, so the body is allowed to warm toward room temperature and the injection is watched closely. Rapid warming, sharply increased pressure and skipped drainage all work against even distribution. Each of those shortcuts can also damage tissue or leave part of the body untreated.
  106. The correct answer is D (Cause of death, time since death, storage conditions and condition of the body): Those four observations are the signals that point to solution strength, injection site, drainage plan and extra treatment. Budget, mourner numbers, religion, fluid index and equipment are either decided later or belong to other parts of the work. The case analysis starts with the body and its history.
  107. The correct answer is D (The need for cavity treatment and for choosing vessels that can still be injected): An autopsied body has been opened, its organs removed and returned, and its vessels cut, so the plan shifts to cavity treatment and to vessels that remain usable for injection. Solution strength is matched to the condition of the body rather than automatically lowered. Drainage is still planned through another route, and preparation does not wait for sutures to heal.
  108. The correct answer is B (The vessels are smaller and more delicate, so gentler handling and lower settings are used): An infant has smaller, more delicate vessels than an adult, so the licensee chooses a gentle approach with lower settings and careful handling. Cavity treatment still follows arterial injection, and drainage remains part of distribution. Scaling adult settings by a fixed fraction is not a substitute for assessing this body.
  109. The correct answer is C (The extra tissue fluid slows distribution and drainage, so the plan needs closer monitoring): Oedema leaves excess fluid in the tissues, which can slow the movement of arterial solution and the return of drainage. The licensee plans close monitoring of swelling, drainage sites and tissue firmness, and adds hypodermic or surface treatment where needed. Oedema is not decomposition, and it does not by itself place the case outside the licensee's scope.
  110. The correct answer is D (Explain the limits of the case honestly and arrange help from a more experienced embalmer): Long immersion, advanced breakdown and distorted features mark this as a case beyond routine experience, so the licensee protects the family's interest by being honest and bringing in an experienced embalmer or specialist. Attempting advanced restoration alone, promising an impossible result, or saying that nobody can help all fail that duty. Asking for help is a professional act, and a delayed or transferred case is better than a damaged one.
  111. The correct answer is C (Record everything done to the body and pass that record to the receiving licensee): The chapter directs the licensee to keep a record of what was done and to communicate clearly with the receiving licensee. The identification tag stays with the body at all times, and washing without preserving does not improve the case. A partial injection that was not part of the plan can complicate the work that follows.
  112. The correct answer is A (The length and kind of gathering the family has planned for each body): The length and kind of viewing is an extrinsic factor, and three days of public visitation asks far more of the preparation than a brief private viewing. Age, weight and cause of death are intrinsic factors that describe the body itself. The casket and its lining do not change how the body is preserved.
  113. The correct answer is B (Consult an experienced embalmer about safe technique before starting work on this case): An unfamiliar infectious case calls for advice and a safe handling plan before the work begins. Declining the case outright does not serve the family, and extra gloves do not replace a proper plan. Speed and a stronger solution do not address the infection risk.
  114. The correct answer is C (Vessel, drainage site, solution strength, pressure, flow rate and extra treatments): The case analysis produces a preparation plan that records the vessel to inject, where drainage will be taken, the solution strength and volume, the pressure and rate of flow, cavity treatment, extra treatments and restoration. Casket, vault, marker and lot choices belong to the funeral arrangements. Paperwork, staffing and chapel details are handled as part of the business.
  115. The correct answer is C (The burned areas need surface and hypodermic treatment plus restoration for the family viewing): Deep burns are damaged tissue that arterial solution cannot reach in the usual way, so surface and hypodermic treatment are used and restoration rebuilds what the family will see. Burns are not decomposition, and the family's viewing request has to be met when the condition allows. A burn case is not automatically beyond the licensee's scope.
  116. The correct answer is D (A stronger solution, thorough drainage, cavity care and extra treatment of the skin): A body that has decomposed in a warm room for two days needs a stronger solution, complete drainage, careful cavity treatment and additional surface or hypodermic work. A weak solution with a humectant suits dry, fragile tissue instead of this body. Cavity fluid is not an arterial solution, and the condition has to be assessed before a viewing is promised.
  117. The correct answer is A (Discuss realistic options with the family and call in a specialist if needed): Advanced decomposition limits what any restoration can achieve, so the licensee talks through realistic options and brings in a specialist when the work is beyond their experience. Accepting an impossible expectation, forcing a closed casket, or concealing the condition all fail the standard of honest care. The family's wishes still guide the choices that remain open.
  118. The correct answer is A (It prevents choosing the wrong solution strength, injection point or drainage plan): The chapter describes the case analysis as the step that keeps the licensee from using the wrong solution strength, the wrong injection point or the wrong drainage plan. It does not replace the record of the work, and it is not a way to withhold information from the family. It is also not a written routine that is shown to families.
  119. The correct answer is B (The affected areas need moisture returned through the embalming solution and through careful topical care): Heat and dry air draw moisture from the eyes, lips, fingers and areas of thin skin, so the plan returns moisture through the solution and through topical care. Drying is not the same as decomposition, and it does not reverse without treatment. It also does not by itself place the case beyond the licensee's scope.
  120. The correct answer is D (Choose another injection site and a matching drainage site based on the vessels available): The chapter says the choice of vessel depends on the case, so an unsuitable area calls for a different artery and a drainage vein that still works. Using the damaged site, draining through the cavity, or waiting for incisions to heal does not give the body the distribution and drainage it needs. The plan follows the condition of the vessels, not a fixed routine.
  121. The correct answer is B (Human remains means a dead human body or the remains left after cremation): Section 1(1) defines human remains as a dead human body or the remains of a cremated human body. That wording brings cremated remains inside the rules that use the term. The definition is not tied to cremation status, to preparation in a funeral establishment, or to a burial permit.
  122. The correct answer is C (Embalming is preservation by a method other than refrigeration, so cooling is not embalming): Section 1(1) defines embalm as preserving and disinfecting part or all of a body by any means other than refrigeration. Cooling is therefore outside the definition, and a holding room stays a holding room. The definition is not tied to the place of treatment or to the point of casketing.
  123. The correct answer is A (Religious rites that involve washing a body are not included in the definition): The definition of embalm excludes religious rites relating to the washing of a body, so the rite stays outside the definition even inside a funeral establishment. Written consent from the family and the identity of the person doing the washing do not change that wording. Refrigeration is the other main practice that the definition leaves out.
  124. The correct answer is B (Premises used to place dead bodies temporarily so people may pay their respects): Section 1(1) defines a funeral establishment as premises established to place dead human bodies temporarily, and in prescribed circumstances cremated human remains, so that persons may attend and pay their respects. Casket storage, office space and crematorium operations do not meet that description on their own. The definition turns on the purpose of the premises.
  125. The correct answer is C (The care and preparation of dead bodies and the coordination of rites and ceremonies): Section 1(1) places the care and preparation of dead human bodies and the coordination and provision of rites and ceremonies within funeral services. Grave opening and closing, crematorium work and retail sales of supplies belong to other definitions. The same wording states that funeral services do not include cemetery or crematorium services.
  126. The correct answer is B (Funeral services do not include cemetery services or crematorium services under the Act): Section 1(1) says funeral services do not include cemetery or crematorium services, so a grave opening charge sits outside the definition. Whether the funeral home arranges or bills the service does not move it into funeral services. The definitions keep each type of service in its own category.
  127. The correct answer is B (Transfer service, which covers transporting bodies and completing the required disposition documents): Section 1(1) defines a transfer service as a service to the public with respect to the disposition of dead human bodies, including transportation and the filling out of the necessary documentation. That wording describes the company's work directly. Funeral, cemetery and crematorium services are separate definitions with their own wording.
  128. The correct answer is A (Cemetery services, crematorium services, funeral services and transfer services): Section 1(1) defines licensed services as cemetery services, crematorium services, funeral services and transfer services, and then includes interment rights, scattering rights and other services sold by a licensed person in the normal course. Casket and marker sales are licensed supplies rather than services. Permits, registrations, flowers and catering are not the categories the Act names.
  129. The correct answer is B (Licensed services includes interment rights and scattering rights along with the four service types): The definition of licensed services lists cemetery services, crematorium services, funeral services and transfer services and then includes interment rights and scattering rights. That wording brings both kinds of rights inside the Act when they are sold or provided by a licensed person. They are not supplies, and the Act does not wait for the burial or the scattering to happen.
  130. The correct answer is D (Caskets and markers and other supplies sold by a licensed person in the normal course): Section 1(1) defines licensed supplies as caskets and markers and any other supplies sold by a person licensed under the Act in the normal course of a business regulated under the Act. Cemetery lots and rights are licensed services rather than supplies. The definition is wider than caskets alone and is limited to the normal course of the business.
  131. The correct answer is C (Operator, meaning a person licensed to operate a funeral establishment or listed business): Section 1(1) defines operator as a person licensed to operate a cemetery, crematorium, funeral establishment, casket or marker retailing business, transfer service or any other business for which a licence may be required. Licensee is the wider term for any person who holds a licence under the Act. Purchaser and bereaved person are not defined in section 1(1).
  132. The correct answer is C (The worker is a licensee, because that term covers any person holding a licence): A licensee is any person who holds a licence issued under the Act, so the funeral director's licence makes the worker a licensee. Operator is the narrower term for the person licensed to run the business itself. An employee is not automatically an interested person, and the worker is not a purchaser in this situation.
  133. The correct answer is A (A casket holds a body and is not a vault or a grave liner): Section 1(1) defines a casket as a container intended to hold a dead human body for funeral, cremation or interment purposes and states that it is not a vault, burial container or grave liner. The sealed outer container in this scenario is therefore not a casket. A casket may be used for cremation or burial, and it is not defined by who supplies it.
  134. The correct answer is B (A marker, because a marker includes a plaque affixed to a burial lot): Section 1(1) defines a marker as a monument, tombstone, plaque, headstone, cornerstone or other structure or ornament affixed or intended to be affixed to a burial lot, mausoleum crypt, columbarium niche or other place intended for human remains. The plaque fits that definition. A casket holds a body, a lot is the land itself, and a private structure is a mausoleum or columbarium.
  135. The correct answer is D (The corporations are associated, so the first one's contravention may affect the second application): Section 1(2) treats corporations as associated when the same person controls each of them directly or indirectly, and section 14(1)(a) makes a contravention by an interested person a ground to refuse a licence. Association does not depend on shared officers, on a declaration by the registrar, or on the corporations being a single legal person. The advisor's advice leaves the interested person rules out of account.
  136. The correct answer is D (The Act applies to licensed supplies and services even when the purchaser is outside Ontario): Section 1.1 states that the Act applies to all transactions relating to licensed supplies and services even if the purchaser, or the person dealing with the purchaser, is located outside Ontario. Neither the purchaser's location nor the place where the contract is signed removes the transaction from the Act. The location of the funeral home does not limit the section either.
  137. The correct answer is B (The applicant has not successfully completed the prescribed educational requirements for the licence): Section 14(1)(g) states that an applicant is not entitled to a licence if the applicant, or another person that is prescribed, has not successfully completed the educational requirements that are prescribed. A business plan, advertising and a hiring decision are not grounds listed in section 14(1). The grounds in that subsection are the ones that can take away the applicant's entitlement.
  138. The correct answer is B (The licensee is in breach of a condition of the licence): Section 14(1)(h) makes a breach of a licence condition a ground on which an applicant or licensee is not entitled to the licence. Keeping the records required by the condition is part of operating the business in compliance with the licence. Education, financial responsibility and filing duties are separate grounds or duties that do not match this situation.
  139. The correct answer is C (The Licence Appeal Tribunal established under the Licence Appeal Tribunal Act, 1999): Section 1(1) defines Tribunal as the Licence Appeal Tribunal established under the Licence Appeal Tribunal Act, 1999, or another tribunal that may be prescribed. Section 18 gives the licensee 15 days after service of the notice to request a hearing by that body. The court, the board of the administrative authority and the ministry are not the Tribunal named in the Act.
  140. The correct answer is D (An applicant is entitled to the licence unless one of the listed grounds applies): Section 14(1) begins by stating that an applicant is entitled to a licence or a renewal unless one of the listed grounds applies. The section therefore starts from entitlement and then lists the grounds that can take it away. Payment of a fee, interviews with licensees and a hearing are not the test that the section sets.
  141. The correct answer is A (An interested person in respect of the applicant is in contravention of the Act): Section 14(1)(a) makes it a ground to refuse where the applicant or an interested person in respect of the applicant is in contravention of the Act or the regulations. A company that provides financing to the applicant's business can be an interested person under section 14(3)(c). Residency, customer lists and a criminal conviction are not the ground that matches this wording.
  142. The correct answer is C (The clause about an applicant who is not a corporation and cannot be financially responsible): Section 14(1)(b)(i) applies to an applicant who is not a corporation and cannot reasonably be expected to be financially responsible in the conduct of business, having regard to the applicant's financial position or that of an interested person. The resources and facilities ground is separate because it concerns what the applicant can provide rather than the applicant's finances. The prescribed requirements and licence condition grounds are different clauses.
  143. The correct answer is A (The past conduct of the applicant gives reasonable grounds for belief about future conduct): Section 14(1)(b)(ii) makes the past conduct of the applicant, or of an interested person, a ground where it affords reasonable grounds for belief that the applicant will not carry on business in accordance with the law and with integrity and honesty. The Act does not impose a blanket bar based on a conviction. Education and licence conditions are separate grounds in the same subsection.
  144. The correct answer is A (The applicant made a false statement in an application for a licence): Section 14(1)(b)(iii) makes a false statement, or the providing of a false statement, in an application a ground for refusing entitlement. The same idea appears for corporations under section 14(1)(c)(ii), where an officer, director, employee or agent makes the false statement. Financial responsibility, another Act and resources and facilities are different grounds.
  145. The correct answer is B (Past conduct of a director suggests the business may not be run honestly): Section 14(1)(c)(i) applies where the past conduct of the applicant's officers or directors, or of an interested person, affords reasonable grounds for belief that the corporation's business will not be carried on in accordance with the law and with integrity and honesty. A corporation can hold a licence, so the first option is wrong. Educational requirements and annual reports are not the ground that this history raises.
  146. The correct answer is A (The registrar may consider the experience and competence of the applicant and managing employees): Section 14(1)(d)(i) lets the registrar hold the opinion that the applicant or the applicant's managing employees lack the integrity, honesty, experience and competence required to manage the business in accordance with the law. The clause looks at the applicant and the managing employees, so a clean financial record alone does not settle the question. A conviction is not the only route to refusal, and the registrar is not required to issue the licence with a training condition.
  147. The correct answer is B (The applicant is unable to provide the resources and facilities required to manage the business): Section 14(1)(d)(ii) allows refusal where the applicant is unable to provide the resources and facilities required to manage a business, which covers the missing equipment and the absent site plan. Financial irresponsibility and past conduct are separate grounds, and this applicant's record does not raise them. A connection between corporations is not by itself a ground under the subsection.
  148. The correct answer is A (The operation creates a risk to public health, safety or decency): Section 14(1)(d)(iii) permits refusal where the registrar has reasonable grounds to believe that the operation of the business creates a risk to public health, safety or decency, and the shared wall and missing separation raise that concern. The other options describe grounds that the facts do not support. The registrar weighs the risk the operation itself creates, not the applicant's intentions.
  149. The correct answer is D (A licence is not transferable, so the buyer needs a licence of their own): Section 14(2) states plainly that a licence is not transferable, so a sale of the business does not carry the licence with it. The buyer must apply for a licence and meet the requirements in section 14(1) before operating. The registrar has no power to approve a transfer of the licence, with or without a signed notice.
  150. The correct answer is D (The lender may be an interested person because of the financing and the board appointment): Section 14(3) deems a person to be an interested person if the person is associated with the other person or if, in the registrar's opinion, the person has or may have a beneficial interest in the business, exercises or may exercise control over the other person, or has provided or may provide financing. Providing most of the financing and appointing a board member engages the financing and control branches of the definition. Share ownership and a charge are not conditions of the definition.
  151. The correct answer is A (A licence to operate a funeral establishment issued under the Act): Section 8 (1) prohibits any person from operating a funeral establishment, or holding themself out as its operator, unless the person is licensed to do so. The licence is the only authorization that permits that activity. Municipal approvals and health permits address premises and sanitation rather than the right to operate.
  152. The correct answer is C (A licence is not required for rites traditionally provided in a place of worship): Section 8 (3) states that a licence is not required with respect to rites or ceremonies traditionally provided in a place of worship. The exception is tied to the traditional nature of the rite and to its setting. Nothing in the subsection turns on the length of the service or on who attends it.
  153. The correct answer is B (To make sure every person to whom responsibility is delegated complies with the rules): Section 9 (1) (c) places a duty on the operator for every other person to whom the operator delegates responsibility, and each of those persons must carry out that responsibility in compliance with the Act and the regulations. The duty follows the delegated task, so the operator stays responsible even when the work is handed to someone else. Telling staff that the contractor bears the responsibility does not shift that duty.
  154. The correct answer is D (A transfer service operator licence granted under the Act): Section 12 (1) states that no person shall operate, or hold themself out as the operator of, a transfer service unless the person is licensed to operate a transfer service. A funeral establishment licence and a commercial vehicle permit are separate approvals that do not authorize this business. The licence class that matches the activity is the transfer service operator licence.
  155. The correct answer is A (A person licensed as a transfer service operator or a funeral establishment operator): Section 12 (2) (a) allows a person licensed as a transfer service operator or a funeral establishment operator to sell, or offer to sell, transfer services to the public. Clause (b) adds a person who holds a prescribed licence and is acting on behalf of one of those operators. A municipal business licence and a college credential do not authorize the selling.
  156. The correct answer is B (Contact made by telephone or by a face to face meeting): Section 29 (1) prohibits a licensee or other person from contacting someone by telephone or in person to solicit or negotiate a contract for a licensed supply or service. Subsection (2) uses wider wording and bans contact by any means, but only where the person is in a hospital, long-term care home, hospice or prescribed institution. Newspaper and website advertising is not the kind of contact named in subsection (1).
  157. The correct answer is C (A hospital, a long-term care home or a hospice): Section 29 (2) names a hospital, a long-term care home, a hospice and any other institution that may be prescribed. Contact with a person in one of those places, by any means, for the purpose of soliciting or negotiating a contract for a licensed supply or service is prohibited. Places of worship, schools and municipal offices are not named in the subsection.
  158. The correct answer is A (The contact is not prohibited when made at the request of that person): Section 29 (3) (a) states that the section does not prohibit a licensee from contacting a person at the request of that person. Because the person made the first move, the contact falls outside the prohibition. The section sets no waiting period, no written confirmation step and no requirement for a second licensee to attend.
  159. The correct answer is D (When the supplies are not for that other business's own use or benefit): Section 29 (3) (b) allows a licensee to contact another licensee or business to solicit or negotiate a contract if the supplies or services under the contract are not for the personal use or benefit of that other licensee or business. The test is who benefits from the contract rather than the age of a licence or the hour of the call. Registrar pre-approval is not part of the exception.
  160. The correct answer is B (A fine up to $50,000, and jail up to two years less a day): Section 79 (3) makes an individual convicted of an offence under the Act liable to a fine of not more than $50,000, to imprisonment for not more than two years less a day, or to both. The $250,000 ceiling in the same subsection applies to a corporation. Both a fine and a jail term are therefore available against an individual.
  161. The correct answer is C (A fine capped at $250,000, which applies to a corporation): Section 79 (3) makes a corporation convicted of an offence under the Act liable to a fine of not more than $250,000. The $50,000 figure in the same subsection applies to an individual. Imprisonment is not an available penalty for a corporation, so its whole exposure is financial.
  162. The correct answer is A (That a person is in charge of day-to-day operations at each licensed location): Section 22 (1) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires an operator to ensure there is a person in charge of the day-to-day operations of each business location identified on the operator's licence. That person must also have completed the education required as a result of section 42 of the regulation. The regulation sets no minimum number of funeral directors per location and no rule about where the operator lives.
  163. The correct answer is D (The duties continue until all of them have been fulfilled by the operator): Section 17 of Ontario Regulation 30/11 applies where a licence expires without a renewal application, is cancelled on the operator's written request under section 21 of the Act, or is suspended, surrendered or revoked. The person who held the licence remains subject to the duties and obligations of an operator until all of them are fulfilled. That includes completing the outstanding prepaid contract.
  164. The correct answer is C (Making a false, misleading or deceptive statement about licensed supplies or services): Section 27 prohibits a licensee from making a false, misleading or deceptive statement in any advertisement, circular, pamphlet or material published or distributed by any means relating to the sale or provision of licensed supplies or services. The rule targets the content of the statement. It does not require a photograph of the operator or advance approval of a business name.
  165. The correct answer is B (A long-term care home as defined in the Fixing Long-Term Care Act, 2021): Section 135 of Ontario Regulation 30/11 prescribes a long-term care home, as defined in the Fixing Long-Term Care Act, 2021, for the purposes of subsection 29 (2) of the Act. The definition links the phrase used in the Act to the current long-term care statute. Private residences and retirement homes are not prescribed by that section.
  166. The correct answer is D (He is in breach, because operating and holding out both require a licence): Section 8 (1) prohibits a person from operating a funeral establishment, or holding themself out as its operator, unless the person is licensed to do so. Displaying the sign and taking arrangement calls amount to both operating and holding out. Hiring a director later or labelling the business as unlicensed would not cure the breach.
  167. The correct answer is A (It holds the shop out as available to sell funeral services without a licence): Section 8 (2) prohibits selling or offering to sell funeral services to the public, and holding oneself out as available to sell them, unless one of the listed conditions is met. Saying that funeral arrangements can be made through the shop is a claim of availability to sell. The registry problem here is the holding out itself.
  168. The correct answer is B (Because the director holds a prescribed licence and acts for the operator): Section 8 (2) (a) permits a person who holds a prescribed licence and is acting on behalf of a funeral establishment operator to sell funeral services to the public. The director meets both parts of that test. Employment alone does not satisfy the clause, because the licence must be a prescribed one and the person must be acting for the operator.
  169. The correct answer is C (The representative holds a prescribed licence and acts for that operator): Section 12 (2) (b) allows a person who holds a prescribed licence and is acting on behalf of a transfer service operator or a funeral establishment operator to sell transfer services to the public. Section 5 (2) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 prescribes the sales representative licence for that clause. The representative does not need a separate transfer service operator licence.
  170. The correct answer is D (A duty to ensure the establishment is operated according to the Act and regulations): Section 9 (1) (a) requires the operator of a funeral establishment to ensure the establishment is operated in accordance with the Act and the regulations. The duty covers the condition and use of the premises rather than only the paperwork. Treating the problem as minor does not remove the duty from the operator.
  171. The correct answer is B (Ensuring the employed licensee carries out duties in compliance with the rules): Section 9 (1) (b) requires the operator to ensure compliance by every licensee employed in the funeral establishment. That duty sits alongside the employee's own accountability, so the operator cannot point at the employee and stop there. Reporting an error to a discipline committee is governed by the complaints and discipline process rather than by this clause.
  172. The correct answer is A (The operator may sell transfer services because of the operator licence held): Section 12 (2) (a) permits a person licensed as a transfer service operator or a funeral establishment operator to sell or offer to sell transfer services to the public. A separate transfer service licence is not required of a funeral establishment operator. The family's location does not change the licence class that applies.
  173. The correct answer is C (Transport must occur no later than seventy two hours after death or refrigeration): Section 35 (4) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 provides that a transfer service operator shall not transport a dead human body that has not been arterially embalmed later than 72 hours after the death or after removal of the body from refrigeration. The regulation measures the limit from those events. A 24 hour or 48 hour limit does not appear in that provision.
  174. The correct answer is D (Decline the request, because arterial embalming sits outside the operator's class of licence): Section 36 (4) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 provides that a Class 1 transfer service operator shall not offer to sell, sell or provide the services of arterially embalming or restoring dead human bodies, apart from placing bodies in caskets and limited care of the body. A family consent form or the presence of a funeral director does not expand the operator's authority. That work belongs to a funeral establishment operator whose establishment provides it.
  175. The correct answer is B (Transporting remains to or from the place where the rite is held): Section 36 (6) prohibits a Class 1 transfer service operator from arranging, co-ordinating, conducting or participating in a visitation, funeral or other rite or ceremony at which human remains are present. The permitted activity is transporting the remains to or from a holding room or the place of the rite, together with scattering remains authorized under section 2. Charging no fee does not make the arranging lawful.
  176. The correct answer is A (Handling personal effects, placing the body in a casket and transporting it): Section 37 (2) requires a Class 2 transfer service operator to ensure no employee has contact with a dead human body except to remove personal effects, place the body in a casket and transport the body. Washing, setting features, restoring and embalming fall outside that list. Those tasks belong to a licensee whose class of licence covers them.
  177.
