The correct answer is D (Transport from the place of removal to a cemetery, crematorium or holding room): Section 37 (4) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 provides that a Class 2 transfer service operator shall not transport a dead human body except from the place where removal was first requested directly to a cemetery or crematorium, or to an approved holding room and then to a cemetery or crematorium. A private viewing at a family home is not part of that route. The operator cannot vary the route by agreement with the family.
  178. The correct answer is C (The operator must obtain the registrar's consent for the shared manager): Section 22 (3) requires a funeral establishment operator or transfer service operator to avoid placing one person in charge of the day-to-day operations of more than one business, or at more than one location, unless the registrar has consented. Distance between two locations does not replace that consent. Section 22 (4) lists factors the registrar may weigh, including geographic separation, business volume and compliance history.
  179. The correct answer is B (The manager must be licensed as a Funeral Director Class 1): Section 33 (4) (a) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires that, where a funeral establishment provides the services of arterially embalming or restoring dead human bodies, the day-to-day operations are managed by a person licensed as a Funeral Director Class 1. Section 33 (3) allows a Funeral Director Class 1 or Class 2 where those services are not provided. Adding arterial embalming moves the establishment into the stricter rule.
  180. The correct answer is A (A Funeral Director Class 1 or Class 2 must manage the daily operations): Section 34 (2) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires a Class 2 funeral establishment operator to ensure the day-to-day operations of the funeral establishment are managed by a person licensed as a Funeral Director Class 1 or Class 2. A sales representative licence does not cover that role. The general person in charge rule in section 22 of the regulation applies as well.
  181. The correct answer is D (The operator remains subject to the duties until all have been fulfilled): Section 17 of Ontario Regulation 30/11 deals with a licence that expires without a renewal application, is cancelled on the operator's written request, or is suspended, surrendered or revoked. In each case the person who was licensed remains subject to the duties and obligations of an operator until they are all fulfilled. The prepaid contracts must still be honoured or refunded under the Act.
  182. The correct answer is C (Section 29 (1), which prohibits telephone contact to solicit or negotiate): Section 29 (1) prohibits a licensee or other person from contacting someone by telephone or in person to solicit or negotiate a contract for a licensed supply or service. The widow did not request the call, so the exception in section 29 (3) (a) is unavailable. Section 27 covers advertising content rather than personal contact, and section 30 covers false information.
  183. The correct answer is A (The licensee breached the rule, because contact by any means is prohibited there): Section 29 (2) prohibits a licensee or other person from contacting a person in a hospital, long-term care home, hospice or prescribed institution by any means to solicit or negotiate a contract for a licensed supply or service. The rule is not limited to the patient's room, and no contract need be signed for a breach. Because the family member did not request the contact, no exception applies.
  184. The correct answer is B (No, because the prohibition covers contact made by telephone or in person): Section 29 (1) prohibits a licensee or other person from contacting someone by telephone or in person to solicit or negotiate a contract for a licensed supply or service. A mass mailing is not contact of either kind, so it does not breach that subsection. The brochure remains subject to other rules, including the truthfulness requirement in section 27 and the promotional content rule in section 119 of the regulation.
  185. The correct answer is D (The call is allowed because the licensee is contacting her at her request): Section 29 (3) (a) states that the section does not prohibit a licensee from contacting a person at the request of that person. Because the resident asked for the call, the contact falls outside both subsection (1) and subsection (2). Approval from the home's administrator is not a condition in section 29.
  186. The correct answer is C (Because the supplies are not for the personal use or benefit of that business): Section 29 (3) (b) allows a licensee to contact another licensee or business to solicit or negotiate a contract if the supplies or services under the contract are not for the personal use or benefit of that other licensee or business. The exception turns on who benefits from the contract. The section does not require that both sides hold a licence.
  187. The correct answer is A (Section 29 (1), because it bans telephone contact made to solicit a contract): Section 29 (1) prohibits a licensee or other person from contacting someone by telephone or in person to solicit or negotiate a contract for a licensed supply or service. The daughter did not request the call, so section 29 (3) (a) does not apply. Section 29 (2) concerns contact with a person in a hospital, long-term care home, hospice or prescribed institution, which is not the situation here.
  188. The correct answer is B (An order requiring the correction or the retraction of that statement): Section 28 (1) allows the registrar who believes on reasonable grounds that a licensee is making a false, misleading or deceptive statement to order the cessation of the material, a retraction or a correction of equal prominence, or both. The power is aimed at the misleading statement itself. An order under the section takes effect immediately, although the Tribunal may grant a stay.
  189. The correct answer is D (The registrar may designate a period of up to twelve months): Section 28 (5) states that the licensee shall submit statements under subsection (4) for a period of time that the registrar designates, which shall not exceed 12 months. The duty follows an order that the licensee did not appeal or that the Tribunal upheld. A designation longer than twelve months would exceed the section.
  190. The correct answer is C (The operator's name and business name, if different from the operator's name): Section 119 (1) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires certain information in a sign or written advertisement, brochure, price list, contract, letterhead, pamphlet, circular or other written material used to promote the sale of a licensed supply or service. The operator's name and any different business name are required, along with the name of a person who controls the operator's business and another nearby licensed business. Employee licence numbers and registrar names are not part of the list.
  191. The correct answer is B (Decline the request and explain the transport limits that its licence class sets): Section 37 (4) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 limits a Class 2 transfer service operator to transporting a body from the place where removal was first requested directly to a cemetery or crematorium, or to an approved holding room and then to a cemetery or crematorium. A family request does not widen the licence class. Adding a stop at a funeral home would not make the route lawful.
  192. The correct answer is A (The exception applies only if the patient herself requests the contact): Section 29 (3) (a) permits a licensee to contact a person at the request of that person, so the request must come from the person being contacted. A request from a hospital social worker is not the patient's request. A visit to a patient in a hospital to solicit or negotiate a contract would also breach section 29 (2), which bans contact by any means in that setting.
  193. The correct answer is D (No, because section 29 (1) applies to a licensee or other person): The opening words of section 29 (1) cover a licensee or other person, so the prohibition reaches someone who holds no licence under the Act. Her conduct also raises the licensing prohibitions in sections 8 and 12 and the offence provision in section 79. Holding a licence is not a precondition for the anti-soliciting rule to apply to her.
  194. The correct answer is C (Both calls breach the rule, since one also reaches a hospital patient): Section 29 (1) bans telephone contact made to solicit or negotiate a contract for a licensed supply or service, so the call to the woman's home already breaches that subsection. Section 29 (2) bans contact by any means with a person in a hospital for the same purpose, so the second call breaches that subsection as well. The woman did not request either call, so the exception in section 29 (3) (a) does not apply.
  195. The correct answer is A (An explanation of the conditions of sale and a full description of what is included): Section 119 (6) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires an operator who advertises a price to include an explanation of all the conditions of sale that relate to the advertised price and a full description of the supplies and services that the price covers. A reader must be able to see what the advertised figure does and does not include. Staff names, trust account balances and regional comparisons are not required.
  196. The correct answer is B (That the director failed to take reasonable care to prevent the offence): Section 79 (2) provides that an officer or director of a corporation is guilty of an offence who fails to take reasonable care to prevent the corporation from committing an offence mentioned in subsection (1). The Crown does not have to prove that the director personally did the act. Share ownership and licence signing are not the test.
  197. The correct answer is B (The licensee must produce the records, because the Act requires cooperation.): Section 67 of the Act allows the registrar or a designate to inspect a licensee's business premises without a warrant at a reasonable time and to review the records kept there. A licensee must produce what the inspector asks for and must not obstruct an inspection, and a duty of confidentiality to a family does not excuse a refusal. Because the inspection power comes from the Act itself, the inspector does not need a court order first.
  198. The correct answer is D ($250,000 for the corporation and $50,000 for the director.): Section 79(3) of the Act sets a fine of not more than $50,000 for an individual and not more than $250,000 for a corporation, and a director or officer who directed the offence can be convicted in their own right. A term of imprisonment of not more than two years less a day is also available where the offender is an individual. The other amounts do not match the penalties the Act provides.
  199. The correct answer is A (No proceeding may begin more than two years after the director learns the facts): Section 79 (4) states that no proceeding under section 79 shall be commenced more than two years after the facts on which the proceeding is based first came to the knowledge of the director. The clock runs from the director's knowledge rather than from the date of the contravention. A complaint to the registrar does not start a different limitation period.
  200. The correct answer is B (Selling, lending or permitting the use of its business name or logo to that agent): Section 132 (1) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 prohibits a licensee from selling, lending or permitting the use of an operator's business name, logo, letterhead or other identifier to a person who is soliciting an annuity or insurance contract, or enrolment in group insurance, with a vulnerable person mentioned in subsection 29 (2) of the Act. A resident of a long-term care home falls within that group. A fee for the use of the logo is not what the section regulates.
  201. The correct answer is B (A price list of the licensed supplies and services that the operator provides.): Section 33(1) requires every operator to maintain a price list of the licensed supplies and services that the operator provides, in accordance with the regulations. Section 33(2) adds that every licensee shall make the price list available to the public in accordance with the regulations. A complaint log, a wage schedule or another cemetery's by-laws are not the document that section 33(1) names.
  202. The correct answer is D (In plain language and printed legibly in ten point or larger type.): Section 54 of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires the price list to be written in plain language and legibly printed in 10 point or larger type. The rule sets both a language standard and a minimum type size. Changing either element makes the choice wrong.
  203. The correct answer is A (Every licensed supply and service that the operator generally provides.): Section 55(1) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires the price list to include every licensed supply and service that the operator generally provides. The list is not limited to popular items or to items above a certain price. A funeral home cannot omit a supply or service that it normally offers.
  204. The correct answer is C (A statement that the price includes the taxes or that taxes are additional.): Section 55(2)(c) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires the price list to state that the price includes the applicable taxes or that the applicable taxes are in addition to the price. The purchaser must be able to tell from the list whether tax is already built into the figure. A statement about a future tax assessment does not meet the rule.
  205. The correct answer is D (Stay up to date and always indicate its effective date.): Section 55(6) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires the price list to always be up to date and to indicate its effective date. The operator may not keep using a list that no longer shows current prices. Comparing prices with other funeral homes is not a requirement of the regulation.
  206. The correct answer is A (The operator's name, business name, business address, telephone number and the person in charge.): Section 59 of Ontario Regulation 30/11 lists the identification information that must appear at the beginning of the price list. That information includes the operator's name, the business name if it is different, the address and telephone number of the business location, and the position and contact details of the person in charge of day-to-day operations. Employee names and past cemetery work do not belong in that opening block.
  207. The correct answer is B (Provide the copy without charge to the person who requested it.): Section 68(1) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires every operator to provide a copy of the price list, without charge, to every person who requests it. The operator may not charge a printing fee or limit the request to prospective purchasers. Section 68(2) also requires the list to be displayed or a notice posted near the main entrance.
  208. The correct answer is C (Charge no additional amount and retain only the amounts that are prescribed.): Section 38 says that if money is paid in advance and the price increases before the supplies or services are provided, the operator shall not charge the purchaser any additional amount in respect of the increase. The operator may retain only the amounts that are prescribed. Passing the supplier's increase to the family is not permitted.
  209. The correct answer is A (All the supplies and services to be provided and the price charged for each.): Section 40(1)(c) says a contract is not enforceable by an operator unless it sets out all the supplies and services to be provided and the price charged for each of them. A combined total or a general category does not satisfy that requirement. The purchaser must be able to see each item and its own price.
  210. The correct answer is D (On the day the last requirement in section 40(1) is met.): Section 42(1) gives the purchaser thirty days to cancel, counted from the day on which the last of the requirements described in section 40(1) is met. That is not necessarily the day the purchaser signed or paid. The operator's later delivery of the signed copy can therefore start the clock.
  211. The correct answer is C (Cancel by written notice within 30 days if the operator has not fully performed.): Section 43(2) allows the purchaser to cancel by giving written notice within 30 days after the day the contract is made if the operator has not fully performed the contract. Section 43(1) supports that right by prohibiting provision of the supplies or services inside that period unless the regulations permit it. A shorter or longer window would not match the section.
  212. The correct answer is B (Cancel the contract at any time by giving written notice to the operator.): Section 44(1) gives the purchaser a further right to cancel at any time once a right under section 42 or 43 no longer applies and the operator has not fully performed the contract. The right is not limited to the first thirty days. It does not apply to interment rights or scattering rights, which section 44(1) excludes.
  213. The correct answer is A (Such other persons as may be prescribed, if the regulations so provide.): Section 45 says the cancellation rights under sections 41, 42, 43 and 44 may also be exercised by such other persons as may be prescribed, and the refund may be required to be paid to those persons, if the regulations so provide. The Act does not leave this to whoever attends the arrangement meeting. Ontario Regulation 30/11 sets out the permitted designations.
  214. The correct answer is D (At any time after the contract is made by giving written notice.): Section 41(1) says a purchaser under a contract that is not enforceable by the operator under section 40(1) may cancel the contract at any time after it is made by giving the operator written notice of cancellation. The right is not tied to a thirty day window. Section 41(2) then requires the operator to refund all money received within 30 days of receiving the notice.
  215. The correct answer is B (The contract must set out the purchaser's cancellation rights under the Act.): Section 40(1)(b) says a contract is not enforceable by an operator unless the contract sets out the purchaser's cancellation rights under the Act. A list of supplies and prices alone does not satisfy this clause. Letterhead, supplier names and registrar approval are not the requirement in section 40(1)(b).
  216. The correct answer is C (A licence under the Act is not required to sell the supplies and services.): Subsection 40(3) of the Act allows prescribed contracts to be exempt, and section 122(1) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 sets out the conditions. One condition is that a licence issued under the Act is not required in order to sell the supplies and services. All of the listed conditions must be met for the exemption to apply.
  217. The correct answer is A (They must be purchased separately from licensed supplies or services.): Section 122(1) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires the supplies and services to be purchased separately from licensed supplies or services. A purchase that is bundled with licensed funeral services does not fit the exemption. The regulation also requires the other listed conditions, such as the price limit and immediate delivery.
  218. The correct answer is D (The purchaser must take immediate delivery of the supply being sold.): Section 122(1) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires the purchaser to be taking immediate delivery of the supply or receiving the service immediately. A customer who wants to pick the item up next week does not meet that condition. The contract would not be exempt.
  219. The correct answer is B (The supply must not be stored by the operator.): Section 122(1) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 exempts a contract only when every condition in that provision is met, and one of the conditions is that the supply will not be stored by the operator. Asking the funeral home to keep the marker in its garage fails that condition, so the exemption cannot apply. The age of the purchaser is not one of the conditions the regulation sets, so it does not decide the question.
  220. The correct answer is C (The operator shall not split contracts to keep each one under the threshold.): Section 122(2) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 prohibits an operator from entering into separate contracts instead of a single contract if the purpose is to keep the value of each contract under $250 so that the exemption applies. The threshold cannot be engineered by splitting the sale. Each contract must be looked at honestly.
  221. The correct answer is A (When they are required for the disposition of human remains within the period.): Section 139(1)(a) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 allows the purchaser to request provision within 30 days after the contract is made when the supplies or services are required for the disposition of human remains within that period. Section 139(1)(b) covers supplies or services required for the co-ordination and provision of rites or ceremonies. Section 139(1)(c) covers contracts that fall under the exemption in section 122(1).
  222. The correct answer is D (The operator is exempt from section 43 with respect to the provision of those rights.): Section 139(5) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 provides that an operator who is a party to a contract for the provision of interment rights or scattering rights is exempt from section 43 of the Act with respect to the provision of those rights. The delivery restriction therefore does not apply to the rights themselves. Section 43(3) separately addresses rights that have already been exercised.
  223. The correct answer is C (The right to cancel does not apply to interment rights that have been exercised.): Section 43(3) states that the right to cancel under section 43(2) does not apply with respect to interment or scattering rights that have been exercised. A burial that has already taken place means the interment rights have been exercised. The purchaser cannot use section 43(2) to undo that burial.
  224. The correct answer is B (The purchaser receives a copy of the contract as signed by the purchaser.): Section 127(1) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires the purchaser to receive a copy of the contract as signed by the purchaser at the time the purchaser signs it. This is separate from the later delivery of the fully signed contract. The copy given at signing must be the copy the purchaser actually signed.
  225. The correct answer is A (Personally, by registered mail, or another way if receipt can be proved.): Section 127(2) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 sets the prescribed manner of delivering the signed contract for the purposes of section 40(1)(d) of the Act. The operator must deliver it personally, send it by registered mail, or send it another way if the operator can prove receipt by the purchaser. Delivery must happen as soon as possible.
  226. The correct answer is D (The individual who negotiated for the operator must have signed the contract.): Paragraph 1 of section 128 of Ontario Regulation 30/11 states that a contract is not enforceable by an operator if the contract was not signed by the individual who negotiated the contract on behalf of the operator. The negotiator's signature is part of enforceability. Witnesses and the order of signatures are not the requirement in that paragraph.
  227. The correct answer is B (By text-based communications between the operator and the purchaser.): Paragraph 2 of section 128 of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires that, if the operator entered into the contract with the purchaser over the internet, the contract was formed by text-based communications. A recorded call, a video meeting or a later paper copy does not satisfy that requirement. The regulation looks at how the online contract itself was formed.
  228. The correct answer is C (A copy of the cemetery by-laws, notice about resale, and a description of the lot location.): Section 40(1)(e) applies to a contract for the purchase of interment rights. The operator must deliver a copy of the cemetery by-laws, written notice of whether the by-laws permit resale to a third party, and a description of the location of the lot that is purchased. Section 40(1)(f) sets the parallel rule for scattering rights.
  229. The correct answer is D (A copy of the crematorium's by-laws for the purchaser to keep.): Section 40(1)(g) applies to a contract for the purchase of crematorium supplies and services. In that case the operator must deliver a copy of the crematorium's by-laws to the purchaser. The other documents listed in the choices are not the document named by that clause.
  230. The correct answer is A (Bring an action in court to recover the amounts paid together with costs.): Section 40(2) gives the purchaser a right of action in a court of competent jurisdiction to recover the amounts paid under the contract, together with costs. The right arises if the purchaser has given notice of cancellation under section 41(1) and the operator has refused to pay the amount payable under section 41(2). A complaint to the registrar is a separate process.
  231. The correct answer is C (The greater of the income earned and the income that should have been earned.): Section 137(1) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires the operator, within 30 days after receiving a notice of cancellation under section 41(1), to pay the purchaser the greater of the income earned on the money and the income that would have been earned had it been deposited as required. The refund of all money received is separate from this additional amount. A fixed registrar rate does not apply.
  232. The correct answer is B (Within 30 days after the day the purchaser gives written notice of cancellation.): Section 138(2)(a) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires the purchaser to perform the purchaser's obligations under section 41(4) of the Act within 30 days after the day the purchaser gives the operator written notice of cancellation. The obligations must be performed during the operator's normal business hours. A demand from the operator does not change that deadline.
  233. The correct answer is D (Permit repossession, return the supplies, or deal with them as prescribed.): Section 41(4) of the Act says that if licensed supplies were delivered under a contract cancelled under section 41, the purchaser shall permit the supplies to be repossessed by the operator, return the supplies to the operator, or deal with them in the manner prescribed. Section 138 of the regulation sets out when return or repossession is not required. Keeping the supplies and waiting for an offer is not one of the choices in section 41(4).
  234. The correct answer is A (The lesser of 10 per cent of the money received plus income, and $350.): Section 140(2) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 allows the operator to deduct the lesser of two amounts: 10 per cent of the money received under the contract plus the additional income amounts under subsection (1), and $350. That figure is then reduced by any amount the operator already retained under section 78(1) as not being prepaid trust money. If the balance is not positive, no deduction is made at all.
  235. The correct answer is C (Nil, because the customization of the supply was completed.): Section 140(3)(a) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 states that the refund for a customized supply is nil if the customization of the supply has been completed at the time the contract is cancelled. Section 140(3)(b) applies the ordinary calculation when customization is not complete, less the expenses the operator incurred. A completed custom casket therefore gives rise to no refund for that supply.
  236. The correct answer is B (The recipient is or would be at least 120 years old.): Section 141(1)(a) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 uses a recipient who is, or if alive would be, at least 120 years old as the first condition for deeming the contract cancelled under section 44 of the Act. The other conditions are that none or only part of the supplies and services have been provided with no request for them, and that the operator cannot locate the recipient or purchaser after reasonable efforts. All of the conditions must be met.
  237. The correct answer is D (The operator must be unable to locate the recipient or purchaser after reasonable efforts.): Section 141(1)(c) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires that the operator is unable to locate the recipient or the purchaser after making reasonable efforts to do so. That condition sits alongside the age condition and the condition about supplies and services not being provided. The regulation does not require a newspaper notice or a registrar report.
  238. The correct answer is A (The recipient or the personal representative of the recipient.): Paragraph 2 of section 144(2) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 applies before the death of the recipient but after the death of the purchaser. In that situation the recipient or the recipient's personal representative may cancel the contract at any time, and the recipient is entitled to receive the refund under sections 41, 42, 43 and 44 of the Act. The paragraph expressly places the refund with the recipient.
  239. The correct answer is B (The recipient's personal representative, who is entitled to receive the refund.): Paragraph 3 of section 144(2) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 applies after the death of the recipient. In that situation the recipient's personal representative may cancel the contract at any time, and the recipient is entitled to receive the refund under sections 41, 42, 43 and 44 of the Act. The regulation does not give that right to a neighbour or to the funeral director.
  240. The correct answer is C (The name of the person who may cancel and the circumstances for cancelling.): Paragraph 15 of section 121(3) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires the contract to state the name of any person other than the purchaser who may cancel the contract and the circumstances under which the person may do so. A purchaser may transfer cancellation and refund rights under section 144 of the regulation. The contract must record who holds those rights and when they may be used.
  241. The correct answer is D (The operator may not charge the increase and may retain only prescribed amounts.): Section 38 of the Act applies when money is paid in advance under a contract and the price of the supplies or services increases before they are provided. In that case the operator shall not charge the purchaser any additional amount in respect of the increase and may retain only the amounts that are prescribed. The operator's higher costs do not change that rule.
  242. The correct answer is A (Cancel at any time by giving the operator written notice of cancellation.): Section 40(1)(b) requires the contract to set out the purchaser's cancellation rights under the Act, so a contract that omits them is not enforceable by the operator. Section 41(1) then allows the purchaser to cancel at any time after the contract is made by giving written notice. Section 41(2) requires the operator to refund all money received within 30 days of receiving the notice.
  243. The correct answer is C (The operator must make reasonable efforts and arrange a refund of the initial amount plus income.): Paragraph 3 of section 128 of Ontario Regulation 30/11 applies when the operator no longer provides a supply or service agreed to under the contract. The operator must have made reasonable efforts to provide it and, if unable to do so, must have arranged an appropriate refund of the initial amount paid plus the income earned on it. Keeping the money or substituting without telling the family does not satisfy the paragraph.
  244. The correct answer is B (The family keeps the cancellation rights under sections 41 and 42.): Section 43(5) of the Act provides that if the operator provides licensed supplies or services within 30 days after the day the contract is made and the regulations do not permit that provision, the purchaser continues to be entitled to the cancellation rights under sections 41 and 42 with respect to the supplies or services that are provided. Early delivery without authority does not remove those rights. The purchaser does not have to pay a restocking fee to keep them.
  245. The correct answer is D (Yes, because the thirty day period runs from May 20.): Section 42(1) gives the purchaser 30 days to cancel, counted from the day on which the last of the requirements described in subsection 40(1) is met. On these facts the last requirement is the delivery of the signed copy on May 20, so the cancellation window runs into late June. The family's June 10 request falls inside that window.
  246. The correct answer is A (Section 44(1) does not apply to that portion, and its own cancellation terms apply.): Section 44(2) states that subsection (1) does not apply to the portion of a contract relating to private structures or a private scattering ground, and the provisions of that portion relating to cancellation do apply. That means the further cancellation right in section 44(1) is not the route for the private mausoleum portion. The contract's own cancellation provisions govern that portion.
  247. The correct answer is C (The total purchase price may not exceed two hundred and fifty dollars.): Paragraph 3 of section 122(1) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires the total purchase price not to exceed $250. A price of $300 does not meet that condition, so the exemption is not available even though the item is delivered immediately and is not stored. All of the exemption conditions must be satisfied.
  248. The correct answer is B (The price list must be available without charge in printable form on the website.): Section 33(2) of the Act requires every licensee to make the price list available to the public in accordance with the regulations. Section 68(3) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires an operator who uses a website to promote or sell licensed supplies or services to make the price list available, without charge, in a printable form in a clearly visible place on the website. Mailing the list to every visitor and filing it with the registrar are not the requirements.
  249. The correct answer is A (A written contract signed by both parties that complies with the regulations.): Section 40(1)(a) says a contract for the provision of licensed supplies or services is not enforceable by an operator unless the contract is written, signed by both parties and complies with the regulations. A verbal quote, even with a promise of paperwork later, does not meet that clause. A price list signature or a registrar approval does not replace the written and signed contract.
  250. The correct answer is D (Not more than one person may be designated for any set of circumstances.): Section 144(1)(b) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 allows the purchaser to designate more than one person in the contract as long as not more than one person is designated for any set of circumstances or any point in time. Two friends with simultaneous rights would breach that limit. The registrar's consent and the operator's approval are not the conditions in section 144(1).

Practice Test 4 Answer Key

  1. The correct answer is A (When the regulations require the advance money to be held in trust): Subsection 52(1) says an operator who receives money for the sale of licensed supplies or services before providing them shall hold the money in trust if the regulations require it. The duty turns on the regulation, not on the size of the payment, a written request or the family's choice of casket. The operator should check the trust rules in Ontario Regulation 30/11 before deciding how to treat the payment.
  2. The correct answer is A (Any administrative fee paid and any disbursements the operator incurred are included): Subsection 52(2) says that money received by an operator for a sale of licensed supplies and services includes any money paid by the purchaser to cover an administrative fee or any disbursements the operator incurred. The planning fee and the courier charge therefore form part of the money received for the sale. They cannot be split off and treated as ordinary business income.
  3. The correct answer is B (The section does not apply to this payment unless the regulations provide otherwise): Subsection 52(3) removes money received by or on behalf of a cemetery operator from section 52 when the money must be paid into an account or fund under section 53. The care and maintenance rules in section 53 govern that money instead. The operator should not also route the same payment through the prepaid trust rules in section 52.
  4. The correct answer is B (Not later than the 35th day after the payment is received): Subsection 52(4) of the Act requires a licensee to pay trust money into a trust account within the prescribed time. Section 78(2) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 sets that time at not later than the 35th day after the day the operator receives the money. The deposit must go into an individual trust account that meets subsection 79(1) or a pooled trust fund that meets subsection 80(2).
  5. The correct answer is A (Payment may be made only in the manner that the regulations allow): Subsection 52(5) says money held in trust under section 52, and any income from that money, shall not be paid out except in accordance with the regulations. The operator cannot treat the interest as ordinary business income or use it for operating costs. A licensee who pays trust money out without following the regulation breaches the trust rules.
  6. The correct answer is D (The section does not apply when the refund is under a prescribed amount): Subsection 52(6) carves out a refund amount owing under a contract when the refund is smaller than the amount the regulations prescribe. The regulation therefore decides where the line falls for small refunds. A larger refund remains subject to the trust rules in section 52.
  7. The correct answer is C (Held separate and apart from all money that is not trust money): Subsection 76(1) requires every operator to hold all trust money separate and apart from all money other than trust money and to deal with it under Division F of the regulation. Keeping prepaid funds in the payroll and rent account breaks that rule even if a ledger tracks the amounts. The separation keeps the money available for the beneficiaries it belongs to.
  8. The correct answer is D (A capped amount, based on what the operator could keep on a cancellation): Subsection 78(1) allows an operator to hold money as prepaid trust money but permits the operator, on or after the 30th day after the contract is made, to retain as its own an amount that does not exceed what it would be entitled to under section 140 if the purchaser cancelled under subsection 44(1) of the Act. The election is capped and tied to the cancellation entitlement. The operator cannot keep the whole balance or the accumulated income.
  9. The correct answer is B (It may be paid only out of the income earned on the fund): Paragraph 3 of subsection 80(2) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 states that trustee fees for a pooled trust fund may be paid only out of the income earned on the fund. The capital belongs to the trust and cannot be used to cover the trustee's invoice. The operator also cannot pass the fee to purchasers as a separate charge.
  10. The correct answer is A (The lesser of the trust money held and the current listed selling price): Subsection 81(2) caps what an operator may retain despite anything in the contract at the lesser of the prepaid trust money plus any annuity or insurance proceeds, and the current selling price for the supplies and services when they are provided, as set out in the price list maintained under section 33 of the Act. If the item is not on the price list, the price for a similar supply or service is used. The operator cannot collect the higher current price when the trust money is lower.
  11. The correct answer is A (Compensating a person who suffers a financial loss caused by a licensee's failure): Subsection 61(2) states that the purpose of a prescribed compensation fund is to compensate a person who suffers a financial loss because a licensee failed to comply with the Act, the regulations or the terms of an agreement made under the Act. The fund is not a subsidy for a business and it does not pay a licensee's defence costs. Subsection 61(3) requires a licensee to make the contributions to the fund that the regulations require, at the times and in the manner prescribed.
  12. The correct answer is C ($250, paid at the time that the applicant applies for the operator licence): Subsection 198(3) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 sets an initial payment of $250 into the Fund when a participant applies for a licence. Subsection 198(2) makes every operator of a funeral establishment and every transfer service operator a participant. Subsection 198(4) allows the Committee to require further payments if the book value of the Fund falls below $1,000,000.
  13. The correct answer is D (Compliance with the code is a condition of every licence under the Act): Section 62 states that it is a condition of every licence issued under the Act that a licensee comply with the code of ethics established by the Minister under section 112. Because compliance is a condition of the licence, the code applies to personal licensees as well as operators. The code is set by regulation and can be enforced against the classes of licensees to which it applies.
  14. The correct answer is D (The Minister, by regulation, for the licensees or classes named in it): Clause 112(1)(a) lets the Minister make regulations establishing one or more codes of ethics for the purposes of section 62 and deciding which licensees or classes of licensees each code covers. The discipline committee applies the code; it does not write it. The registrar and the Lieutenant Governor in Council are not the bodies named in that clause.
  15. The correct answer is B (A fine of up to $50,000, or up to two years less a day): Subsection 79(3) makes an individual who is convicted of an offence liable to a fine of not more than $50,000 or to imprisonment for a term of not more than two years less a day, or both. A corporation faces a fine of not more than $250,000. The $25,000 figure belongs to the discipline committee's fine power under subsection 64(2), not to an offence conviction.
  16. The correct answer is B (Leave the money in trust and arrange other funds for the payroll instead): Subsection 52(1) and section 78 of Ontario Regulation 30/11 require a prepaid payment to be held in trust, and section 76(1) requires trust money to be kept separate from all other money. Subsection 52(4) requires the licensee to deposit the money into a trust account within the prescribed time. Using it for payroll is a breach that can lead to discipline and prosecution, even if the money is replaced later.
  17. The correct answer is A (Deposit the money by June 5, the 35th day after the contract was made): Subsection 78(4) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 deals with a contract for supplies or services to be provided within 30 days after the contract is made. If they are not provided within that 30 day window, the operator must deposit the money into an individual trust account or pooled trust fund no later than the 35th day after the contract was made. The 35th day after May 1 is June 5.
  18. The correct answer is D (Pay the leftover money to the purchaser or the person named by the contract): Paragraph 2 of subsection 81(1) says that prepaid trust money remaining after the supplies or services are provided, and after all amounts payable to the operator and other suppliers are paid, must be paid to the purchaser or to the person the contract designates. Paragraph 3 sends the money to the compensation fund only when the operator cannot identify or locate that person. The operator does not keep a surplus as a credit or share it.
  19. The correct answer is D (To a compensation fund set up to hold and invest unclaimed trust money): Subsection 82(1) applies when the operator cannot identify or locate the purchaser or the designated person by the end of the year after the contract is completed or cancelled. The operator must pay the unclaimed trust money to a compensation fund established under subsection 61(1) that has the holding and investment of unclaimed money as one of its purposes, or to the Minister of Finance if that fund does not exist. Subsection 82(4) requires the fund to pay the money to a person who later proves entitlement on a balance of probabilities.
  20. The correct answer is B (The operator is exempt from the forwarding rule for amounts under $50): Subsection 82(5) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 exempts an operator from the unclaimed money rule when the amount is less than $50. The exemption is narrow and turns on the size of the amount only. For $50 or more, subsection 82(1) requires the money to go to the compensation fund or the Minister of Finance.
  21. The correct answer is A (Audited statements on that trust account and information about the account): Subsection 58(1) lets the registrar or the Public Guardian and Trustee require a licensee or trustee to provide audited financial statements on any trust account or trust fund that the Act requires and any information about those accounts or funds. Subsection 58(2) requires the licensee or trustee to provide the statements or information promptly. The power is not limited to cemetery operators or to a particular calendar period.
  22. The correct answer is C (Apply to the Superior Court of Justice to pass the accounts of the trust): Subsection 59(1) says that, on a written direction from the registrar or the Public Guardian and Trustee, a person who must establish a trust fund or hold money in trust shall apply to the Superior Court of Justice to pass the accounts. The court may review any agreement made by a licensee and may make any order needed to carry out the trust. The licensee does not substitute an internal review or a transfer of the money for the court process.
  23. The correct answer is C (A licensee may not charge the purchaser for setting up or keeping the trust): Section 60 states that no licensee shall charge a purchaser of licensed supplies or services, or receive compensation or payment from the purchaser, for establishing or maintaining a trust fund or account. A contract clause does not create an exception to that rule. The cost of running the trust is a business expense of the licensee.
  24. The correct answer is C (In the operator's licensed name, as trustee for the purchaser of those supplies): Paragraph 1 of subsection 79(1) requires an individual trust account to be established in the name of the operator as it appears on its licence, as trustee for the purchaser. Paragraph 2 limits the account to trust money from contracts with the same purchaser for the same recipient. Paragraph 3 makes the operator the trustee for the money in the account.
  25. The correct answer is D (The current value, the types of investments held and the depositary's name): Subsection 79(2) requires an operator, if the purchaser asks, to provide a statement once a year for each individual trust account. The statement must set out the current value of the account as of the end of the month before the request, the types of investments held, and the name of the eligible depositary for any account holding the prepaid trust money. The statement is not a profit report or a price forecast.
  26. The correct answer is B (They must let the operator track and report the money contract by contract): Subsection 80(1) allows an operator to hold prepaid trust money in a pooled trust fund only if the records maintained for the fund permit tracking and reporting on the funds on an individual contract basis. A single combined balance is not enough because each purchaser's share must be identifiable. The rule protects each purchaser's money when the fund is large.
  27. The correct answer is D (Deposit it with an eligible depositary, in the licensed name, and not use it): Subsection 76(2) applies when prepaid trust money is temporarily deposited somewhere other than an individual trust account or a pooled trust fund. The operator must use an eligible depositary, hold the account in the name shown on the licence, preserve the money for the beneficiary and maintain adequate account controls. A personal account or the office safe does not meet those conditions.
  28. The correct answer is A (Every funeral establishment operator and every transfer service operator in Ontario): Subsection 198(2) makes every operator of a funeral establishment and every transfer service operator a participant in the Fund. Participation does not depend on holding prepaid contracts or on having a claim made in the past. The duty applies to both classes of operator named in the subsection.
  29. The correct answer is C (Within six months of the date they could reasonably have known payment was due): Subsection 202(3) says a claimant may make a claim within six months of the date the person could reasonably have been expected to know that payment was due. Subsection 202(6) allows the Committee to extend the time if the circumstances warrant, and that decision is final. The six month clock is tied to knowledge that the payment was due, not to the date of the funeral or the contract.
  30. The correct answer is C (By giving written notice of the claim to the registrar for the Committee): Subsection 202(4) says a claimant makes a claim by giving written notice to the registrar, who gives a copy of the claim to the Committee. The Committee then determines eligibility and the amount of the claim under subsection 202(7). A court action against the funeral home is a separate step from the fund claim.
  31. The correct answer is C (A maximum of $40,000 for the transaction, not counting any legal costs): Subsection 202(9) says the Trustee shall not pay more than $40,000, exclusive of costs, to each claimant in respect of each transaction for one or more claims against any one participant. Subsection 202(11) reduces that maximum by any amount the claimant recovers from another source. The $25,000 figure belongs to the discipline committee's fine power and not to the Fund.
  32. The correct answer is A (Mail or deliver a written request for a hearing within 15 days after service): Subsection 203(2) requires the Committee's notice to tell the claimant that a hearing by the Tribunal may be requested by mailing or delivering a written notice to the registrar and the Tribunal within 15 days after the Committee's notice is served. Subsection 203(3) makes the Committee's decision final if no hearing is requested. The request must be in writing and must go to both the registrar and the Tribunal.
  33. The correct answer is B (To hear and determine whether licensees have failed to comply with the code): Subsection 63(1) requires the Minister to establish one or more discipline committees, in accordance with the regulations, to hear and determine issues about whether licensees have failed to comply with a code of ethics established under section 112. Subsection 63(2) provides for appeal committees that consider appeals from the discipline committees. Inspections and licence application reviews belong to other parts of the Act.
  34. The correct answer is B (To the appeal committee, which may overturn, affirm or modify that order): Subsection 64(3) lets a party to the discipline proceeding appeal the decision of the discipline committee to the appropriate appeal committee. Subsection 64(4) allows the appeal committee to overturn, affirm or modify the order and to make an order described in subsection 64(2), which includes a fine of up to $25,000. The appeal committee's order may itself impose one of the penalties listed in subsection 64(2).
  35. The correct answer is D (On or before the 60th day after the date of the discipline order): Subsection 64(6) says a fine imposed by a discipline committee or an appeal committee must be paid on or before the day named in the order or, if no day is named, on or before the 60th day after the date of the order. If the fine is under appeal, the clock runs from the appeal committee's order instead. The same 60 day default applies in both cases.
  36. The correct answer is D (Receive the complaint, make inquiries and attempt to mediate or resolve the dispute): Subsection 66(1) lets the registrar receive complaints about conduct that may contravene a requirement under the Act, make inquiries, gather information or make written requests for information, and attempt to mediate or resolve a complaint. Subsection 66(2) requires any inquiry or written request to indicate the nature of the complaint. A court action by the family is not a precondition to the registrar acting.
  37. The correct answer is A (Provide the requested information to the registrar in a timely way): Subsection 66(2) requires any inquiry or written request made by the registrar to indicate the nature of the complaint. Subsection 66(3) requires a person who receives that inquiry or request to provide the requested information to the registrar. The duty does not wait for a court order, and sending the request to a lawyer does not discharge it.
  38. The correct answer is A (Refer the matter, either in whole or in part, to a discipline committee): Paragraph 3 of subsection 66(4) allows the registrar, on forming the opinion that a person has contravened a requirement under the Act, to refer the matter in whole or in part to a discipline committee. Paragraph 1 covers a written warning, paragraph 2 covers an educational program for a licensee, and paragraph 4 covers action under section 17. The referral is the step that puts the alleged breach of the code of ethics before a discipline committee.
  39. The correct answer is C (The registrar or a designated person, to check compliance or a complaint): Subsection 67(1) allows the registrar, or a person designated in writing by the registrar, to conduct an inspection to check compliance with a requirement under the Act, to deal with a complaint under section 66, or to confirm that a licensee or licence applicant is entitled to be licensed. Subsection 67(2) then allows the inspector to enter and inspect business premises at any reasonable time without a warrant or court order. A family member or a municipal employee has no inspection power under this section.
  40. The correct answer is B (Produce it within the time set and help make it readable for the inspector): Subsection 67(8) requires the person who has custody of anything demanded by an inspector to produce it within the time set out in the demand. On the inspector's demand, the person must also give whatever help is reasonably necessary to produce information in a readable form and to interpret a record. A written demand must state the nature of the thing required and when it is to be produced.
  41. The correct answer is D (The prepaid funeral payment, because section 52 excludes the care fund money): Subsection 52(1) applies to money received for licensed supplies or services in advance of their provision when the regulations require trust. Subsection 52(3) removes money received by or on behalf of a cemetery operator that must be paid into an account or fund under section 53. The care and maintenance payment follows section 53, while the prepaid funeral payment follows section 52 and Division F of Ontario Regulation 30/11.
  42. The correct answer is C (A code of ethics breach and a section 64 order are excluded from the offence): Clause 79(1)(b) makes it an offence to contravene or fail to comply with a requirement under the Act, but it expressly excludes a code of ethics established by the Minister under section 112 and an order made under section 64. A breach of the code is dealt with through the discipline process. The exclusion keeps the discipline route from being duplicated by a prosecution for the same code breach.
  43. The correct answer is C (The appeal committee may make an order described in subsection 64(2), including costs): Subsection 64(4) allows an appeal committee to overturn, affirm or modify the order of the discipline committee and to make an order described in subsection 64(2). Those orders include an educational requirement, a fine of up to $25,000, a suspension or postponement of a requirement, and costs. The appeal committee is not limited to confirming or overturning the original decision.
  44. The correct answer is A (Require the operator to arrange and fund educational programs for its employees): Paragraph 2 of subsection 64(2) allows a discipline committee, when the licensee is an operator, to require the operator to fund educational programs for any person employed by the operator, or to arrange and fund those programs, on the terms the committee specifies. Paragraph 1 covers attendance at a specified program, and paragraph 3 covers a fine of up to $25,000. The committee does not close a business or suspend staff as punishment under this section.
  45. The correct answer is B (The registrar may act whether the information comes from a complaint or otherwise): Subsection 66(4) applies if the registrar is of the opinion, whether as a result of a complaint or otherwise, that a person has contravened a requirement under the Act. The registrar may then give a written warning, require an educational program, refer the matter to a discipline committee, take an action under section 17, or take further action. A consumer complaint is one route to that opinion, not a precondition.
  46. The correct answer is D (That the loss has not already been fully compensated from another source): Subsection 202(1) entitles a person to payment from the Fund only if the person makes a claim and satisfies the Committee that the person suffered a financial loss and has not otherwise been fully compensated. A claimant who has already recovered the whole loss from an insurer has no unpaid loss for the Fund to cover. The Fund is not a second payment for the same loss.
  47. The correct answer is D (The inspector may take the binder on a receipt and must return it reasonably soon): Paragraph 5 of subsection 67(5) lets an inspector remove anything relevant for review, examination, testing or copying after giving a receipt. Subsection 67(9) requires anything removed to be made available to the person it was taken from on request and to be returned within a reasonable time. Keeping the binder for four months without leaving a copy does not match that return duty.
  48. The correct answer is C (No person shall obstruct an inspector or destroy any record relevant to the inspection): Subsection 67(11) says that no person shall obstruct an inspector conducting an inspection, or a person accompanying the inspector, or withhold, conceal, alter or destroy anything or information relevant to the inspection. Deleting the ledger would breach that subsection and could also support an offence under the Act. The funeral home's ownership of the records does not create an exception.
  49. The correct answer is C (A fine of not more than $250,000, which is the corporate maximum): Subsection 79(3) makes a corporation that is convicted of an offence liable to a fine of not more than $250,000. An individual faces a fine of not more than $50,000 or imprisonment for not more than two years less a day, or both. Subsection 79(2) separately makes an officer or director guilty of an offence if the officer or director fails to take reasonable care to prevent the corporation's offence.
  50. The correct answer is B (No proceeding may start more than two years after the director learns of the facts): Subsection 79(4) says no proceeding under section 79 shall be commenced more than two years after the facts on which the proceeding is based first came to the knowledge of the director. The clock starts with the director's knowledge, not with the date of the contravention. A file that sits for more than two years after the director learns of the facts cannot support a late prosecution.
  51. The correct answer is A (Cemetery Operator licence, which authorizes the licensee to operate a cemetery.): Section 18 prescribes Cemetery Operator as one of the ten licence classes. The other three names are not classes created by section 18. The prescribed classes cover cemetery, crematorium, funeral establishment and transfer service operators, along with funeral directors, sales representatives and funeral preplanners.
  52. The correct answer is C (Preparing a dead human body and placing it into a casket.): Section 1 defines a holding room as a room designated for holding dead human bodies, preparing them and placing them in caskets. The same definition excludes arterial embalming and restoring from a holding room. Those services belong in an embalming room.
  53. The correct answer is B (The name of the operator together with the other name in use.): Section 15(1) requires an operator licence to set out the name of the operator. Section 15(2) adds that if the operator carries on business under another name permitted by law, the licence must also set out that other name. The operator's address, owner and licence number are not the names the section addresses.
  54. The correct answer is A (An individual who manages the operator's business at a particular location.): Section 1 defines the person in charge of the day-to-day operations as an individual managing the business of the operator at a particular location. A director who signs an application, a major shareholder and a manager spread across several sites do not fit that wording. The phrase ties to one specific location.
  55. The correct answer is A (Completion of the education that section 42 requires for the role.): Section 22(1) requires the operator to make sure that the person in charge of the day-to-day operations at each licensed location has completed the education required as a result of section 42. Living nearby, having management experience or holding a particular class of director licence is not what the subsection asks for.
  56. The correct answer is C (The person may be in charge of no more than one business location.): Section 22(3) provides that, unless the registrar consents, one person is not in charge of the day-to-day operations of more than one business or at more than one business location. The rule applies to Funeral Establishment Operator Class 1 and Class 2 and Transfer Service Operator Class 1 and Class 2 licences. A limit based on years or on the owner's presence does not appear in the section.
  57. The correct answer is B (That a licensed funeral director reviews and signs the negotiated contract.): Section 32(3) requires the operator to make sure that a contract negotiated by a person who is enrolled in a funeral director or funeral preplanner education program and is not licensed gets reviewed and signed by a licensed funeral director. The student may take part in the discussion, but the review and signature must come from a licensee. Cancelling the contract or seeking the instructor's approval is not what the section requires.
  58. The correct answer is D (A person who holds a Funeral Director Class 1 licence in Ontario.): Section 33(3) normally lets a Class 1 or Class 2 funeral director manage a Funeral Establishment Operator Class 1 business. Section 33(4) raises that requirement when the establishment provides arterial embalming or restoring, because the day-to-day operations must then be managed by a Funeral Director Class 1. A Class 2 director, a preplanner or an educated employee does not meet the higher condition.
  59. The correct answer is A (Premises where human remains are temporarily placed so people may pay respects.): Section 34(3) bars a Class 2 operator from offering or providing a transfer service or any funeral services except maintaining premises used to temporarily place human remains so that people may attend and pay their respects. Section 34(5) confirms that this use is a funeral service. Transfer, embalming and full funeral arranging belong to other licence classes.
  60. The correct answer is C (Later than 72 hours after death or removal from refrigeration.): Section 35(4) provides that a transfer service operator shall not transport a dead human body that has not been arterially embalmed later than 72 hours after the death or the removal of the body from refrigeration. The 24, 48 and 96 hour choices do not match the regulation. The clock runs from death or from removal from refrigeration, whichever applies.
  61. The correct answer is B (Washing a dead human body and applying a disinfectant externally.): Section 36(4) allows a Transfer Service Operator Class 1 to sell and provide the services of placing dead human bodies in caskets or caring for and preparing them, including washing with an external disinfectant and setting the features. The same subsection excludes arterial embalming and restoring. Pacemaker removal is governed by a separate provision.
  62. The correct answer is D (Removing personal effects, placing the body in a casket, and transporting it.): Section 37(2) requires a Transfer Service Operator Class 2 to make sure that no employee has contact with the dead human body except to remove personal effects, place the body in a casket and transport the body. Washing and setting features go beyond that limit for this class. Directing a visitation is also beyond what this class may do.
  63. The correct answer is A (A current home address and the name and address of the primary operator.): Section 38(2) requires a personal licensee to make sure the registrar is given a current address for the licensee's place of residence and the name and address of the primary operator that employs the licensee. A lease, a new application or an operator consent is not what the subsection asks for. The duty sits with the licensee, not the employer.
  64. The correct answer is C (An individual licensed to act for an operator on contracts made before the death.): Section 1 defines a funeral preplanner as an individual who, in respect of contracts made before the death of the intended recipient of supplies and services, is licensed to act under subsection 6(1) on behalf of a person licensed as a Funeral Establishment Operator Class 1 or Class 2, or who is licensed to hold oneself out as available to do so. The role is tied to contracts made before the death. Embalming, managing a business location and selling cemetery supplies are different duties.
  65. The correct answer is B (Exempt the applicant from some of the educational requirements and impose conditions.): Section 42(6) allows the registrar to exempt an applicant or licensee who holds or held an equivalent licence, registration or certification outside Ontario from any of the educational requirements set by the registrar, and to impose conditions on that exemption. The provision does not promise a full exemption or a reduced fee. It also does not replace the exemption with a residency or supervision term.
  66. The correct answer is B (A person licensed as a Funeral Director Class 1 supervising the student.): Section 4(1)(b) lets a student enrolled in an approved education program embalm while working under the supervision and in the presence of the course instructor, or under the supervision of a person licensed as a Funeral Director Class 1. The presence requirement applies to the instructor branch, and the second branch names the Class 1 director. An establishment owner, any funeral director or the registrar's designate does not fit either branch.
  67. The correct answer is A (Embalming bodies at the faculty of medicine or school of anatomy.): Section 4(1)(c) exempts a person employed in a faculty of medicine or a school of anatomy designated under the Anatomy Act from the licence requirement for embalming. The exemption is tied to that teaching setting. It does not extend to a funeral establishment across the province, and it does not depend on a director signing a record or on passing the provincial examination.
  68. The correct answer is D (A sales representative licence before acting or holding out as one.): Section 5(1) prohibits a person from acting as a sales representative on behalf of an operator, or holding out as one, unless the person is licensed as a sales representative. Section 1 defines a sales representative as an individual, other than a funeral preplanner, who is licensed to act under section 5(1) on behalf of an operator other than a funeral establishment operator. A note from the employer or short term registrar approval is not a licence.
  69. The correct answer is C (The student must work under the supervision of a licensed funeral director.): Section 6(1)(c) allows an enrolled student to sell funeral supplies and services on behalf of a Funeral Establishment Operator Class 1 or Class 2 while employed by that operator and working under the supervision of a Funeral Director Class 1 or Class 2. The student does not need a Class 2 director licence of their own. A limit based on the business location or on past customers is not part of the condition.
  70. The correct answer is B (Written authorization to act as a sales representative for that operator.): Section 6(4) makes a funeral director's licence a prescribed licence for the purposes of clause 6(2)(a) of the Act if the funeral director has received written authorization to act as a sales representative on behalf of a crematorium operator. A separate preplanner licence is not the mechanism described. The other operator's class and the cemetery's approval are not what the subsection requires.
  71. The correct answer is D (Carrying on the business authorized by the licence under an unapproved name.): Section 15(4) states that an operator shall not carry on the business authorized by the licence under a name other than a name set out in the licence. Section 15(3) adds that the operator must keep the registrar current about the names described in subsections (1) and (2). Registering a name with the province or gaining municipal approval does not place it on the licence.
  72. The correct answer is C (Make sure the registrar holds current information about the operator's names.): Section 15(3) requires an operator to make sure the registrar has current information about the operator's names described in subsections (1) and (2). The duty rests on the operator and does not wait for a request from the registrar. A licence of the same class stays in place; the names on it are updated.
  73. The correct answer is A (An operator may not operate from a location not identified on the licence.): Section 21(1) provides that an operator shall not operate from a location other than a location identified on the licence. Section 21(2) adds a related limit on inviting the public to enter into contracts elsewhere. Municipal approval or a notice to the public does not add a location to the licence.
  74. The correct answer is B (The operator may enter the contract at a location that the purchaser requests.): Section 21(2) bars an operator from inviting the public to enter into contracts at a location other than the business location identified on the licence. Section 21(3) states that this does not prohibit the operator from entering into a contract with a purchaser at a location requested by the purchaser. Registrar approval and a family waiver are not part of the exception.
  75. The correct answer is C (This person is not required to complete the education in these circumstances.): Section 22(2) excuses the person in charge at a cemetery that is not a commercial cemetery from the education required as a result of section 42 unless the cemetery operator holds or is required to hold money in trust under section 52 of the Act, or employs a sales representative to work in respect of the cemetery. Neither condition applies here. The size of the staff does not affect the rule.
  76. The correct answer is D (The geographic separation of the locations and the volume of business.): Section 22(4) lets the registrar consider the geographic separation of locations, the volume of business at each location or business, the knowledge and experience of the person who would be in charge, the compliance history of the operators and the number of persons employed. Salary, lease length, the manager's history in the region and the commute are not listed. The list guides how the registrar decides whether to consent under section 22(3).
  77. The correct answer is B (Charging a fee for a licensed supply not purchased from the operator.): Section 24(1) states that no operator shall charge a fee in relation to a licensed supply or service not purchased from the operator. A charge for storing a casket, for a graveside service or for staff preparation time relates to services the operator itself provides. The prohibition targets a fee attached to an item the family bought elsewhere.
  78. The correct answer is A (Requiring a person to pay for access to the operator's premises.): Section 24(2) states that no operator shall require a person, including a person delivering supplies, to pay for access to the operator's premises. Charging a supplier for goods the operator bought, setting delivery hours and asking suppliers to book ahead are not access fees. The prohibition protects anyone who needs to enter the premises.
  79. The correct answer is C (Charging for arranging or negotiating a contract for licensed supplies.): Section 24(4) provides that no operator shall charge for providing advice about, or for arranging or negotiating, a contract for the purchase of licensed supplies or services by a purchaser from the operator or from another operator. Section 24(5) preserves a charge for design work on a customized supply when the purchaser requests it. Charges for preparation time and chapel use are not covered by this prohibition.
  80. The correct answer is D (When the purchaser asks for design services for a customized casket supply.): Section 24(4) bars a charge for arranging or negotiating a contract, but section 24(5) states that the prohibition does not apply to charges for the time spent providing design services for a customized supply or private structure at the purchaser's request. The exception is tied to the purchaser's request and to design work. Advertising the service, signing the contract or obtaining registrar approval does not create the exception.
  81. The correct answer is A (Presenting for cremation a body that could not lawfully be cremated.): Section 27 states that no licensee shall present a dead human body for cremation that could not be cremated by reason of clause 31(2)(b), which covers a body with a pacemaker or radioactive implant and a casket containing prohibited material. Removing a pacemaker is allowed when the person doing the removal is described in section 52. Telling the family about the delay or dealing with the coroner's certificate is not what section 27 prohibits.
  82. The correct answer is B (The date, manner and place where the remains were scattered.): Paragraph 3 of section 28(1) requires the operator to prepare a record whenever cremated human remains are received, listing the name of the deceased person, the date the operator received the remains, the name and address of the person who authorized the scattering and the date, manner and place of scattering. The crematorium's licence number and the service price are not part of that record. The identity of the funeral director is also not required by the paragraph.
  83. The correct answer is D (Provide the record free of charge to the person who asks.): Paragraph 4 of section 28(1) requires the operator to disclose the record free of charge if the purchaser, the personal representative or a family member of the deceased person requests it. The record is the one prepared under paragraph 3 for the remains received. A copying fee, a summary and a registrar request are not part of the rule.
  84. The correct answer is C (The conditions do not apply to this operator in these circumstances.): Section 28(2) states that subsection (1) does not apply to a cemetery operator in respect of cremated human remains that the operator receives for scattering in the cemetery and scatters in the cemetery. Those detailed conditions cover storage, co-mingling, records and disclosure for operators that scatter for consideration elsewhere. A family request, a common lot and a written record do not change the subsection (2) exception.
  85. The correct answer is A (Inspection of the storage facility by the registrar during business hours.): Section 28(3) requires an operator who stores cremated human remains with another person to enter into a contract requiring that person to comply with the operator's duties under subsection (1) and to make the storage facility available during regular business hours for inspection by the registrar or a person designated in writing by the registrar. Storage fees, family reports and insurance are not required by the subsection. The inspection right supports the operator's duty to store the remains respectfully.
  86. The correct answer is D (Immediate access to an embalming room that the registrar has approved.): Section 33(4) applies when a Funeral Establishment Operator Class 1 provides arterial embalming or restoring. The operator must make sure the day-to-day operations are managed by a Funeral Director Class 1 and must have immediate access to an embalming room approved by the registrar that meets section 48. A holding room under section 47 and a vehicle under section 51 are separate conditions in subsections (2) and (5), and a service agreement with another establishment is not what the subsection requires.
  87. The correct answer is B (An inexpensive transfer service for families in the area that is served.): Section 33(6) requires a Funeral Establishment Operator Class 1 to make available an inexpensive transfer service. Section 36(3) places the same duty on a Transfer Service Operator Class 1. Operating a crematorium, scattering remains for free and opening a holding room to the public are not duties created by section 33(6).
  88. The correct answer is C (A vehicle for transporting bodies and a holding room approved by the registrar.): Section 35(2) requires a transfer service operator to have immediate access to a vehicle for transporting dead human bodies that meets the requirements of section 51 and immediate access to a holding room that the registrar has approved and that meets the requirements of section 47. An embalming room is not one of the transfer service conditions in that subsection. Providing a funeral director or operating a crematorium are separate matters.
  89. The correct answer is A (A person licensed as a Funeral Director Class 1 in Ontario.): Section 36(2) requires a Transfer Service Operator Class 1 to make sure that the day-to-day operations of the transfer service are managed by a person licensed as a Funeral Director Class 1. This differs from a Funeral Establishment Operator Class 1 or Class 2, where section 33(3) or section 34(2) allows a Class 1 or Class 2 director. A Class 2 director, a preplanner or a sales representative does not meet section 36(2).
  90.
