The correct answer is B (From the hospital directly to a holding room and then to the crematorium.): Section 37(4) prohibits a Transfer Service Operator Class 2 from transporting a dead human body except from the place from which removal was first requested directly to a cemetery or crematorium, or to a holding room that meets section 47 and then to a cemetery or crematorium. A stop at the operator's garage or a family home falls outside the permitted route. Handing the body to another transfer service is not part of the route the subsection describes.
  91. The correct answer is D (Neither worker, because the service requires a Funeral Director Class 1.): Section 40(b) states that a condition of every Funeral Director Class 2 licence is that the director shall not provide the services of arterially embalming or restoring dead human bodies. Section 4(1) sets out who may embalm, and section 39 confirms that a Class 1 director may provide the service only when acting for an operator holding a Funeral Establishment Operator Class 1 licence. Supervision by a Class 2 director does not cure the limit.
  92. The correct answer is A (Premises used only occasionally for that purpose are not included in the condition.): Section 34(4) excludes two kinds of premises from the Class 2 condition in subsection (3): premises whose primary functions do not include temporarily placing human remains for people to attend and pay respects, but which are occasionally used for that purpose, and premises identified as premises of a Funeral Establishment Operator Class 1. The hall in this scenario fits the first exclusion. Charity ownership, the number of events and a rental agreement are not the test.
  93. The correct answer is C (The operator may transport the body to and from the funeral location.): Section 36(6) bars a Transfer Service Operator Class 1 from arranging, co-ordinating, conducting or participating in a visitation, funeral or other rite or ceremony at which human remains are present, except to transport the remains to or from a holding room or the location of a visitation, cremation, funeral or other rite or ceremony, or for scattering authorized under section 2. Driving the body to the church and on to the crematorium is transport. Staying to arrange, direct or take part in the service goes beyond the exception.
  94. The correct answer is B (Stop the work, because employees may only move and transport bodies.): Section 37(2) requires a Transfer Service Operator Class 2 to make sure that no employee has contact with the dead human body except to remove personal effects, place the body in a casket and transport the body. Washing, setting the features and dressing the body fall outside that limit. Training, a family request or the promise of a future licence change does not authorize the work now.
  95. The correct answer is D (The representative may not sell services that the employing operator cannot sell.): Section 38(4) is a condition of every personal licence: the licensee shall not offer to sell, sell or provide any licensed supplies or services that the operator by whom the licensee is employed is not authorized to offer to sell, sell and provide. A cemetery operator is not authorized to provide funeral services at a funeral establishment. The representative's authority cannot exceed the employer's authority, and notice to the registrar or a director's signature does not change that.
  96. The correct answer is B (Require the director to complete the specified education again before any renewal.): Section 42(7) allows the registrar to require an applicant or licensee to complete again any of the educational requirements set by the registrar, or to complete other educational requirements the registrar specifies, if the person completed the original requirements for a licence under the Act or a predecessor Act and then had a period during which the person did not carry on the activities authorized by the licence. The subsection gives the registrar a power to specify education, not an automatic internship or a refusal. A prior Ontario licence does not waive the requirement on its own.
  97. The correct answer is A (When the ceremony is near the time of death with the remains present.): Section 1(3) brings the co-ordination and provision of rites and ceremonies with respect to cremated human remains into the definition of funeral services when the services are provided in close proximity to the time of death of the person whose remains they are and the remains are to be present. The subsection excludes rites or ceremonies taking place at the time of interment or scattering. A separate fee and the location of the ceremony are not part of the test.
  98. The correct answer is C (No, because the person doing the removal must be described in section 52.): Section 33(7) allows a Funeral Establishment Operator Class 1 to contract out or arrange for the removal of a pacemaker or radioactive implant only if the person carrying out the removal is a person described in section 52. Section 52 describes a Funeral Director Class 1 acting on behalf of an operator that holds a Funeral Establishment Operator Class 1 or Transfer Service Operator Class 1 licence, or a legally qualified medical practitioner. Experience alone does not qualify the technician, and the crematorium operator is not required to do the work.
  99. The correct answer is D (The operator must arrange for a funeral director to provide those preparation services.): Section 36(5) provides that if a Transfer Service Operator Class 1 provides the services permitted under subsection (4), the operator shall make sure that a funeral director provides the services. Washing bodies and setting the features fall within the permitted list in section 36(4), but the work itself must be done by a funeral director. Neither family consent nor supervision by the operator's own staff replaces that requirement.
  100. The correct answer is A (The Class 1 location needs a Funeral Director Class 1 managing its operations.): Section 33(4) provides that when a Funeral Establishment Operator Class 1 provides arterial embalming or restoring, the day-to-day operations must be managed by a Funeral Director Class 1. Section 34(2) allows the Class 2 location to be managed by either a Class 1 or a Class 2 director. Moving one manager between the locations does not change either condition.
  101. The correct answer is B (At least two types chosen from the least expensive caskets available.): Section 43 (a) requires at least two types of caskets with either or both fixed interior linings and ornamentation. Those two types must come from among the least expensive available on the market. The operator cannot satisfy the rule by choosing mid range or high priced models.
  102. The correct answer is D (A lining installed to prevent leakage from the body.): Section 43 (b) permits a lining that prevents leakage even though the casket is not ornamented and does not have a fixed interior lining. A satin lid lining, a fixed lining and a decorative quilted lining all fall outside that exception. The purpose is to allow protection from fluids without defeating the low priced option.
  103. The correct answer is B (A colour image of each casket that the operator offers.): Section 44 (2) (b) requires a colour image of the casket. A warranty, a dimension sketch and a fabric list do not replace that image. The image must accompany the price and the identifying details for each casket.
  104. The correct answer is B (Include a display model for each casket type that section 43 requires.): Section 44 (3) says that if display models are used, a similar display model must be included for each type of casket that section 43 requires the operator to offer. The rule keeps the less expensive choices visible on the same floor. Photographs, warehouse lists and a broader display of every casket sold do not meet that condition.
  105. The correct answer is A (Arterially embalming or restoring the body in the holding room.): Section 47, paragraph 3 ii, requires equipment to prepare bodies, including cleaning, sanitizing and disinfecting them. That paragraph expressly excludes arterially embalming or restoring them. A holding room therefore cannot be equipped and used as an embalming or restoration space.
  106. The correct answer is C (It must be located in premises named as business premises on the licence.): Section 48, paragraph 1, requires the embalming room to be located in premises that a Funeral Establishment Operator Class 1 licence indicates are business premises of the funeral establishment. A building that is not on the licence does not qualify. Distance from public areas and municipal zoning approval are separate matters.
  107. The correct answer is A (A funeral director with a Class 1 licence serving the operator.): Section 52 (a) permits a person who holds a Funeral Director Class 1 licence and is acting on behalf of an operator that holds a Funeral Establishment Operator Class 1 or Transfer Service Operator Class 1 licence. The stem confirms the operator holds a Transfer Service Operator Class 1 licence. A Class 2 funeral director, a sales representative and an embalmer do not fall within that clause.
  108. The correct answer is B (One year from the date the remains were cremated at the crematorium.): Section 53 (2) allows interment when cremated human remains are not claimed within one year from the date of cremation. The operator must also have made reasonable efforts to contact the purchaser, the personal representative or a family member. The remains may then be interred in a cemetery, including a common lot.
  109. The correct answer is B (Whether the listed price includes the applicable taxes or has them added.): Section 55 (2) (c) requires a statement that the price includes the applicable taxes or that the taxes are in addition to the price. The purchaser must be able to tell from the list what will actually be paid. Cash discounts, registrar approval and nearby market prices are not part of that requirement.
  110. The correct answer is D (That the supply is offered for rent as well as for sale.): Section 55 (3) requires the price list to indicate whether a particular supply is available for rent. The customer can then compare renting with buying. Deposits, staff names and replacement values are not the item that subsection (3) requires.
  111. The correct answer is C (The effective date on which the price list takes effect.): Section 55 (6) requires the price list to be always up to date and to indicate its effective date. A price list without an effective date cannot show when its prices began to apply. Registrar approval, print counts and destruction dates are not required.
  112. The correct answer is C (The operator's name, business name, address, telephone number and person in charge.): Section 59 lists the items that must appear at the beginning of the price list. They are the operator's name, the business name if it is different, the address and telephone number of the business location, and the position, business address and telephone number of the person in charge of day to day operations. Staff names, licence expiry dates and the registrar's address are not part of that list.
  113. The correct answer is D (The price at which each supply and service would be sold separately.): Section 60 requires the details of any package the operator offers, the price of the package and the price at which each supply and service would be sold separately. The separate price of each item must be shown even when the package is discounted. Instalment costs, staff names and the date the package was first offered are not required.
  114. The correct answer is B (Which caskets are not suitable to be cremated at the crematorium.): Section 62 (2) requires the price list to clearly indicate which caskets are not suitable for cremation. That warning protects a family from choosing a casket that cannot be used for the service they have arranged. Seasonal discounts, manufacturing location and factory linings are not part of that requirement.
  115. The correct answer is A (A statement that more information about these options is available on request.): Section 66 requires the price list to describe the funding, payment and financing options the operator provides, if any, and to state that additional information is available on request. That statement tells a family how to get the full details before signing. Authority approval, lender rate setting and silent changes by the operator are not required by section 66.
  116. The correct answer is C (At least one inexpensive casket with no ornamentation and no fixed lining.): Section 43 (b) requires at least one type of inexpensive casket that is neither ornamented nor has a fixed interior lining, apart from a lining to prevent leakage. A director cannot answer a request for the least expensive option with a discount on a more expensive casket. A rental casket and a single model chosen by the director do not satisfy the section.
  117. The correct answer is D (Every casket generally sold or offered, including the section 43 caskets.): Section 44 (1) requires information about each casket the operator generally sells or offers for sale, and it expressly includes the caskets that section 43 requires the operator to offer. The less expensive choices cannot be left out of the information given to purchasers. A casket that is only a special order and is not generally sold falls outside that description.
  118. The correct answer is B (The material, colour, finish and thickness of the casket exterior.): Section 44 (2) (c) (iii) requires the information to be sufficient to identify the material, colour, finish and thickness of the casket exterior. The weight, the warranty and the country of assembly are not listed. Interior lining details are covered by a different subparagraph.
  119. The correct answer is B (The prices for caskets, urns, outer burial containers, vaults and grave liners.): Section 62 (1) requires the price list to include the prices for caskets, urns, outer burial containers, vaults or grave liners and any other licensed supplies and services related to them. The same section also requires the price list to clearly indicate which caskets are not suitable for cremation. Cremation prices, visitation prices and transportation prices belong to other sections.
  120. The correct answer is C (The interior must not be visible from a public area when the doors are closed.): Section 47, paragraph 1, requires that the interior of the holding room not be visible from a public area when the doors to the room are closed. A glass panel that lets families see into the room fails that standard. Dimmed lighting, a curtain and a rear door are not the requirement the section sets.
  121. The correct answer is C (A device that prevents back-flow of water into the supply.): Section 47, paragraph 3 iii, requires a supply of running water with a device to prevent back-flow of water. The device protects the water supply from contamination during the preparation of bodies. A filter, a meter and a heater are not the required feature.
  122. The correct answer is A (A supply of personal protective equipment and clothing for each person.): Section 47, paragraph 3 iv, requires a sufficient supply of personal protective equipment and clothing for each person in the room who handles a dead human body. The supply must cover everyone who takes part in handling the body. A log, a locker and a work station do not satisfy that requirement.
  123. The correct answer is A (The price must be a fixed charge or a per unit charge.): Section 55 (4) requires prices on the price list to be expressed as a fixed charge or, if the price is based on a per unit charge, as a per unit charge. That lets a purchaser compare one operator with another on the same basis. A range, an hourly rate for every service and a tax added price do not meet subsection (4).
  124. The correct answer is C (Facilities that allow bodily fluids and waste to be disposed of.): Section 48, paragraph 4 v, requires facilities to dispose of bodily fluids and waste appropriately. The embalming room must be able to handle the material produced during preparation. A storage tank, a hauling contract and a storm drain connection are not the requirement the section sets.
  125. The correct answer is A (The room may be used only as an embalming room at the location.): Section 48, paragraph 5, says the embalming room shall be used only as an embalming room unless it is also a holding room, in which case it may be used only for those two purposes. Storing chairs or holding meetings goes beyond that limited use. The room's different purpose is not excused by disinfecting surfaces or closing the door.
  126. The correct answer is D (In premises named as business premises on the Class 1 licence.): Section 48, paragraph 1, requires the embalming room to be located in premises that a Funeral Establishment Operator Class 1 licence indicates are business premises of the funeral establishment. Leasing a building across town does not make it business premises. Ventilation, distance and municipal zoning approval do not change that requirement.
  127. The correct answer is D (The director may not be engaged for the removal under section 52.): Section 52 permits only a person who holds a Funeral Director Class 1 licence and is acting on behalf of a Funeral Establishment Operator Class 1 or Transfer Service Operator Class 1 licence, or a legally qualified medical practitioner. A Class 2 funeral director does not qualify. Written authorisation and supervision by a Class 1 director do not extend the list in section 52.
  128. The correct answer is A (The physician may be engaged to remove the pacemaker from the body.): Section 52 (b) allows an operator to engage a legally qualified medical practitioner to remove a pacemaker or radioactive implant. The physician's offer can therefore be accepted. A hospital setting and a coroner's consent are not conditions in section 52, and a funeral director is not the only person who may be engaged.
  129. The correct answer is D (Retain them until claimed or interred in a cemetery, whichever occurs earlier.): Section 53 (1) requires the operator to retain unclaimed cremated human remains that were not in the operator's possession for the purposes of interring or scattering. The operator must keep them until they are claimed or interred in a cemetery, whichever is earlier. Scattering them, delivering them to the Authority or returning them to the coroner is not what the section requires.
  130. The correct answer is C (The operator must make reasonable efforts to contact the purchaser or family.): Section 53 (2) allows interment in a cemetery, including a common lot, when the remains are not claimed within one year from the date of cremation. Before taking that step the operator must have made reasonable efforts to contact the purchaser of the cremation service or the personal representative or a family member. Newspaper notices, the cemetery owner's consent and a further waiting period are not required by the section.
  131. The correct answer is D (Refund the deposit because the remains were claimed before any interment.): Section 53 (3) requires the operator to refund any refundable deposit charged for the interment if the cremated remains are claimed before they are interred under subsection (2). Eight months is inside the one year period, so the family claimed the ashes in time. Keeping the deposit, applying it to an urn or transferring it to a cemetery is not permitted by subsection (3).
  132. The correct answer is D (Every licensed supply and service that the operator generally provides to families.): Section 55 (1) requires the price list to include every licensed supply and service the operator generally provides. Specialty items that the operator offers only occasionally are not generally provided, so they fall outside that phrase. Limiting the list to items kept in stock or to items the registrar names would not meet subsection (1).
  133. The correct answer is A (The position, business address and telephone number of the person in charge.): Section 59 requires the price list to begin with the operator's name, the business name if different, the address and telephone number of the business location, and the position, business address and telephone number of the person in charge of day to day operations. Only the last item is missing from the list described in the stem. Staff home addresses, incorporation dates and insurance details are not required.
  134. The correct answer is A (A copy must be provided without charge to any person who asks.): Section 68 (1) requires every operator to provide a copy of the price list, without charge, to every person who requests it. There is no requirement that the person be a client or have a meeting booked. Charging a printing fee or waiting for a signed contract would not comply.
  135. The correct answer is D (A notice that the price list is available upon request and without charge.): Section 68 (2) (a) allows the operator either to display copies near the main entrance for the taking or to post a notice near the main entrance that the price list is available upon request and without charge. A notice limited to office hours, to the arrangement office or to a printing fee does not match that wording. The operator must still provide a copy free of charge when asked.
  136. The correct answer is A (The price list in printable form, free of charge, in a visible place.): Section 68 (3) requires an operator who maintains or uses a website to promote or sell licensed supplies or services, or to enter into contracts, to make the price list available without charge in a printable form in a clearly visible place on the website. An email quote, a statement about the office and a summary of some prices do not meet that requirement. The full printable list must be on the site.
  137. The correct answer is D (Yes, because drivers may be required as a condition of using a vehicle.): Section 70 (1) (a) permits the operator to require the purchase of services listed in Column 2 as a condition of the purchase when the purchase is not by way of a package. The table pairs the use of a vehicle with drivers. The operator's ownership of the vehicle is not the reason the requirement is allowed, and the family's preference does not override the permitted tie.
  138. The correct answer is C (Yes, because the package price may be conditioned on the catering services.): Section 70 (1) (b) permits the operator to require the purchase of a Column 2 service as a condition of paying the package price when the purchase is by way of a package. The table pairs the use of facilities where the purchaser wants food served with catering services. The restriction about outside caterers is not in the regulation.
  139. The correct answer is D (The price for the removal of pacemakers and radioactive implants from a body.): Section 61, paragraph 3 iv, requires the price list to include the prices for the removal of pacemakers and radioactive implants. That price belongs with the other preparation and embalming prices. Device disposal fees, physician certification fees and container prices are not the item listed in that subparagraph.
  140. The correct answer is C (As separate prices for staff services, facilities, catering and other items.): Section 61, paragraph 2, requires separate prices for four categories: staff services for visitations, ceremonies and receptions, the use of facilities for those events, catering services, and any other licensed supplies and services related to them. A single combined price or a per guest price does not separate the categories. Splitting only the chapel and the staff also fails the requirement.
  141. The correct answer is B (The requirement is not permitted because the tie is not listed in section 70.): Section 38.1 of the Act prohibits an operator from requiring a purchaser to buy other supplies and services as a condition of a sale unless the operator does so in the prescribed circumstances. Section 70 sets out the permitted ties in its table, and a casket purchase is not one of them for cremation services. A written acknowledgement from the family would not make the requirement lawful.
  142. The correct answer is B (It is not permitted because the family does not want food served.): The table in section 70 permits an operator to require catering services only when the purchaser is using facilities where the purchaser wants food served. The family here wants no food or drink served at all, so that condition is not met. Listing catering in the table does not allow the operator to require it in every room rental.
  143. The correct answer is B (No, because the details, the package price and separate prices are required.): Section 60 requires the price list to include the details of any package the operator offers, whether or not the package is discounted, the price of the package, and the price at which each supply and service would be sold separately. A discount does not remove those requirements. The regulation does not require approval before a discounted package is offered.
  144. The correct answer is C (The room fails because mechanical ventilation to the outside must be installed.): Section 47, paragraph 3 v, requires mechanical ventilation directly to the outside of the building. A window that opens is not mechanical ventilation, even when it brings in fresh air. The problem is the missing system, not the presence of a window.
  145. The correct answer is D (The price list must indicate any minimum prices that apply to a service.): Section 55 (5) requires the price list to indicate whether there are any minimum prices for a particular supply or service. A minimum charge that is hidden from the list does not meet that requirement. Negotiability, a maximum price and an effective date for the minimum are not what subsection (5) requires.
  146. The correct answer is B (It fails because the lined caskets must be among the least expensive.): Section 43 (a) requires at least two types of caskets with either or both fixed interior linings and ornamentation, and those two types must come from among the least expensive available on the market. Mid range lined caskets do not satisfy that part of the rule even though two are displayed. The plain casket addresses section 43 (b), not clause (a).
  147. The correct answer is C (Prices must be given to persons who inquire by telephone or in person.): Section 68 (2) (b) requires information about prices for licensed supplies and services to be provided to persons who make inquiries of the operator in person, by telephone or in writing. A receptionist cannot limit price information to a booked arrangement meeting. The section does not allow the operator to wait for a name, a deposit or a signed contract.
  148. The correct answer is A (The size of the casket and the material and colour of the interior.): Section 44 (2) (c) (i) and (ii) require the information to be sufficient to identify the size of the casket and the material and colour of the casket interior. Weight, country of manufacture and supplier arrival dates are not listed. The retail price is required separately by section 44 (2) (a), but the delivery charge is not.
  149. The correct answer is A (The layout fails because identifying information belongs at the beginning of the list.): Section 59 requires the identifying information to be included at the beginning of the price list, not merely somewhere in it. Operators must not rely on a customer reading to the last page to find the business location or the person in charge. Section 59 does not prohibit a welcome message.
  150. The correct answer is A (Describe the payment options offered and state that details are on request.): Section 66 requires the price list to include the funding, payment and financing options the operator provides, if any, and a statement that additional information is available on request. A family should be able to see the choices before making a decision. Sending the family to the lender or waiting for a signed contract does not meet the section.
  151. The correct answer is B (Separate and apart from all money that is not trust money.): Section 76(1)(a) requires every operator to hold all trust money separate and apart from all money other than trust money. Section 76(1)(b) adds that the operator must deal with trust money in accordance with Division F. Mixing prepaid money with general business receipts breaks that rule even when the operator plans to put the money back later.
  152. The correct answer is A (Preserve the trust money for the beneficiary and refrain from using it.): Section 76(2)(c) states that when prepaid trust money sits temporarily outside an individual trust account or a pooled trust fund, the operator shall not use the money and shall preserve it for the beneficiary. Subsections 76(2)(a) and (b) also require an eligible depositary and an account in the operator's licensed name. A short term cash flow problem does not turn beneficiary money into operating money.
  153. The correct answer is D (Not later than the 35th day after the day the operator receives the money.): Section 78(2) requires an operator to deposit prepaid trust money not later than the 35th day after the day the operator receives the money. The deposit goes into an individual trust account that meets subsection 79(1) or a pooled trust fund that meets subsection 80(2). Section 78(3) then requires the money to stay in that account or fund until the Division permits a transfer or a payout.
  154. The correct answer is C (In the name of the operator as shown on its licence as trustee.): Section 79(1) paragraph 1 requires the individual trust account to be established in the name of the operator as it appears on its licence as the trustee for the purchaser. Paragraph 3 of the same subsection confirms that the operator is the trustee for the money held in that account. A title that leaves out the operator's licensed name or the trust relationship does not match the rule.
  155. The correct answer is A (Contracts with the same purchaser for the same recipient, held as a trust account.): Section 79(1) paragraph 2 says the individual trust account shall be used only as a trust account to hold trust money received in respect of contracts with the same purchaser for the same recipient. Grouping unrelated families into one account does not fit that description. A pooled trust fund, governed by section 80, is the route for holding many purchasers' money together.
  156. The correct answer is C (Six years from the date the contract is fully performed or is cancelled.): Section 101(1) sets one period for contract records: six years from the date the contract is fully performed or is cancelled. The clock runs from the completion or cancellation date, not from the signing date or the deposit date. Section 101(2) applies a separate six year rule to price lists.
  157. The correct answer is B (Within 15 days after the change occurs, in a form specified by the registrar.): Section 104(3) requires the individual to give the notice of change to the registrar within 15 days after the change occurs, in a form and manner specified by the registrar. Section 104(1) creates the duty to give written notice when employment with the primary operator changes. Section 104(2) lists the details that the notice must contain.
  158. The correct answer is D (The class, expiry date and status of the licence, including its conditions.): Section 111(1) paragraph 1 requires the registrar to make available the class, expiry date and status of a licensee's licence, including all conditions mentioned in subsection 16(1) of the Act except for conditions prescribed by regulation. Paragraph 2 covers the operator's name, business address, business telephone number and licence number. Records of family addresses, employee wages and cremation counts are not part of the entry for the licence.
  159. The correct answer is A (A gift, a bequest or any other transfer made without consideration of any kind.): Section 112(1) defines transfer as a gift, a bequest or any other transfer made without consideration. The definition sits in the Division that deals with disclosure requirements. A sale for value is handled through the sale and resale rules rather than through this definition.
  160. The correct answer is C (A person designated in the contract for whom the supply is provided.): Section 120 defines recipient as a person designated in a contract for the provision of a licensed supply or service as the person for whom the service or supply is to be provided. Section 120 also defines group insurance by reference to subsection 171(1) of the Insurance Act. The recipient is not necessarily the purchaser who signs and pays.
  161. The correct answer is B (In plain language and legibly printed in 10 point or larger type.): Section 121(2) requires the contract to be written in plain language and legibly printed in 10 point or larger type. Section 121(1) makes the whole section apply to every contract for the provision of licensed supplies or services. Section 121(3) then lists the information that the contract must contain.
  162. The correct answer is D (A dead human body with a pacemaker shall not be cremated.): Section 125(1)(a)(i) requires the contract to include a statement that a dead human body shall not be cremated if the body has a pacemaker. The same clause lists the radioactive implant exception and the casket material restriction. Section 125(1)(b) adds the separate statement that a body shall not be cremated unless the operator has received a certificate issued by the coroner authorizing the cremation.
  163. The correct answer is C (The Funeral Services Compensation Fund, which the regulation names for that purpose.): Section 193 names the Funeral Services Compensation Fund as the compensation fund for the purposes of subsection 61(1) of the Act. Subsection 61(2) describes the purpose of the fund, which is to compensate a person who suffers a financial loss because a licensee failed to comply with the Act, the regulations or an agreement made under the Act. Subsection 61(3) requires a licensee to make the contributions that the regulation requires.
  164. The correct answer is A (Each named cemetery's operator is exempt from sections 53 and 54 of the Act.): Section 1 of Ontario Regulation 184/12 exempts the operator of each listed cemetery from sections 53 and 54 of the Act in respect of that cemetery. The list names specific cemeteries, mostly serving Mennonite, Amish and Old Order communities, by lot and concession across Ontario. The exemption is tied to the named cemeteries and does not remove any other duty under the Act.
  165. The correct answer is B ($350, unless the operator also operates a cemetery.): Section 2 of Ontario Regulation 184/12 sets the maximum refundable deposit at $350 for an operator that is not also a cemetery operator. If the operator is also a cemetery operator, the maximum is the lesser of $350 and the lowest amount that the cemetery operator charges for the interment of cremated remains in a common lot. Section 53 of Ontario Regulation 30/11 allows the operator to inter unclaimed remains, and subsection 53(3) requires the deposit to be refunded if the remains are claimed before interment.
  166. The correct answer is D (No later than the 35th day after the day the contract was made.): Section 78(4) applies when a contract is for the provision of licensed supplies or services within 30 days after the day the contract is made. If the supplies or services are not provided within those 30 days, the operator must deposit the money paid under the contract. Clause 78(4)(b) sets the deadline as no later than the 35th day after the day the contract was made.
  167. The correct answer is A (On or after the 30th day, retain an amount allowed by section 140.): Section 78(1) requires money paid in advance to be held as prepaid trust money, but the operator may elect, on or after the 30th day after the day the contract was made, to retain an amount as not being prepaid trust money. The retained amount cannot exceed what the operator would be entitled to under section 140 if the purchaser cancelled under subsection 44(1) of the Act. That election does not change the deposit deadline set by section 78(2).
  168. The correct answer is C (As the licensed supplies and services are provided in accordance with the contract.): Section 81(1) paragraph 1 allows prepaid trust money to be paid to the operator in consideration for the licensed supplies and services purchased under the contract as those supplies and services are provided in accordance with the contract. Paragraph 2 then directs any money left after all amounts payable have been paid to the purchaser or to the person whom the contract designates. Paragraph 4 deals with the part of a contract that is cancelled.
  169. The correct answer is B (It is paid out to the operator, which then determines the refund owed.): Section 81(1) paragraph 4 says that when a contract is cancelled in whole or in part, the prepaid trust money relating to the cancelled part is paid out to the operator. The operator must then determine, in accordance with the Act and the regulations, the amount to be refunded. The operator must refund that amount to the purchaser or to the person whom the contract designates as entitled to receive it.
  170. The correct answer is A (The name and address of the person claiming the remains and the date claimed.): Section 100(1) paragraph 1 requires the record to show the name and address of the person claiming the remains and the date on which they are claimed. Paragraph 2 covers the different situation where unclaimed remains are interred in a common lot under section 53, and then the record must show the date and place of interment. Section 101(3) requires the record to be kept for six years from the date the remains are claimed or interred.
  171. The correct answer is D (Disclose the record without charge to the person making the request.): Section 100(2) requires the operator to disclose the record without charge when the purchaser, personal representative or family member of the deceased person asks for it. The request must relate to the record prepared under section 100(1) for that deceased person. Charging a copying fee or sending the request to the registrar does not match the rule.
  172. The correct answer is C (Six years from the date the price list was last in effect.): Section 101(2) requires an operator to retain a price list for six years from the date the price list was last in effect. The period runs from the end of the list's life rather than from the day it was written. Section 101(1) applies a separate six year period to records relating to a contract.
  173. The correct answer is A (Six years from the date of the cremation to which the certificate relates.): Section 101(5) requires a crematorium operator to retain the coroner's certificate authorizing the cremation for six years from the date of the cremation of the dead human body to which the certificate relates. The period is tied to the cremation date rather than to the date the certificate was issued. Section 125(1)(b) requires the operator to receive that certificate before a body is cremated.
  174. The correct answer is B (Six years from the date the record is created by the operator.): Section 101(6) requires an operator to keep every other record required under the Act or Part I for six years from the date it is created. The earlier subsections of section 101 deal with contract records, price lists and cremation records. The general rule in subsection (6) catches records that those specific rules do not cover.
  175. The correct answer is D (Give the registrar a notice of change in writing about the employment change.): Section 104(1) requires an individual holding a personal licence to give the registrar a notice of change in writing if a change occurs with respect to the individual's employment with the primary operator. Section 104(2) sets out the details that the notice must include. Section 104(3) sets the 15 day deadline.
  176. The correct answer is C (The reason that the operator gave for ending the employment.): Section 104(2) requires the notice of change to set out all of the details of the change, including the date the individual begins employment, the date the individual ceases employment and, in the case of a termination by the operator, the reason for the termination. The notice concerns the employment relationship with the primary operator. The 15 day deadline in section 104(3) runs from the date the change occurs.
  177. The correct answer is A (The licensee's name and number plus the employer's name and number.): Section 111(1) paragraph 8 requires the register to show, for holders of personal licences, the licensee's name and licence number plus the name, business address, business telephone number and licence number of the primary operator that employs the licensee. That entry lets the public connect a personal licensee to a licensed business. Paragraph 2 covers the separate information posted for an operator.
  178. The correct answer is D (Whether a hearing is scheduled and, if so, its date and location.): Section 111(1) paragraph 11 requires the registrar to make available information about whether a discipline committee or an appeals committee is scheduled to hear a matter with respect to the licensee under section 64 of the Act and, if so, the date and location of the hearing. Paragraph 10 separately requires a description of each action taken, the grounds for it, the date and the final result. The register does not list the evidence or the witnesses.
  179. The correct answer is B (A copy of the consumer information guide prepared by the registrar.): Section 113(1)(a) requires the operator to make sure that, before a contract for the sale of licensed supplies or services is entered into, the prospective purchaser has received a copy of the consumer information guide if the registrar has prepared the guide. Section 112(1) defines the consumer information guide as the guide the registrar prepares that sets out a consumer's rights and obligations under the Act. Section 113(1)(b) separately requires a copy of the operator's price list.
  180. The correct answer is C (The rights proposed for the contract and the rights under the Act.): Section 113(1)(c) requires an explanation of the cancellation rights and refund entitlements that the operator proposes to include in the contract and an explanation of the cancellation rights and refund entitlements under the Act. The two parts matter because a contract may add rights without taking away the statutory ones. Section 121(3) paragraph 18 requires any added cancellation, refund and return policies to appear in the contract itself.
  181. The correct answer is A (That the cemetery is governed by by-laws available from the operator.): Section 113(1)(f) requires a statement that the cemetery is governed by by-laws and that a copy of the by-laws is available from the operator for review. Section 113(1)(e) separately requires an offer to provide a copy of the cemetery by-laws. Section 113(1)(g) requires information about restrictions in the by-laws that deal with markers, lot decorations and private structures.
  182. The correct answer is B (The operator's licence number and a description of its licence type.): Section 117(1) requires an operator who uses a website to promote or enter into contracts for licensed supplies or services to make the operator's licence number and a description of the operator's type of licence available in a clearly visible place on the website. Section 112.2 adds a separate requirement to post a link to the registrar's consumer information guide. The proof of licence rule for individuals is in section 117(2).
  183. The correct answer is D (Carry the licence and produce it when a person asks to see it.): Section 117(2) requires individuals licensed under the Act to carry their licence on their person when they conduct business relating to the sale or provision of a licensed supply or service and to produce it upon request. Keeping the licence at the office does not meet the rule for off-site meetings. Section 117(1) sets the separate website requirement for an operator.
  184. The correct answer is C (A unique identification number or code that is issued in sequence.): Section 121(3) paragraph 1 requires the contract to contain a unique identification number or code which is sequential. That number lets the operator and the regulator trace a contract through the records. Section 121(3) paragraph 2 separately requires the date of the contract.
  185. The correct answer is B (The individual's name and, if licensed, that person's licence number.): Section 121(3) paragraph 5 requires the contract to state the name of the individual who negotiated the contract on behalf of the operator and, if that individual is licensed under the Act, his or her licence number. That requirement helps a purchaser identify the person who arranged the agreement. Paragraph 3 separately requires the operator's licence number and the details of each party to the contract.
  186. The correct answer is A (Inform the purchaser of the substitution and whether its value is equal or greater.): Section 121(3) paragraph 14 sets out the operator's undertaking about substitutions. The operator must inform the purchaser of the proposed substitution and whether it is of equal or greater value than the original supply or service. The operator must also inform the purchaser of the cancellation rights, determine whether the purchaser wishes to cancel and avoid any price increase if the purchaser does not cancel.
  187. The correct answer is D (The total price of the supplies and services does not exceed $250.): Section 122(1) exempts a contract from subsections 40(1) and (2) and sections 41, 42, 43 and 44 of the Act and sections 121, 123 and 128 of the Regulation when all five listed requirements are met. One requirement is that the total purchase price does not exceed $250. The others are that no licence is needed to sell the supplies and services, that they are purchased separately from licensed supplies or services, that the purchaser takes immediate delivery or receives the service immediately, and that the supply is not stored by the operator.
  188. The correct answer is C (Details about how the money will be held, invested and paid out.): Section 123(2) paragraph 1 requires the contract to contain details concerning the holding, investment and payment out of the money in accordance with the regulations, including the manner in which the funds will be held in trust. Section 123(1) applies the section when money is paid before the supplies or services are provided and must be held in trust. Paragraph 3 or 4 of the same subsection, depending on the kind of account, requires the contract to describe the purchaser's yearly statement right.
  189. The correct answer is A (The name, address and telephone number of the issuer and the contract number if known.): Section 124(1)(a) requires the contract to contain the name, address and telephone number, if any, of the insurer or the issuer of the annuity contract and the number of the insurance or annuity contract if known. Section 124(1) applies when the price is funded in whole or in part out of insurance or annuity proceeds, including group insurance. Section 124(2) requires the operator to attach the policy and related documents if they are available.
  190. The correct answer is B (Give the purchaser a copy of the contract as signed by the purchaser.): Section 127(1) requires the operator to make sure that the purchaser receives a copy of the contract as signed by the purchaser at the time the purchaser signs it. Section 127(2) then prescribes how the fully signed contract is delivered after all parties have signed: personally, by registered mail, or in another manner if the operator can prove receipt. Mailing the contract the following week does not satisfy the requirement in subsection (1).
  191. The correct answer is C (The lesser of the prepaid trust money and the current selling price.): Section 81(2) caps what an operator may retain in consideration for the supplies and services at the lesser of the prepaid trust money and any proceeds from an annuity or insurance contract in respect of them, and the current selling price at the time they are provided as set out in the price list maintained under section 33 of the Act. Section 81(3) deals with the case where the current selling price for a supply or service is not on the price list, and the operator then uses the price charged for a similar supply or service. A contract term that promises more does not overcome the cap.
  192. The correct answer is D (A statement of the right to receive the excess money held in trust.): Section 123(2) paragraph 6 requires the contract to describe the purchaser's right to receive on completion of the contract any amount held in trust in excess of all amounts paid or payable to the operator and any other suppliers, and the manner in which the operator will calculate the amount to be paid. Section 81(1) paragraph 2 gives effect to that right when the money is paid out. Section 81(1) paragraph 3 sends the money to the unclaimed trust process in section 82 if the operator cannot identify or locate the person entitled to it.
  193. The correct answer is B (That naming the operator as beneficiary counts as payment under the contract.): Section 124(1)(f) requires the acknowledgement that once the operator is named as a beneficiary or assigned the right to designate the beneficiary, or the beneficiary's rights to proceeds are assigned to the operator, money is considered for the purposes of section 38 of the Act to be paid under the contract in an amount equal to the proceeds that will be paid directly or indirectly to the operator. That acknowledgement matters because section 38 of the Act deals with money paid under a contract. Section 124(1)(g) also requires statements about what will occur if the application is denied, if the contract is not paid for in full, if either contract is cancelled, and if the named beneficiary changes.
  194. The correct answer is A (Made reasonable efforts to provide it and arranged a refund including income earned.): Section 128 sets out the requirements that must be satisfied for a contract to be enforceable by an operator, for the purposes of clause 40(1)(h) of the Act. Paragraph 3 deals with a supply or service that the operator no longer provides: the operator must have made reasonable efforts to provide it and, if unable to do so, arranged to refund the initial amount paid plus any income earned on it. Subsection 40(1) of the Act states the general rule that a contract is not enforceable by an operator unless the listed requirements are met.
  195. The correct answer is D (Make a refund in accordance with the Act and the regulations.): Section 133 requires an operator to make a refund in accordance with the Act and the regulations when the operator receives insurance or annuity proceeds to fund the purchase of licensed supplies or services and the amount received exceeds the amount payable to the operator and any other suppliers. The same duty applies when the operator provides no licensed supplies or services in consideration for the proceeds. The surplus is not a fee for arranging the insurance.
  196. The correct answer is C (The supply is substantially completed and the purchaser has paid for it in full.): Section 136(3) lists the conditions for storing a supply in advance of its use. The supply must be in a substantially completed condition and the purchaser must have made full payment for it. The operator must also know or ought to know that the materials will withstand prolonged storage, the storage must not give rise to a lien, the contract must record the purchaser's agreement to the storage and to treating the supply as provided, and the contract must not make the purchaser responsible for storage or insurance costs. Section 136(4) adds that the supply cannot be stored within the 30 days after the day the contract for the supply is made.
  197. The correct answer is B (The greater of the income earned and the income that should have been earned.): Section 137(1) requires the operator, within 30 days after the day the operator receives a notice of cancellation under subsection 41(1) of the Act, to refund all money received under the contract and to pay the purchaser the greater of the income earned on the money and the income that would have been earned had the money been deposited as required under the Act and the regulations. The extra payment recognizes what the trust deposit should have earned. Subsection 41(2) of the Act separately requires the refund of all money received under the contract.
  198. The correct answer is A (The lesser of 10 per cent of the money received and $350, less the amount retained.): Section 140(2) allows the operator to deduct, in calculating the refund after a cancellation under subsection 44(1) of the Act, the lesser of 10 per cent of the sum of the money received under the contract and the additional amounts required to be refunded under subsection (1), and $350, less the amount the operator has retained under subsection 78(1) as not being prepaid trust money if the balance is positive. Section 140(1) requires the operator to refund all money received plus the greater of the income earned and the income that would have been earned. The deduction rule does not apply to every contract, because subsection 140(3) sets different treatment for a customized supply.
  199. The correct answer is C (The purchaser is deemed to have cancelled the contract under section 44 of the Act.): Section 141(1) deems the purchaser to have cancelled the contract under section 44 of the Act when the operator has reasonable grounds to believe that the recipient is or, if alive, would be at least 120 years old, when none or only part of the supplies and services have been provided and no request has been made for them, and when the operator cannot locate the recipient or the purchaser after making reasonable efforts. Subsection 141(2) says the deeming rule does not apply in the circumstances in which section 49 of the Act applies. A deemed cancellation brings the refund rules in section 44 of the Act and section 140 of the Regulation into play.
  200. The correct answer is D (The lesser of $1,300 and the price shown on the operator's price list.): Section 3(1) of Ontario Regulation 184/12 caps the amount that an operator may specify on a price list for providing services under subsection 164(1) or (2) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 at the lesser of $1,300 for all services provided with respect to the interment or scattering and the price the operator charged for the services as shown on the operator's price list. That amount is payable by the delivery agent who sent the written direction requesting the services. Subsection 3(2) sets a separate maximum of the lesser of $650 and the lowest price the operator charges for interment rights and opening and closing services when the operator receives human remains from an irregular burial site under section 178 of Ontario Regulation 30/11.
  201. The correct answer is D (An area that affords privacy for discussing, making arrangements, and entering into contracts.): Section 46 of Ontario Regulation 30/11 applies to an operator whose premises the public may enter to contract for licensed supplies and services. That operator has to make an area available where those discussions and contracts can happen in private. A bookable alcove, a staff room or a busy front desk does not satisfy the requirement.
  202. The correct answer is D (The family decides who takes part, and the licensee welcomes its guests.): The competency profile expects the licensee to be sensitive to the needs and expectations of the person making the arrangements. It also expects the licensee to understand how grief affects a family from childhood through adulthood and to respond appropriately. The family decides who attends, and the licensee adapts the meeting to the people in the room.
  203. The correct answer is C (The Statement of Death, also called Form 15, completed by the funeral director.): The funeral director completes the Statement of Death, Form 15, which collects the information Ontario needs to register the death. The attending physician or nurse practitioner completes the medical certificate of death, which is a different document. The proof of death and the burial permit are separate as well, so neither one matches the form described.
  204. The correct answer is B (It lets the family settle accounts and claims with banks, insurers and government offices.): The funeral director's proof of death is the document families use with banks, insurers and government offices after the death. It shows that the death took place and that the funeral home is handling the arrangements. It does not replace the medical certificate, register the death or give anyone authority over the disposition.
  205. The correct answer is C (Six years from the date the contract was fully performed or was cancelled.): Section 101 (1) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 requires an operator to retain records relating to a contract for six years from the date the contract is fully performed or cancelled. A seven year, ten year or twelve year period does not match the regulation. The same regulation sets other retention rules for price lists and for records about human remains, so the licensee has to apply the correct rule.
  206. The correct answer is B (A licensed funeral director must review and sign any contract the student negotiates.): Section 32 (3) of Ontario Regulation 30/11 deals with a contract negotiated by someone enrolled in a funeral director or funeral preplanner education program who is not yet licensed. That contract must be reviewed and signed by a licensed funeral director. Observation, a written notice to the family or a reduced fee is not what the section requires.
  207. The correct answer is A (When the family is ready to choose between two specific days for the visitation.): A closed question offers a narrow set of choices and helps a family settle one decision once the discussion has narrowed. Open questions invite the family to describe what it wants and are used earlier in the conversation. Asking what the deceased person was like in life, opening the meeting or drawing out a silent family all call for open questions instead.
  208. The correct answer is D (It gives the family a chance to correct a detail before it is written down.): Summarizing and paraphrasing confirm that the licensee heard the family correctly. They also give the family a chance to fix a detail before it goes into the contract or the file. Showing who is in charge, filling a silence or announcing the next subject are not the purposes of this technique.
  209. The correct answer is A (Silence gives the family room to think and to decide whether to speak.): Allowing silence is one of the communication techniques the competency profile lists. A person who has just heard a large number may need a few seconds before responding. Filling every silence with speech takes that time away, and silence is not a signal of disagreement or a way to protect the licensee from a question.
  210. The correct answer is B (The names of the deceased person's parents and the person's usual occupation.): The Statement of Death records the information the province needs to register the death, and parent names and occupation belong in that set. The licensee also confirms dates and places of birth, marital status and any benefit information the form requires. The funeral home's name, the director's licence number and the amount paid belong to the business file, not to the registration information.
  211. The correct answer is D (Hold the announcement until the daughter has read the full text and approved it.): The death announcement is often the family's first public statement about the death, so accuracy and taste matter. The competency profile expects the licensee to assist in preparing the announcement, confirming the wording with the family and getting permission before publishing. Sending it out while a family member is still asking to review the text risks an error the family cannot undo.
  212. The correct answer is B (Keep the family's information confidential and do not discuss the arrangement with the friend.): The competency profile requires the licensee to maintain confidentiality of information at all times. Details learned during an arrangement are not shared with friends, neighbours or anyone else who does not need them to do their work. A familiar name in a small town does not create an exception, and a friend's promise of secrecy does not create one either.
  213. The correct answer is D (Refer the person to a physician or grief counsellor and continue with the arrangements.): The competency profile expects the licensee to assess emotional needs while recognising the limits of the scope of practice. Where a need goes beyond that scope, the licensee makes the person aware of counselling or refers the person to an appropriate health care professional. The licensee does not treat a depression, promise that it will pass or run a counselling session in the office.
  214. The correct answer is A (Speak directly to the family member and keep sentences short and free of idioms.): Working with an interpreter, the licensee speaks to the family member rather than to the interpreter, because the conversation is with the family. Short sentences and plain words give the interpreter a fair chance to translate accurately. Asking for a summary instead of a translation, or repeating every sentence three times, changes what the family hears or slows the meeting without helping.
  215. The correct answer is A (Telephone the family to confirm the service details and to answer any new questions.): The competency profile expects the licensee to arrange follow up as required after the initial enquiry and the later contacts. A call after the arrangement confirms the details, catches anything that was misunderstood and gives the family a chance to ask new questions. Waiting for the family to call, mailing a signature request or sending a survey is not the follow up the profile describes.
  216. The correct answer is C (Tell the neighbour to step away and confirm that emergency services were called.): A sudden or unexplained death is controlled by emergency services and the coroner, so the caller is asked to step away and to avoid touching anything. The licensee confirms whether emergency services have been called and waits for their direction. Starting a transfer, sending the caller through the apartment or promising a quick arrival all ignore what the situation requires.
  217. The correct answer is D (Whether the physician has completed the medical certificate of death and released the body.): At the first contact the licensee confirms where the deceased person is, whether the place of death requires any special permission, and whether a coroner, physician or police officer is involved. A death at home under palliative care still has paperwork that must be completed before the funeral home can proceed. Parking, a catalogue choice and a deposit are practical questions for later in the process.
  218. The correct answer is A (Review the file, print the forms, and set out enough chairs in the room.): Preparation means knowing the file, having the documents ready and making the privacy area fit the number of people who are coming. The licensee also thinks about who will attend and what the family may need. Holding calls helps once the meeting is under way, while collecting vital statistics by telephone or presenting a package before the family has talked takes decisions out of the family's hands.
  219. The correct answer is A (Introduce yourself, explain the purpose of the meeting, and ask what the family knows.): The conference usually moves from information to options to decisions, and it starts with the family and the reason for the meeting. Introducing the licensee, explaining what will happen and asking what the family already knows helps people settle before any decision is made. Handing over the price list, asking for a signature or starting with the forms all push the family into paperwork before they are ready.
  220. The correct answer is D (Sit at the same level as the family, with an open posture and few interruptions.): A family reads body language before the licensee says a word, so sitting at the same level, keeping an open posture and protecting the conversation from interruptions communicate respect. Standing over the family, sitting across a wide table or folding both arms all create distance. None of those choices helps a family that is meeting the licensee for the first time.
  221. The correct answer is B (Decline the call, silence the telephone, and apologize briefly to the family.): A ringing telephone during a conversation about a person's life tells the family that something else matters more. By the second ring the licensee silences the telephone and offers a short apology, then returns to the family. Taking the call, leaving the room or pretending nothing happened all interrupt a meeting that should belong to the family.
  222. The correct answer is B (Read the name back, spell it aloud, and ask the family to confirm the spelling.): The competency profile expects the licensee to obtain complete and accurate information for documentation. Reading information back and spelling names aloud catch errors before the name goes onto forms that are hard to change later. Choosing a spelling from memory, letting one relative guess or leaving both spellings on the form all risk a misspelled name on the registration and the death announcement.
  223. The correct answer is C (Record the full legal name, and note the other names the person was known by.): The licensee asks for the exact legal name and records any other name the deceased person used. Both details matter, because the registration and the notice may be read by people who knew the person by a different name. Recording only the familiar name or the workplace nickname leaves the legal record incomplete, and a nickname does not have to appear on identification before it is worth noting.
  224. The correct answer is D (Read the date back and ask the family to confirm it from a document.): A date of birth taken from memory on the telephone is a risk, because a wrong date can hold up an estate payment or the death certificate later. Reading the date back and having the family confirm it from a document takes a few seconds and prevents an error that is hard to correct. Guessing, voting or leaving a blank line on the form all pass the problem to someone else.
  225. The correct answer is C (Whether the person belonged to a union, a veterans group or a benefit program.): The licensee gathers the information the law and the business require, and that includes military service, union membership and any benefit programs that may apply. Families often do not expect these questions, so the licensee explains why they are asked. Casket material, favourite hymns and flower choices belong to service planning, not to this part of the information gathering.
  226. The correct answer is B (Check in with the family and offer a break before continuing the meeting.): A conference that has already run 90 minutes reaches a point where a grieving family stops taking in new information. The licensee checks in and offers a break before beginning the next subject. Rushing ahead, splitting the signatures or reading on without stopping all risk decisions the family did not really make.
  227. The correct answer is A (Present one subject at a time in order and summarize before moving on.): A large amount of information has to be broken into a sequence the family can follow. Presenting one subject at a time, in a logical order, and summarizing before moving to the next subject lets the family keep pace. Giving every document at once, starting with the price list or letting the family set the order all make it harder for a grieving family to follow what is being decided.
  228. The correct answer is B (Describe in plain words what the family and guests would see at each stage.): The words funeral professionals use every day are not plain language for a family arranging its first funeral. Describing what the family and guests would see at each stage lets the family picture the choice instead of translating a term. Repeating the jargon, handing over a brochure with the same words or sending the family elsewhere does nothing to close the gap.
  229. The correct answer is A (Let him finish, keep your voice level, and acknowledge the frustration he feels.): The basic technique for a raised voice is to lower the temperature, which means letting the person finish and keeping the licensee's own voice level. Acknowledging the frustration shows that the licensee heard him without defending a decision that was already explained. Answering more loudly, sending him out of the room or handing the decision to the rest of the family makes the conflict worse.
  230. The correct answer is A (Narrow the choice to two caskets and ask which one the family prefers.): A family that has circled the casket room twice is stuck, so the licensee narrows the field with a closed question instead of repeating the tour. Reducing the choice to two options gives the family something it can actually decide. Choosing for the family, setting a deadline inside the meeting or opening the rest of the arrangements first all take the decision away from the people who must make it.
  231. The correct answer is D (Ask the brother with authority for his direction, and record it in the file.): When two family members give opposite instructions, the licensee stops, confirms who holds the authority and takes direction from that person. The other brother is treated with respect, but the funeral home cannot act on an instruction the person with authority has not given. Letting the brothers settle it alone, following the loudest voice or recording both requests as instructions leaves the arrangement without a clear basis.
  232. The correct answer is C (Explain kindly that the meeting should wait until she is able to take part fully.): A person who has been drinking cannot give the careful direction an arrangement requires, and the licensee protects the family by postponing the decisions. The explanation is given kindly, without judgement, and a new time is set. Taking instructions quickly, having someone else sign for her or calling the police all fail to protect the family's interests or her dignity.
  233. The correct answer is C (Ask the parents whether they want the children to stay or to play elsewhere.): The competency profile expects the licensee to understand how grief affects a family from childhood through adulthood and to respond appropriately. Children in the room are the parents' decision, so the licensee asks the parents what they want. Sending the children out, telling them to sit still or announcing that children are not permitted all take that decision away from the parents.
  234. The correct answer is A (Speak clearly at a normal volume, face her, and allow extra time for answers.): An older person who does not hear well needs the licensee to face her and speak clearly at a normal volume, with extra time for each answer. Shouting does not improve understanding, and answering for her through her daughter leaves the person who holds the information out of the conversation. Handing over a long form shifts the work to the person who is already struggling.
  235. The correct answer is C (Ask about the customs and follow the family's direction where the law allows.): The licensee is sensitive and accepting of a person's cultural and religious persuasion, so the family is asked to explain what its tradition requires. The licensee follows that direction within the law and asks questions rather than guessing. A single standard procedure, a promise that every request will be carried out exactly, or sending the family to another funeral home all fail the family at a difficult time.
  236. The correct answer is D (Speak to the family through the interpreter and keep the interpreter to that task.): A neighbour who interprets has two roles, and the licensee keeps the interpreting role separate from the opinions the neighbour holds as a friend. The licensee speaks to the family through the interpreter and asks the interpreter to translate what the family says. Letting the interpreter advise on the casket, dropping the interpreter or asking for partial translation all leave the family without a clear voice in its own arrangement.
  237. The correct answer is B (Listen to the mother, acknowledge her pain, and avoid saying anything that assigns blame.): A parent who has lost a child often asks whether the death could have been prevented, and the question is part of grief rather than a request for the licensee's opinion. The licensee listens, acknowledges the pain and avoids words that place blame. Answering the question directly, explaining what treatment might have done or changing the subject all add to the family's distress.
  238. The correct answer is D (Omit the cause of death, confirm the wording, and keep the information private.): The family decides what the public announcement says, so the licensee prepares a notice that leaves out the cause of death. The wording is confirmed with the family, and everything learned about the death stays confidential. Printing the cause because of a newspaper duty, printing it because a reporter asked, or asking the coroner's office to approve the wording all ignore the family's right to direct its own announcement.
  239. The correct answer is D (Address the mother directly, ask for her own view, and note who holds the authority.): A family member who takes over can quietly push the person with authority out of the conversation. The licensee addresses the mother directly, asks what she wants and records who holds the authority. Letting the son continue, sending him out of the room or asking the mother to sign a note about her son all avoid the real question, which is what the person with authority wants to decide.
  240. The correct answer is B (Summarize the decisions, review the contract with the purchaser, and obtain signatures.): The conference ends with a review: the licensee summarizes what was decided, reviews the contract with the purchaser and obtains the signatures. It also gives the family a chance to catch a detail before the meeting closes. Sending the family home to sign later, demanding payment before anyone leaves or delaying the file notes all leave the arrangement without the record it needs.
  241. The correct answer is D (Give the price information she asks for, answer her questions, and let her decide.): A family is free to ask another funeral home for information even when an arrangement is already under way elsewhere. The licensee answers the questions honestly, provides the price information requested and lets the caller decide what to do. Criticising the other funeral home, refusing to talk or offering to negotiate on the caller's behalf all go beyond the licensee's role.
  242. The correct answer is B (Ask the widow directly what she would like, then record her direction.): The widow holds the authority, and the argument between her children is not a reason to leave her out of her own arrangement. The licensee asks her directly what she would like and records the direction she gives. Letting the children settle it, following the most involved child or sending the family home leaves the decision with people who do not hold the authority.
  243. The correct answer is A (Arrange an independent interpreter so that a young relative does not carry that role.): A 15 year old grandchild should not be asked to interpret a contract discussion for her grandparents, even when the family has managed that way before. The licensee arranges an independent interpreter so the family hears the contract and the cost accurately. Using the granddaughter, asking for a partial translation or handing over a contract in English all leave the grandparents without a full explanation.
  244. The correct answer is C (Explain that the licensee must wait for direction from the person with authority.): A friend may hold strong views about the funeral, but the funeral home takes direction from the person with legal authority. The licensee explains this kindly and waits until that person can be reached. Recording the friend's instructions, asking the friend to sign for the cost or beginning the transfer all take the arrangement out of the hands of the person who is entitled to give it.
  245. The correct answer is A (Explain the next steps, gather the contact details, and arrange a follow up call.): A caller in another country needs clear information, a way to stay in contact and an understanding of what happens next. The licensee explains the next steps, takes the contact details and offers a follow up. Telling the caller to wait, handing the arrangement to a neighbour or saying the decisions should wait all leave the family with no way forward.
  246. The correct answer is C (Listen with concern and suggest that he speak with his own doctor soon.): A man who cannot sleep and fears a breakdown needs the licensee's attention and a referral, not a diagnosis. The licensee listens, shows concern and points him to a physician, which is what the competency profile expects when a need goes beyond the scope of practice. Calling the reaction normal, offering only a rest room or refusing to discuss feelings all fail to connect him with the help he needs.
  247. The correct answer is C (Slow down and let the family decide each remaining choice in its own time.): A family that agrees to anything so it can go home is not really deciding, and a choice made that way can be regretted later. The licensee slows the meeting down and gives the family time for each remaining decision. Choosing for the family, sending them home to finish by telephone or asking for a signature now all push the arrangement past the people it belongs to.
  248. The correct answer is A (Set the pen aside, explain who signs, and record what was said in the file.): The purchaser is the person who is responsible under the contract, and a man who has been drinking is not in a position to take that role on because he reached for the pen first. The licensee sets the pen aside, explains who signs and records what happened. Letting him sign, clearing the room or accepting payment from whoever offers it all leave the contract in an unsafe position.
  249. The correct answer is C (Write down the change, confirm it with the person holding authority, and record it.): A request made after the conference still has to come from the person with authority and still has to be recorded. The licensee writes down the change, confirms it with that person and updates the file. Refusing every change, acting on the daughter's request alone or demanding a written request before doing anything all mishandle a routine change.
  250. The correct answer is B (Follow the parents' direction, keep the cause private, and be kind to the aunt.): The parents hold the authority, and the family decides what the announcement says about how their daughter died. The licensee follows their direction, keeps the cause of death private and treats the aunt with care. Printing the cause, letting the aunt decide the wording or delaying the notice until everyone agrees all put the announcement before the family's decision.
